\chapter{Theorem provenance and status ledger}
\index{provenance ledger}\index{theorem status}
\label{app:provenance}

This appendix records where the principal theorems of the book come from,
which hypotheses belong to their original statements, and what has been
proved inside this text.  Its purpose is logical, not merely historical.
A theorem imported from the literature remains an imported theorem even when
we give a detailed proof architecture; a result proved in a manuscript
supplied by the author is not described as a published theorem; and a book-level
argument is identified as such.

The literature cut-off of the original release snapshot was
\[
                         \text{24 August 2026}.
\tag{L.1}\label{eq:appL-cutoff}
\]
The present text also records later dated additions, including the
September supplied local-link manuscript and the source repairs in the
October editorial programme. Equation \eqref{eq:appL-cutoff} describes
the historical snapshot, not a cut-off excluding those additions.
Version-sensitive statements are tied to the version and date appearing in
the bibliography.  Later revisions may strengthen, weaken, or correct them.

% Dense provenance tables are set as full-width records.  Repeating the
% field names keeps each record intelligible after a page break and avoids
% the narrow columns that make long citations and hypotheses unreadable.
\newcommand{\AppLFirstField}{}
\newcommand{\AppLSecondField}{}
\newcommand{\AppLThirdField}{}
\newenvironment{AppLProvenanceLedger}[3]{%
  \par\medskip
  \renewcommand{\AppLFirstField}{#1}%
  \renewcommand{\AppLSecondField}{#2}%
  \renewcommand{\AppLThirdField}{#3}%
}{%
  \par\medskip
}
\newcommand{\AppLProvenanceRecord}[3]{%
  \par\addvspace{.8\baselineskip}%
  \noindent\textbf{\AppLFirstField.} #1\par\nobreak
  \noindent\emph{\AppLSecondField.} #2\par\nobreak
  \noindent\emph{\AppLThirdField.} #3\par
}

\section{How to read the ledger}
\label{sec:appL-how}

Each entry has one of five statuses.

\begin{description}
\item[Proved.]
The book supplies a complete argument from earlier results in the book or
from standard algebraic facts explicitly stated at the point of use.

\item[Deep import.]
The proof is not reproduced in full.  The theorem is stated with its
source hypotheses and an exact citation or theorem number.

\item[Reconstruction.]
The book proves the result by reorganizing ingredients from the cited
source.  The mathematical credit remains with that source.

\item[Supplied manuscript.]
The argument comes from a manuscript supplied by the author for this
project.  The text has been checked and rewritten, but the result is not
represented as independently published.

\item[First-draft result.]
The claim is proved in this book and is not assigned to an external source.
Such a claim is kept separate from the historical theorem stream and is
accompanied by its exact scope or by a counterexample.
\end{description}

These labels concern provenance, not importance.  An elementary lemma may
be a deep dependency if the available proof uses resolution, alteration, or
test ideals.  Conversely, a long calculation may be proved here from
definitions.

\subsection{Current statements and historical receipts}
\label{sec:appL-current-boundary}
The chapter and appendix quality passes distinguish a corrected current
statement from an archived independent verification of earlier exact bytes.
All uses of \emph{exact-current} or \emph{exact-live} in a dated receipt
below refer to its recorded snapshot and byte boundary. They do not assert
that a later editorial repair has a new independent-agent or production
receipt. Frozen packet digests and rejected runs are retained as historical
evidence; the present theorem statement and its local source note govern
its mathematical scope.

Likewise, inventory counts in a dated research record describe that gate.
The active inventory is the one in \Cref{chap:major-open-problems}, with 42 atoms in
12 formal problem environments at the 2 October 2026 editorial checkpoint.
A later narrowing or retirement is read from that current inventory, not
from an older count of 53 or 55. This editorial pass makes no new research
claim and no independent verification claim. Build and rendered-page
artifacts remain in the external editorial ledger.

\subsection{Second-draft verification boundary}
\label{sec:appL-second-draft-boundary}

The 22 August 2026 expansion pass reaches every numbered chapter, but this
does not turn the historical first-pass records into a new whole-book
mathematical or source audit.  Independent mathematical and source audits,
an exact-source whole-book build, a log scan, and rendered-page inspection
are separate release gates.  Their artifact-dependent evidence is recorded
outside the typeset book in the release summary, so this provenance appendix
does not make a self-referential build claim.  The earlier trace record R3-19
is superseded: the complete supplied
source gives an
\(\mathcal I\)-input/\(P\)-output theorem, which is recorded as a source
import rather than as a Rethlas novelty claim.  These are
verification-status statements, not a change in the provenance of any
theorem.

The first Chapter~18 result is a direct counterexample assembled from the published
GJR injection and curve-cohomology formula and the good-reduction extreme of
Raynaud uniformization; it does not settle the totally degenerate restriction.
The second is a book-level deduction from the GJR residue and common-model
formalism and an integral splitting of the degree functional; it applies in
residual dimension at most one, including all good strict analytic curves,
and its arbitrary-dimensional boundary is settled by the signed-span theorem
and positive-cone counterexample recorded below.  The Chapter~70 theorem is a
book-level derivation from BFJ's intersection theory and the Hodge index
theorem for smooth complex germs; singular and nonnormal germs remain outside
its scope.
The Chapter~23 result is book-local and fixed-skeleton only.  The Chapter~49
result is confined to the positive-volume probability-normalized BJ energy
framework.  The toric theorem in Chapters~55 and~69 is a synthesis of the
published Cao, Guenancia, and toric-character inputs, with no novelty claim,
and covers only invariant currents in integral or rational big toric classes.
The author-supplied proof of Xia's Conjecture~8.8 remains unpublished; its
verified scope is the connected smooth-projective, big-line-bundle,
positive-Cao-volume framework, excluding zero volume, transcendental classes,
singular varieties, and attainment of the supremum.

non-final run into a receipt.

\begin{remark}[Citation granularity]
\label{rem:appL-citations}
Chapter source notes contain the exact local theorem numbers.  This appendix
collects the major dependencies at the level of a theorem family.  When the
two appear to differ, the narrower hypothesis and the more precise local
citation govern.
\end{remark}

\section{Proof boundary of the technical appendices}
\label{sec:appL-appendix-proof-boundary}

Results in \crefrange{app:valuation-algebra}{app:complex-reference}
carry proofs or explicit attributions to their controlling sources.
A block headed \emph{foundational input} is
not represented as a proof of that input: it states the imported assertion,
gives its source, and proves only the deductions made from it.  The precise
irreducible cores are as follows.

\begin{description}[style=nextline,leftmargin=1.5em,labelindent=0pt]
\item[\Cref{app:valuation-algebra}.]
The converse and finite Rees-valuation reduction in the valuative criterion
for integral closure are imported at the stated Noetherian/Nagata
hypotheses from \cite[Theorems~6.8.3 and~10.2.2]{SwansonHuneke2006},
with the classical background in \cite[Chapter~VI]{ZariskiSamuelII}; the square--cube and universal
homeomorphism criteria are exactly Stacks Project Tags 0CND, 0EUH, and 04DF;
and the additive-power formula is exactly
\cite[Theorem~4.17, Proposition~4.2, and Corollary~4.3]{RobertsSingh}.

\item[\Cref{app:birational-geometry}.]
The imported scheme-theoretic cores are admissible domination (Tag 081T),
Raynaud--Gruson flattening (Tag 0815), and Noetherian Stein factorization
(Tag 03H0), together with the normalization statement used for its middle
term (Tag 035L).  Resolution and simultaneous principalization are the stated
Hironaka inputs \cite{Hironaka1964I,Hironaka1964II}; the smooth projective
alteration input is \cite[Theorem~4.1]{deJong1996}.

\item[\Cref{app:ideals}.]
The analytic inputs are Nadel coherence
\cite[Theorem~10.20]{Bou17} and strong openness
\cite[Theorem~1.1]{GZ15}.  The algebraic inputs are local vanishing
\cite[Theorem~9.4.1]{PAG2}, subadditivity
\cite[Theorem~2.3]{DEL00}, geometric Skoda division
\cite[Section~9.6]{PAG2}, and the uniform-generation input used in
\cite[Theorem~5.20]{BJ22}.  The regular \(F\)-finite test-ideal construction and its fixed-level
subadditivity are imported from \cite[Definition~2.2, Proposition~2.5, Lemma~2.7, Definition~2.9 and Proposition~2.11(4)]{BMS08}.
The real-exponent Skoda identity uses \cite[Proposition~2.25]{BMS08}.
The additional positive-characteristic inputs are the
asymptotic test-ideal statements of
\cite[Section~3 and Proposition~3.1]{MustataTest}, Canton's discrepancy
estimate \cite[Definition~3.3 and Proposition~4.1 in arXiv v3]{Canton}, the
Brian\c con--Skoda theorem
\cite[Theorem~6.9(iii)]{ST12} (with
\cite[Theorem~2.1]{HY03}), and the fixed-datum reduction comparison
\cite[Theorem~6.8]{HY03}.

\item[\Cref{app:convexity}.]
The Lorentzian signature and solvability of the Alexandrov equation used for
Alexandrov--Fenchel are exactly
\cite[Theorems~12.3 and~13.3]{Ber26Convex}, with the classical formulation
\cite[Theorem~7.3.1]{Sch14}.  Continuity of Bedford--Taylor products for
convex superpotentials is imported from
\cite[Theorem~4.2]{Ber26Convex}.  The mixed-subdivision construction is
anchored at \cite[Theorem~5.1.7]{Sch14}; the finite-dimensional area formula
used for the smooth Alexandrov density is
\cite[Theorem~3.8]{EvansGariepy}.  All reductions from those inputs,
including degenerate bodies and the factor \(n!\), are proved in the
appendix.

\item[\Cref{app:nets}.]
The point-set arguments are proved directly.  Compactness of the probability
measure space uses only Tychonoff compactness
\cite[Chapter~5]{Kel75} and the Riesz representation theorem
\cite[Theorem~2.14]{Rud87}; the Portmanteau inequalities are then proved
from continuous minorants.

\item[\Cref{app:intersection-bdivisors}.]
Finite-level intersection theory imports the operational Chow projection
formula \cite[Proposition~2.3(c)]{Ful}.  The extension to nef Weil
\(b\)-classes imports exactly decreasing approximation
\cite[Theorem~2.1]{DF20}, existence and independence
\cite[Proposition--Definition~3.1]{DF20}, and upper semicontinuity and
monotone continuity \cite[Theorem~3.2]{DF20}.

\item[\Cref{app:tropical-algebra}.]
The analytic-calibration theorem imports precisely the local-degree and
gluing statements
\cite[Proposition~14.3.1, \S14.3.2, Lemma~14.3.4, and
Definition~14.3.5]{CLD25}, harmony
\cite[Theorem~15.6.1]{CLD25}, and calibrated pushforward
\cite[Proposition~14.4.2]{CLD25}.  The balancing and lattice-index
deductions are proved in the appendix.

\item[\Cref{app:complex-reference}.]
The nonpluripolar, relative-energy, envelope, strong-openness, geodesic, and
test-curve inputs are isolated by exact internal anchors to
\cref{chap:complex-prerequisites,chap:rays-test-curves}; unrestricted
total-mass monotonicity is imported from
\cite[Theorem~1.1]{DDNL18mono}, with the small-unbounded-locus predecessor
\cite[Theorem~1.16]{BEGZ10}.  In particular,
the analytic-singularity assertion for the ideal envelope uses envelope
maximality and is not deduced from divisorial orders alone.
The multiplier-ideal reconstruction uses the uniform-
\(\eta\) valuative criterion \cite[Corollary~10.18]{Bou17}, not separate
strict inequalities with a divisor-dependent margin.
\end{description}

\Cref{app:stacks,app:normalizations,app:notation,app:provenance} contain
dependency, convention, notation, and provenance ledgers rather than new
theorem-like environments.  Their displayed formulas are cross-referenced
to proofs or foundational inputs in the preceding appendices and main
chapters.

\section{Hypothesis signatures}
\label{sec:appL-signatures}

The short signatures below are used only in this appendix.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{.16\textwidth}p{.72\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Code & Meaning \\
\midrule
\(\mathrm{CDVF}_0\)
  & complete discretely valued field with residue characteristic zero \\
\(\mathrm{CNA}\)
  & complete nontrivially valued non-Archimedean field \\
\(\mathrm{TVF}\)
  & trivially valued field \\
\(\mathrm{SmProj}\)
  & smooth projective variety \\
\(\mathrm{UniProj}\)
  & integral projective unibranch variety \\
\(\mathrm{Amp}\)
  & ample line bundle or ample numerical class \\
\(\mathrm{Big}\)
  & big line bundle or big cohomology class \\
\(\mathrm{Kah}\)
  & compact Kähler manifold or the stated compact Kähler space \\
\(\mathrm{Mod}\)
  & model, formal, or PL regularity \\
\(\mathrm{Cont}\)
  & continuous potentials or metrics \\
\(\mathrm{Bd}\)
  & bounded psh potentials \\
\(\mathrm E^1\)
  & the full-mass finite-energy domain in the relevant theory \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The signatures are reminders, not substitutes for theorem statements.
For example, \(\mathrm{CDVF}_0+\mathrm{SmProj}+\mathrm{Amp}\) does not record
the algebraizability or support conditions in the original BFJ equation.
Those conditions are written separately below and in
\crefrange{chap:bfj-ma}{chap:bfj-continuity}.

  {Known-source application in \Cref{ex:height-one-idp-obstruction}.
   The published Hilbert-partition obstruction is an explicit import.
   The integral coordinate identification and the local toric family
   give a reduced central fibre and an index-$39$ singular cone whose
   smooth reduced toric subdivision over the same parameter would
   require an excluded unimodular triangulation. No nontoric or
   after-base-change impossibility is asserted. The constant-coordinate
   equivalence with coherent triangulability retains its special
   hypothesis and its earlier two-parameter provenance.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Cyclic quotients and height-one local models}
  {\cite[Example~3.10 and Theorem~3.1]{SS20FujikiV4}, version~4}
  {Known-source worked application in
   \Cref{ex:fujiki-oka-height-one-model}. The quotient
   $\tfrac1{15}(1,2,4,8)$ is realized as a flat local toric family.
   Four specified stars give fifteen cones of lattice multiplicity one;
   an explicit rational concave lifting verifies projectivity, and all
   eight primitive rays have parameter height one. The smooth charts
   have $\tau=z_1z_2z_3z_4$. These are direct finite calculations for
   this known example, with no new general criterion, global gluing
   assertion or independent verifier receipt. The source's general
   criterion concerns its Fujiki--Oka procedure; its iterated-term
   obstruction in Proposition~3.9 is explicitly toric.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Semipositive metrics}
  {\cite{Zhang1995,GM19}}
  {Reconstruction of algebraic, formal, PL, and uniform-limit
   semipositivity in \cref{chap:metrics}.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Model measures}
  {\cite{CL06}}
  {Deep import for the original Chambert--Loir intersection measure;
   agreement with later constructions is proved only on common loci.}
\end{AppLProvenanceLedger}

The signs of model functions are conventions of this book, checked directly:
\[
                       \phi_D=v(f_D),
               \qquad \phi_{\cX_s}=1.
\tag{L.2}\label{eq:appL-model-sign}
\]
No citation can replace this normalization check, since several sources use
opposite metric weights.

The main source for \cref{part:cld} is version~2 of the
Chambert--Loir--Ducros manuscript \cite{CLD25}.  The exposition is rebuilt in
a different order: Euclidean superforms and polyhedral Stokes come first,
then tropical charts, analytic forms and currents, and finally singular
products and metrics.

\begin{remark}[No universal CLD--BFJ theorem]
\label{rem:appL-cld-bfj-boundary}
The model and continuous common loci in
\cref{chap:bfj-ma,chap:master-dictionary,chap:compare-ma} have proved
comparison maps.  The ledger contains no theorem identifying every singular
CLD psh function with every global BFJ psh metric.
\end{remark}

one-variable function field, and a prescribed measure supported on the dual
complex of an SNC model.

The addendum \cite{BJ22b} proves the envelope property for all numerical
classes when \(X\) is smooth and either the characteristic is zero or the
dimension is at most two, together with the stated ample-class identity for
singular varieties.  These are recorded as the last published stage before
the supplied proof.

\paragraph{Closure-package repair and verification boundary.}
The 22 August repair of \Cref{thm:bj-basic-closure} uses the original closure
package of \cite[Definition~4.1, Theorem~4.5, Lemmas~4.6 and~4.9,
Theorem~4.7, Corollary~4.17, Theorem~4.22, and Corollaries~4.23 and~4.25]{BJ22}.
It restricts scalar multiplication to \(a>0\), omits a zero-coefficient
summand rather than forming \(0(-\infty)\), and replaces the former
unconditional pointwise-convergence claim by divisorial determination and the
source-stated decreasing-net conclusion.  The correction in \cite{BJ22b}
concerns the envelope property and the energy of a regularized envelope; it
does not replace this basic closure package.  The exact repaired block is
source-audited, but its production Rethlas review is pending; hence it is not
described here as a Rethlas-verified new result.
The 5 October source check additionally records that the strict comparison
in \cite[Lemma~4.6, equation~(4.5)]{BJ22} does not hold at common
$-\infty$ values.  The general closure assertion of Theorem~4.5 is
retained as an identified source input; its unrestricted abstract proof
is not treated as independently repaired.  This proof-provenance issue
is separate from the changing divisor coefficient in Theorem~6.21.

The metric geometry of graded norms and valuations used in
\cref{chap:bj-test-configurations} and
\crefrange{chap:bj-strong-topologies}{chap:bj-stability} is sourced to
\cite{BJKS1}; divisorial stability and openness are
sourced to \cite{BJKS2}.  The synthetic theory of finite-energy measures is
\cite{BJ25FiniteEnergy}.  Green functions are used only through the regularity
warnings and exact citations given in
\cref{chap:bj-finite-energy,chap:bj-linear-growth}.

\paragraph{BJ22 Siu-division proof correction, 4 October 2026.}
The proof of \cite[Theorem~6.21, pp.~736--737]{BJ22} makes an incorrect
monotonicity inference for $\psi_i-t_i\log|s_E|$ with varying $t_i$.
\Cref{warn:ch35-siu-source-gap} records the critical endpoint as the
additional assertion $\mathrm{(SD)}_k$; it is no longer treated as an
established foundational import. The strict fixed-coefficient proof is
separate. \cite[Proposition~17]{BJGreen24} uses independently known
envelope properties under its smooth characteristic-zero hypotheses and
does not repair the arbitrary normal case in an envelope proof.
No failure of the theorem itself or new independent verifier receipt is
claimed. The general supplied-manuscript envelope route and its dependent
uses inherit this unresolved input.

The same boundary is stated in the reading routes and the standing inputs
of \Crefrange{chap:bj-energy}{chap:bj-stability}. Their envelope-dependent
compactness, existence and completeness conclusions require the envelope
property in the class used; general unibranchness supplies it through this
assembly only under $\mathrm{(SD)}_k$. The Calabi--Yau corollary therefore
retains that envelope hypothesis. Finitary intersection and fixed-class
decreasing regularization retain their separate scopes.

For the smooth complex projective variety in
\Cref{chap:dxz-bj-comparison}, \cite[Theorem~A]{BJ22b} supplies the
all-class envelope input independently. Its comparison of suprema does
not use the conditional general unibranch assembly.

\section{The general envelope argument and its extra input}
\label{sec:appL-envelope}

\Crefrange{chap:bj-envelope-statement}{chap:bj-envelope-consequences}
reconstruct the proof in the user-supplied archive
\emph{The Envelope Property for Unibranch Projective Varieties}.  At the
source freeze, the archive has no complete author and publication metadata.
It is therefore cited in prose as the supplied envelope manuscript rather
than assigned a fictitious bibliographic entry.

Subject to $\mathrm{(SD)}_k$ of \Cref{warn:ch35-siu-source-gap}, the
assembled statement in the book is:
\[
\begin{gathered}
k\ \text{algebraically closed and trivially valued},\quad
X\ \text{integral, projective, and unibranch},\\
\theta\in N^1(X)_{\mathbf R}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\text{the BJ envelope property for }\theta .
\end{gathered}
\tag{L.5}\label{eq:appL-envelope-scope}
\]
Together with \cite[Theorem~5.12]{BJ22}, this yields the geometric
characterization by unibranchness in the ample case.  The two directions have
different provenance: necessity is the published BJ theorem; sufficiency is
the supplied manuscript.

\subsection{Tropical spectra of complex spaces}
\label{sec:appL-complex-spectra}

The compact semivaluation space \(X^{\bethan}\), PL functions, divisorial
density, inverse limits of dual complexes, log discrepancy, psh and
finite-energy functions, and the cscK interface are sourced to
\cite{MP24}.  \Crefrange{chap:tropical-spectra-complex}{chap:mp-psh}
distinguish:

\begin{enumerate}
\item topological and algebraic recovery of the complex space;
\item PL and \(b\)-divisor constructions;
\item psh and finite-energy completions; and
\item the Kähler, \(\cI\)-maximal, finite-energy comparison with test-curve
      data proved in the source.
\end{enumerate}

The local algebraic--analytic step inside the divisorial comparison has its
primary provenance in \cite[Aussage~2.8]{Bingener76Stein}.  Its exact
hypotheses are a proper \(A\)-scheme of finite presentation and a Stein
compactum.  For the singleton compactum, excellence, analytification,
coherent algebraization, closed subspaces, and morphisms through graphs are
tracked through \cite[Corollary~4.4.5, Theorems~5.3.1 and 5.4.4, and
Corollaries~5.4.12--5.4.14]{XiaStein26}.  This input is used only to turn a
local algebraic prime-divisor center into an analytic codimension-one center;
it supplies none of the later positivity or finite-energy statements.

The last item is not a comparison of unrestricted psh cones; the
topological identification of underlying projective analytifications in
\cref{chap:tropical-spectra-complex} does not supply such a positivity theorem.

A separate source-explicit argument in \cref{chap:pseff-mp-dxz}
does supply the unrestricted comparison in the projective ample-class
framework.  It algebraizes the finitely many flag ideals needed for each
rational PL function, compares relative nefness on common refinements, and
then applies the MP and BJ decreasing-completion theorems.  Its exact frozen
statement and proof have SHA-256
\begin{quote}
\footnotesize\ttfamily
e40829e3586bcabeeb866229dc068c2e518a736fc908dec32f3bf3957c59c9ab\\
baad23068e9dcae47619f3e2d81cfd26fa5bd51b1e4c4d4baa1a7c024824e671.
\end{quote}
The production archive
\nolinkurl{p001_mp_bj_full_cone_run_20260828} records verdict
\texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps after two independent
exact-byte audits.  The final exact-live statement and proof have SHA-256
\begin{quote}
\footnotesize\ttfamily
949819c48c8d0e991acbb92ed7c15d504dd691056f06553c58ac1d4ab564d33e\\
58c2954ad1e541ab41e0649c4e84e22252299265b422159df41fe5772f2e8064.
\end{quote}
The superseding archive
\nolinkurl{p001_mp_bj_full_cone_live_run2_20260828} records verdict
\texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps.  The argument is a book-level synthesis
of \cite{MP24,BJ22,DXZ23}, with no novelty or priority claim.

The transcendental non-Archimedean Calabi--Yau theorem, weak and strong
topologies, measure approximation on dual complexes, and the model-stability
application are sourced to the 2025 preprint \cite{MPWN25}.  They are dated
preprint results.  \Cref{chap:mpwn-k-stability} does not turn model uniform K-stability into
every formulation of ordinary compact-Kähler YTD.

The positive-product formulas used in that application require
\(f_D\geq1\).  The normalized model inequality is equivalent to
finite-energy coercivity; stability for all raw big classes implies it.
\Cref{ex:mpwn-big-translation-failure} shows that arbitrary base
translation need not preserve the raw invariants.
The additional comparison needed for the converse to the all-big-model
condition is recorded in \Cref{rem:mpwn-unnormalized-model-boundary}.

Three source-normalization issues are isolated rather than silently
attributed to the cited papers.  First, MP24 equation~(3.2.4) constructs
total discrepancy and Definition~6.2.1 integrates it, but the resulting
entropy-slope assertion fails the constant-ray test.  The later primary
source \cite[Definition~2.4.1]{MPWN25} explicitly defines entropy with total
discrepancy minus one.  \Cref{chap:mp-dual-complexes} imports that convention,
while \Cref{chap:mp-psh} records the obstruction and does not promote the
expected repair of the MP24 proof to an independently proved theorem.  The
finite-energy cscK implication is imported directly from
\cite[Theorem~2.4.3]{MPWN25}.  Second, MPWN25
Section~2.4.2 prints the coefficient
\(s=-V^{-1}\alpha^{n-1}\cdot K_X\), while Definition~1.1.1, equation~(9.1.2),
and Theorem~9.1.1 force the factor \(n\) used in
\eqref{eq:mpwn-s-constant}; its verification marker is
\texttt{mpwn-factor-n-correction}.  This correction is independently
verified, with verdict \texttt{correct} and no gaps.  Its audit data are
\begin{description}[style=nextline,leftmargin=1.5em,labelindent=0pt]
\item[Receipt]
\texttt{20260814T175959Z\_484442fcc393}.
\item[Proof digest]
\texttt{403ac2a601abe51c}\\*
\texttt{1d0b4a8852f52b42}\\*
\texttt{004332088236a00b}\\*
\texttt{e3e8460c80af7527}.
\end{description}
Third, the printed proof of
Theorem~7.1.2 checks a finite vertex set where boundedness of the whole dual
complex is needed.  The missing convex-geometric boundedness step is supplied
in \Cref{thm:mpwn-convex-restriction} under marker
\par\nopagebreak
\noindent\texttt{mpwn-dual-complex-boundary-repair}.
\par\nopagebreak
\noindent This repair is independently
verified with verdict \texttt{correct} and no gaps.  Its audit data are
\begin{description}[style=nextline,leftmargin=1.5em,labelindent=0pt]

\texttt{20260814T181019Z\_3beefd710782}.

The standalone reduced entropy-slope bridge remains pending independent
verification.  It has no \texttt{RETHLAS-NEW} marker and is not used as a
proved intermediate theorem; only the later source's explicitly stated
cscK implications are imported.

Version~2 of 19 May 2026 of the overview \cite{BJ26ComplexNA} is used for
orientation and current-status cross-checks, not as a substitute for proof
sources.  Version~2 of 17 August 2026 of \cite{Trusiani26} is mentioned only
with its smooth polarized projective and automorphism-relative hypotheses and
dependency warnings.  It retains Theorems~A--B and Corollaries~A--B, the
projective entropy-regularization/YTD claims, and the corresponding
maximal-ray entropy-slope equality; adds Theorem~C for the weighted-relative
smooth polarized correspondence and its log-concave weighted-extremal
consequence; and includes an appendix by S\'ebastien Boucksom proving the
entropy approximation over \(k((t))\) in the non-Archimedean formalism.  None
of these dated preprint claims is promoted here to a timeless theorem about
all compact Kähler manifolds.

   Two independent exact-byte hostile audits passed.  Production run

   controlling receipt records the full digests.
   Automatic scalar-extension stability for arbitrary continuous Hessian

\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Finite-flat continuous traces and arbitrary-field strict discs}
  {\cref{prop:p062-finite-flat-continuous-trace,%
   prop:p062-arbitrary-field-disc}}
  {Complete nontrivially valued fields.  Calibrated fibre integration under
   a finite flat map gives a continuous trace, identifies its current with
   direct image, and preserves zero Hessian.  Applying this to the minimal
   polynomial of each rigid point, with a graded Gauss-fibre degree
   calculation that retains inseparable multiplicity, proves constancy of
   every continuous zero-current function on a strict open disc and on a
   strict closed disc when the equation holds on its analytic interior.
   The statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{67a8d8a21e23}/\texttt{479643ee3aa5}; full digests are displayed
   in \cref{chap:delta-forms} and the controlling receipt.  An independent
   exact-byte
   hostile audit passed.  The dependency-closed production request
   \nolinkurl{20260830T033245Z_5c4711a474d8}, using
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh}, returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, zero gaps, and thirteen checked
   citation uses; the endpoint assigned no run or item identifier.
   At this verification gate, arbitrary-field strict polydiscs in dimension
   at least two and arbitrary analytic germs remained outside the result.
   The first scope is covered by the next record; no novelty or priority
   claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Continuous zero-current classification on standard strict endpoint
   polycylinders}
  {\cref{prop:p062-strict-product-fibre-slicing-v4-research,%
   thm:p062-endpoint-polycylinder-v4-research}}
  {Complete nontrivially valued fields; finite products of standard strict
   open or closed discs and standard strict open, closed, or half-closed
   annuli.  If the CLD Hessian current of a continuous function vanishes on
   the absolute analytic interior, then the function is uniquely
   \(c+\sum_j\lambda_j\log|S_j|\) on the whole product.  The proof slices
   only on the product of the factor interiors and reaches included endpoint
   faces through rigid base points, finite residue extensions, the
   one-dimensional endpoint theorem, and density.  The principal statement
   and proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{56ffaad6c42e0a14}/\texttt{2616ac69cd450010}.  An independent
   exact-byte hostile re-audit passed.  Dependency-closed production Rethlas
   using \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh} returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps; the request,
   response, and header SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{7b5bdbb4a15fb8d5}/\texttt{c0d9aaf5a8ef3400}/%
   \texttt{1013a47eaabe8bde}.  The theorem itself does not cover non-strict
   radii; the next record gives both the obstruction and the scalar-stable
   repair.  Arbitrary analytic germs, nonproduct local domains, and an
   equation known only on the coordinatewise strict radial core remain
   outside the result.  P06.2 stays active and the atom count is unchanged.
   No novelty or priority claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Boundary resonance and the scalar-stable arbitrary-radius repair}
  {\cref{prop:p062-nonstrict-boundary-resonance-counterexample,%
   prop:p062-nonstrict-scalar-stable-repair}}
  {For \(R\notin\sqrt{|K^\times|}\), the reciprocal-radius closed bidisc
   contains a nonempty relatively open tube in the absolute analytic
   boundary.  A nonconstant continuous cutoff supported there has zero CLD
   Hessian on the interior, disproving the unrestricted arbitrary-radius
   extension.  For every standard endpoint polycylinder with arbitrary
   positive radii, the full formula
   \(h=c+\sum_j\lambda_j\log|S_j|\) is restored if the pulled-back current
   vanishes on the full absolute interior after one complete strictifying
   extension.  The exact principal statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{27ff3ad7a6174b8b}/\texttt{15ff9b3a28b63c1e}.
   An independent exact-byte hostile re-audit passed, and the
   dependency-closed production request using \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at
   \texttt{xhigh} returned \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero
   gaps.  The request, response, and header digests begin with
   \texttt{bb90f025d3e6fb61}/\texttt{839c5b3f0506f978}/%
   \texttt{3f31d65b71c13800}.  No automatic ascent of an arbitrary
   continuous Hessian current and no nonproduct-germ conclusion is inferred.
   P06.2 remains active and the atom count is unchanged.  No novelty or
   priority claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Scalar-stable descent through proper smooth curve bundles}
  {\cref{prop:p062-scalar-stable-proper-curve-bundle-descent}}
  {Over an arbitrary complete nontrivially valued field, a continuous
   zero-current function on a locally trivial bundle with geometrically
   connected smooth proper curve fibre descends uniquely to a continuous
   zero-current function on the base, provided every rigid fibre
   restriction retains zero Hessian after one completed algebraic closure
   of its completed residue field.  Wanner comparison and the maximum
   principle give rigid-fibre constancy; a finite faithfully flat
   multisection from an arbitrary closed point replaces a rational section,
   including over imperfect fields.  The exact statement/proof SHA-256
   prefixes are
   \texttt{cce5a5ff689f110e}/\texttt{b371852f0f87b4a1}.  The repaired
   standalone packet passed the local verifier as item
   \nolinkurl{pi_3cceee63c457c144abd9869c}, using
   \texttt{gpt-6-astra} at \texttt{xhigh}, final \texttt{correct}, zero
   critical errors, and zero gaps.  The rejected precursor is retained
   because it exposed the false claim that every closed point on a smooth
   curve has separable residue field.  No automatic scalar ascent of the
   downstairs current, arbitrary-fibration conclusion, or nonproduct-germ
   conclusion is asserted.  P06.2 remains active and the atom count is
   unchanged.  No novelty or priority claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Vanishing CLD current for a proper full-rank moment factor}

  {Good separated strict pure-dimensional analytic spaces over complete
   nontrivially valued fields; the forward proposition allows boundary, while

   through the displayed proper full-rank moment and does not resolve the

\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {First-stage collapse for finite ambient-induced real-affine-star psh sheaves}
  {\cref{thm:ch16-collapse-finite-tropical-curves}}
  {Finite unions of pairwise distinct half-open rays in a finite-dimensional
   real vector space, with smooth functions and smooth psh generators induced
   from the ambient vector space; and spaces locally identified with these
   models in a way that preserves the generator sheaf.  The first-stage and
   full generated model psh sheaves equal the branchwise-convex
   junction-Jensen sheaf, with decreasing smooth-psh approximation on one fixed
   neighbourhood.  The exact statement SHA-256 digest is
   \texttt{c8131b22d8002212}\allowbreak
   \texttt{e31cd52609531773}\allowbreak
   \texttt{4a2b67be5d47f138}\allowbreak
   \texttt{7e5ee6bc041af5e3}; the proof digest is
   \texttt{921e8b46690e69cb}\allowbreak
   \texttt{acea66bf86cbcdf6}\allowbreak
   \texttt{28e7dbc95651f526}\allowbreak
   \texttt{d62cc6d755d7cbcf}.  The direct production endpoint, using
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh}, returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps; its response is
   preserved under local archive label
   \nolinkurl{20260827T103835Z_c8131b22d800}.  The direct endpoint assigned
   no run identifier and no item identifier.  This does not identify all
   sedentary or nonambient tropical-boundary smooth structures with the
   ambient-induced model, and it does not treat infinite stars or arbitrary
   higher dimension. Both P07.1 and P07.2 remained active, so the inventory
   stayed at 55 atoms at that verification gate. The later P00.1 retirement
   and subsequent supplied integrations are recorded separately; the current
   total is given in \Cref{sec:appL-current-boundary}.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {First-stage CLD collapse on the exact CLD infinite star}
  {\cref{thm:p071-infinite-star-collapse-candidate}}
  {For every nonempty branch set \(B\), the exact star \(X_B\) of
   \cite[\S6.7.3]{CLD25}, with its cofinite-branch topology at the centre and
   its balanced smooth structure in which only finitely many branches fail
   to be both full and constant, has
   \(\operatorname{Psh}_{X_B,1}=\operatorname{Psh}_{X_B}=\mathscr J_B\).
   The comparison sheaf \(\mathscr J_B\) is given by branchwise extended-real
   convexity, exact trace at every included endpoint \(-\infty\), and the
   finite-subsum junction inequalities.  Every germ has a decreasing
   smooth-psh approximation on one fixed neighbourhood; at a finite centre
   value only countably many full branches can be nonconstant, while centre
   value \(-\infty\) forces local identically \(-\infty\).  The exact statement
   SHA-256 digest is
   \texttt{99c1a6a6a712005e}\hspace{0pt}
   \texttt{73ee3b7cdae29760}\hspace{0pt}
   \texttt{6895b22ce79c80a6}\hspace{0pt}
   \texttt{411dfd7f4863c963}; the proof digest is
   \texttt{ca49ad210cf8e641}\hspace{0pt}
   \texttt{00cc00c31c16a76f}\hspace{0pt}
   \texttt{207ecd0d4b37dc36}\hspace{0pt}
   \texttt{5e8e1c61ce903090}.  Production item
   \texttt{pi\_00f6c7330308}\hspace{0pt}
   \texttt{7d94da9eb724}, using
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh}, returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps.  This theorem
   removed the blanket infinite-star residual but did not retire P07.1 or
   P07.2 at that gate: arbitrary higher-dimensional spaces and other nonambient
   local models remained open. The inventory then stayed at 53 atoms in 14
   formal problem environments. The later supplied P07.1 counterexample is
   recorded in \Cref{sec:provenance-supplied-20260924}.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {First-stage collapse on an exact infinite star times a finite
   constant-collar endpoint}
  {\cref{thm:p071-infinite-star-finite-collar-product-collapse}}
  {For every nonempty exact CLD branch set, the first-stage and full
   generated psh sheaves on the product with the nonambient finite
   constant-collar endpoint coincide with the explicit comparison sheaf.
   The comparison conditions retain joint branch--interval convexity,
   exact terminal traces, countable node activity, the product junction
   Jensen inequality, and the endpoint boundary minimum.  The proof uses
   positive-part extension, a monotone-coordinate refinement of the
   verified star--Euclidean regularization, and nested smooth convex clamps
   which restore exact collars.  The exact statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes
   are \texttt{55480e4cfcb6f0af}/\texttt{004b9441221acb56}.  The repaired
   four-dependency packet passed the local verifier as item
   \nolinkurl{pi_dab08cd0ade65f20ea9867b4}, using
   \texttt{gpt-6-astra} at \texttt{xhigh}, final \texttt{correct}, zero
   critical errors, and zero gaps.  Two rejected precursors remain archived:
   they exposed missing dependency records and the need for separate compact
   cores at node, ordinary-branch, and full-endpoint strata.  The result does
   not cover the collar endpoint at \(-\infty\), products of two branched
   stars, non-CLD infinite stars, or nonconstant Stone-affine directions.
   At this checkpoint P07.1 remained active and the atom count was unchanged.
   No novelty or priority claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {First-stage collapse on an exact infinite star times the endpoint
   \(-\infty\)}
  {\cref{thm:p071-infinite-star-infinite-collar-product-collapse}}
  {For every nonempty exact CLD branch set, the first-stage and full
   generated psh sheaves on the product with the nonambient
   constant-collar endpoint \(-\infty\) coincide with the explicit
   trace-comparison sheaf.  The proof uses ordered smooth convex clamps
   moving the endpoint to \(-n\), simultaneous finite-activity and lower
   truncations on compact strips, uniform nonnegative-slope
   star--Euclidean smoothing, composition, and a summable tail correction.
   The exact statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are

   nonconstant Stone-affine directions. At this checkpoint P07.1 remained

\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {First-stage collapse for a product with a bivalent exact star}
  {\cref{thm:p071-bivalent-star-product-collapse}}
  {Let one factor be an arbitrary exact CLD star and let the other have
   exactly two branches.  Unfolding the bivalent factor to
   \([-\infty,+\infty]\) converts its node balance law into equality of the
   one-sided normal derivatives.  Natural two-star generators are therefore
   jointly convex across the unfolded node.  Exact star--Euclidean
   approximation handles finite unfolded coordinates, and exact
   star--infinite-collar approximation handles either endpoint.  The
   first-stage and full generated sheaves coincide with the unfolded
   comparison sheaf, with decreasing approximation on one fixed smaller
   product neighbourhood.  The exact statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{20063aa4bb331d0b}/\texttt{e3083a681f91a97b}.  The
   six-dependency packet passed the local verifier as item
   \nolinkurl{pi_48270fe4a3ce7d5ad5251d47}, using
   \texttt{gpt-6-astra} at \texttt{xhigh}, final \texttt{correct}, zero
   critical errors, and zero gaps.  Products whose two node valences are
   both at least three remain outside this result. At this checkpoint P07.1
   remained active and the atom count was unchanged. No novelty or priority
   claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Mixed double-junction obstruction for two trivalent exact stars}
  {\cref{prop:p071-trivalent-double-junction-obstruction}}
  {On a product of two trivalent exact-star node charts, every section of
   the full generated sheaf satisfies the mixed Jensen inequality for every
   finite atomic configuration whose two radial marginals are balanced.
   Simultaneous radial scaling makes the weighted value convex in the scale;
   this propagates the inequality from an arbitrarily small node collar to a
   fixed product chart and closes the shrinking-neighbourhood gap through
   the sheafified hierarchy.  The function
   \(u_{ij}(r,s)=r+s-3\delta_{ij}\min\{r,s\}\) is jointly convex on the nine
   branch rectangles and satisfies both complete one-star--line junction
   families, but violates the mixed inequality on the three equally
   weighted diagonal atoms.  Thus coupled double-junction moments are
   necessary; their sufficiency remains open.  The exact statement/proof
   SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{160f5a469a9a43cc}/\texttt{563f8b4992f16d95}.  The repaired
   principal-only packet passed the local verifier as item
   \nolinkurl{pi_5cdbc27108d6b165871775d9}, using
   \texttt{gpt-6-astra} at \texttt{xhigh}, final \texttt{correct}, zero
   critical errors, and zero gaps.  The rejected precursor remains archived
   with its shrinking-neighbourhood diagnosis. At this checkpoint P07.1
   remained active and the atom count was unchanged. No novelty or priority
   claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Finite-rank support-plane form of the trivalent double-node condition}
  {\cref{prop:p071-double-node-support-plane-reduction}}
  {For a continuous real-valued function on the product of two open exact
   trivalent-star charts, the complete balanced double-node Jensen family
   is equivalent to its subfamily with at most five atoms and to one
   ambient affine minorant touching at the double node.  The five-atom
   reduction is an extreme-point consequence of normalization plus four
   moment coordinates.  Collarwise convex envelopes and a nested compact
   family of dual feasible sets produce one supporting plane on the whole
   open chart.  An explicit affinely independent balanced five-point
   configuration shows that five is sharp for barycentre representation.
   The result removes infinite atomic support from the residual mechanism
   but does not prove sufficiency or retire P07.1.  The exact
   statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{12b983557fd9a0da}/\texttt{ec523a5fbe05d10d}.  The repaired
   principal-only packet passed the local verifier as item
   \nolinkurl{pi_34acd32abc0bfae9990b71d7}, using
   \texttt{gpt-6-astra} at \texttt{xhigh}, final \texttt{correct}, zero
   critical errors, and zero gaps.  The two superseded typesetting runs
   remain archived. At this checkpoint P07.1 remained active and the atom count
   was unchanged.
   No novelty or priority claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Off-centre convex-roof obstruction on two trivalent exact stars}
  {\cref{prop:p071-offcenter-convex-roof-obstruction}}
  {A smooth quadratic member of the stage-zero trivalent two-star generator
   class has positive-semidefinite Hessian on every branch rectangle, common
   smooth axis restrictions, and zero row and column sums in its mixed
   coefficient matrix.  Five explicitly weighted atoms have ambient
   barycentre \((p_2(1/4),q_2(2))\), but violate the corresponding Jensen
   inequality by exactly \(7169/688\).  Hence the generated sheaf is not an
   unrestricted ambient-barycentric convex class, and a convex-roof
   extension to the ambient hull cannot establish sufficiency of the mixed
   double-node conditions.  The exact statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{8d97947f837ea1b4}/\texttt{068e16e7e4a07c08}.  The
   principal-only packet passed the local verifier as item
   \nolinkurl{pi_d4506c7b32b78fce239673a8}, using
   \texttt{gpt-6-astra} at \texttt{xhigh}, final \texttt{correct}, zero
   critical errors, and zero gaps. At this checkpoint P07.1 remained active
   and the atom count was unchanged. No novelty or priority claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Affine cross-collar smoothing on a trivalent two-star product}
  {\cref{prop:p071-trivalent-affine-cross-collar-smoothing}}
  {Let a continuous finite function on a trivalent--trivalent product be
   convex and piecewise affine on the nine branch rectangles and agree with
   one ambient affine function whenever either radial coordinate is
   sufficiently small.  Finite affine-maximum representations and a raised
   copy of the common affine minorant permit exact regularized maxima which
   glue smoothly across both node axes and preserve both balance laws.  A
   summable tail gives a uniformly convergent decreasing generator sequence
   on one fixed product neighbourhood.  Arbitrary finite polyhedral mixed
   coupling is allowed away from the axes, but the affine cross-collar is an
   extra hypothesis, so P07.1 remained active at this checkpoint. The exact
   statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{2e538f70b29d2ce3}/\texttt{396d2be290d6262c}.  The
   principal-only packet passed the local verifier as item
   \nolinkurl{pi_725a917f37c426b97fa2db13}, using
   \texttt{gpt-6-astra} at \texttt{xhigh}, final \texttt{correct}, zero
   critical errors, and zero gaps.  The atom count is unchanged.  No
   novelty or priority claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {First-stage CLD collapse on Stone--polyhedral products}
  {\cref{thm:p071-stone-polyhedral-collapse}}
  {Let \(Z\) be a locally compact Hausdorff space with a clopen basis and
   only locally constant affine directions, and let \(P\) be one nonempty
   convex polyhedron, possibly unbounded, lower-dimensional, or
   zero-dimensional.  The first-stage and full generated psh sheaves on
   \(Z\times P\) coincide with the upper-semicontinuous functions which
   locally factor through a compact metrizable zero-dimensional quotient
   and are extended-real convex in the \(P\)-variable.  Every germ has a
   decreasing smooth-psh approximation on one fixed product neighbourhood.
   The principal statement and proof SHA-256 digests begin with
   \texttt{b46b8e22fa8b3f8f} and
   \texttt{37a3b6a12f6ddd2a}, respectively; the theorem text and its
   production receipt record the complete digests.
   After independent mathematical and exact-source audits, a
   dependency-closed production request using \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at
   \texttt{xhigh} returned final \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors,
   and zero gaps.  The request and response digests begin with
   \texttt{6697bc79ad50c8b7} and
   \texttt{c5871408ef86be15}, respectively; the production receipt records
   the complete digests.
   The theorem covers every face and corner of a single convex chamber and
   chartwise transports preserving the generator sheaf.  It does not cover
   nonconvex or branched chamber gluings or nonconstant affine directions
   on \(Z\), and it does not retire P07.1.  No novelty or priority claim is
   made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {First-stage collapse on generator-convex finite polyhedral complexes}
  {\cref{thm:p071-generator-convex-polyhedral-complex-collapse}}
  {Let \(\Sigma\) be a finite Hausdorff face-to-face polyhedral complex
   whose complete smooth-psh generator germ is locally identified, in both
   directions, with the single-chamber generator sheaf on a relatively open
   convex subset of one polyhedron.  For every locally compact Hausdorff
   clopen-base parameter \(Z\), the first-stage, full generated, and
   chartwise Stone-factorized fibrewise-convex psh sheaves on
   \(Z\times|\Sigma|\) coincide.  Every germ admits a decreasing
   smooth-psh approximation on one fixed neighbourhood crossing all chart
   faces.  The principal statement and proof SHA-256 digests begin with
   \texttt{2bc8574fecdc46ec} and \texttt{ab3348d8d0dc31c4}; the
   dependency-closed materializations begin with
   \texttt{e163679c6f1140eb} and \texttt{1505aa473b3092f8}.
   An independent exact-byte hostile reaudit passed.  Production Rethlas,
   using \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh}, returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps.  The request,
   response, and header digests begin with \texttt{dd51939097d53f3c},
   \texttt{8bab39fea7dd28f7}, and \texttt{35f9ddd2168e155c}; the production
   receipt records every complete digest.  The verified two-ray example
   shows that topological unbranching and independent cellwise positivity
   do not imply the required generator-preserving development: the ambient
   affine function \(-x-y\) unfolds to \(-|t|\).  Branched links, singular
   lower links, nonconstant affine parameter directions, and other germs
   without such a development remain outside this result. At this checkpoint
   P07.1 remained active and the atom count was unchanged. No novelty or
   priority claim is made.}

\AppLProvenanceRecord

\AppLProvenanceRecord

\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Dimension-one positive-current classification and continuous-pair
   Bedford--Taylor independence}
  {\cref{lem:p21p3-gf-legal-source-localization,%
   prop:p21p3-gf-virtual-annulus-bridge,%
   lem:p21p3-gfc-rigid-zero-atom,%
   prop:p21p3-curve-rigidity,%
   prop:p21p3-rigid-exception-extension,%
   prop:p21p3-curve-rigidity-nonreduced,%
   lem:p21p3-componentwise-normal-rigid-zero-atom,%
   prop:p21p3-componentwise-rigid-exceptions,%
   rem:p21p3-componentwise-residual,%
   rem:p21p3-nr-double-line}}
  {\begingroup\setlength{\emergencystretch}{2em}%
   Over every complete nontrivially valued field, on a good separated strict
   pure one-dimensional boundaryless analytic space, suppose that every
   normalized reduced irreducible component is rig-smooth away from a
   locally finite set of rigid points.  Then every positive closed current
   of bidegree \((0,0)\) is a unique locally finite nonnegative linear
   combination of the normalized component currents.  No reducedness or
   irreducibility assumption is made.  Legal localization through analytic
   moments and canonical virtual-annular collars retains residual closed
   points of arbitrary degree.  The normal-curve extension treats the rigid
   exceptional points, and the local normal zero-atom estimate prevents
   cancellation at rigid conductor and intersection points.  Reduction
   preserves the ordinary and \(G\)-form calculi, compact-test topology,
   currents, positivity, closedness, and psh sheaves, while the fundamental
   current records the generic multiplicities.  The result proves
   Bedford--Taylor independence for continuous psh pairs; it does not assert
   the literal discontinuous predicate of
   \cite[Definition~8.6.2]{CLD25}.

   The arbitrary-field reduced packet passed
   \nolinkurl{20260828T042906Z_f2a1c1b9f2a3}; the exact live nonreduced
   packet passed \nolinkurl{20260828T051601Z_db92555a72e1}; and the
   normal-curve rigid-exception packet passed
   \nolinkurl{20260829T021315Z_cc0c48725114}.  The local normal zero-atom
   packet, with exact statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes
   \texttt{469024d04e8e}/\texttt{661100a4b25e}, passed
   \nolinkurl{20260829T060048Z_469024d04e8e}, item
   \nolinkurl{pi_0d4235a44db2b33950f8dd55}.  The exact componentwise
   statement/proof prefixes are
   \texttt{5b03b18f2556}/\texttt{3893a89ffd59}; the complete dependency
   materialization has prefixes
   \texttt{2bf673687e93}/\texttt{a27c40fbb132} and passed
   \nolinkurl{20260829T061505Z_2bf673687e93}, item
   \nolinkurl{pi_ee24d64b88f0c4e60ad1acbd}.  These controlling runs used
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh} and returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps.

   The first combined materialization omitted the transitive
   canonical-collar bridge.  The rejected run was
   \nolinkurl{20260829T060846Z_9e819244c341}.  Its item was
   \nolinkurl{pi_5c78fec3148f11d4fd5a15cb}; it returned final
   \texttt{wrong}, one critical error, and zero gaps.  That noncontrolling
   packaging rejection is retained; the exact candidate theorem bytes were
   unchanged, and the successful run supplied and rechecked the complete
   dependency closure.  Exact hashes and responses are preserved in the
   dated archives.

   The classification scope of this dated nontrivially valued chain is now
   subsumed by the arbitrary-valued record below.  Its collar and local
   statements remain useful, and no novelty or priority claim is made.
   \par\endgroup}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Arbitrary-valued normal and componentwise curve classifications}
  {\cref{lem:p213-trivial-gauss-rigid-zero-atom,%
   lem:p213-variation-graph-source-cutoff,%
   lem:p213-euclidean-one-dimensional-current-revised,%
   prop:p213-normal-curve-arbitrary-valuation-current-revised,%
   prop:p213-componentwise-arbitrary-valuation}}
  {\begingroup\setlength{\emergencystretch}{2em}%
   Over every complete non-Archimedean field, possibly trivially valued, a
   positive closed ordinary CLD current of bidegree \((0,0)\) on a connected
   good separated normal boundaryless pure analytic curve is a nonnegative
   multiple of the integration current.  No strictness or smoothness
   hypothesis is used, and the conclusion is the full CLD Bedford--Taylor
   predicate.  On a good separated strict boundaryless pure curve with
   arbitrary reducible and nonreduced structure, every such current is a
   unique locally finite nonnegative combination of normalized
   reduced-component currents.  Nilreduction preserves the ordinary CLD
   calculus and current topology while the fundamental current retains
   generic multiplicities; the componentwise conclusion gives
   Bedford--Taylor independence for continuous psh pairs.

   The exact normal statement/proof prefixes are
   \texttt{339f8cb085fd}/\texttt{d6d02762690b}.  Production item
   \nolinkurl{pi_d9543424f0492e1412e0d203} returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps.  The exact
   componentwise principal prefixes are
   \texttt{91ce52597273}/\texttt{098995604f7c}; its dependency-closed
   materialization has prefixes
   \texttt{d1ad274eb03a}/\texttt{cd7f2e4cc32c}.  Production item
   \nolinkurl{pi_1ca3e6d14c7e8596372d863d} used
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh} and returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps, with the exact
   materialized proof digest.  Independent hostile and binding audits
   passed.  Every one-dimensional case within P21.3's ambient hypotheses is
   retired; its single residual atom is now confined to analytic dimension
   at least two.  No novelty or priority claim is made.\par\endgroup}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Higher-dimensional Bedford--Taylor independence from a zero-Hessian-regular basis}
  {\cref{prop:p213-hessian-regular-basis-v2-candidate,%
   cor:p213-polycylinder-basis-v2-candidate,%
   prop:p213-fixed-polycylinder-v2-candidate}}
  {\begingroup\setlength{\emergencystretch}{2em}%
   On a good topologically separated pure boundaryless analytic space, a
   genuine ordinary-topology basis on whose members continuous zero-current
   functions are ordinary CLD-smooth with zero smooth Hessian implies
   continuous-pair Bedford--Taylor independence for every positive
   \(\mathrm d'\)- and \(\mathrm d''\)-closed CLD current.  Over a
   nontrivially valued field, a basis of standard strict open
   polycylinders has this property.  On one such polycylinder the same
   identity holds for equal-curvature pairs defined on its whole domain.
   This last assertion is deliberately fixed-domain: neither a mere atlas
   nor a polycylinder neighbourhood failing to refine the domain of the
   potentials proves the every-open predicate.

   The exact principal statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \nolinkurl{955e1ab4323d} and \nolinkurl{70f34cad9f97}; the
   dependency-closed materialization has prefixes
   \nolinkurl{6c90e664468b} and \nolinkurl{6eff86ba6c98}.  The archived
   request/response prefixes are
   \nolinkurl{add3a522de8d} and \nolinkurl{8ef692e78c8a}.  Production Rethlas used
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh} and returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps after an
   independent exact-byte hostile audit.  A superseded first candidate
   conflated a cover with a basis and was rejected before submission.  The
   repaired result narrows but does not retire P21.3.  No novelty or priority
   claim is made.\par\endgroup}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Endpoint classification through mixed locally trivial towers}
  {\cref{thm:p213-endpoint-mixed-tower-classification,%
   cor:p213-endpoint-mixed-tower-bt,%
   cor:p213-endpoint-mixed-tower-cover-bt,%
   cor:p213-endpoint-mixed-tower-basis-bt}}
  {\begingroup\setlength{\emergencystretch}{2em}%
   The strict endpoint-polycylinder classification already proved in the
   book is composed with the exact zero-current descent propositions for
   locally trivial standard strict open-polydisc, projective-space, and
   proper smooth connected curve bundles.  This yields a unique
   logarithmic-affine formula on the endpoint base through an arbitrary
   finite mixture of the three fibre types.  Pullback of ordinary CLD forms
   and the smooth multiplier rule then give the whole-space, cover, and
   basis Bedford--Taylor conclusions without pulling back a nonsmooth
   current.

   The exact dependency map and scope firewalls are preserved in
   \nolinkurl{solver_dossiers_20260911/p213_endpoint_mixed_towers_20260911}.
   The cover proof also repairs an older overbroad citation of the
   current-sheaf proposition: it globalizes ordinary smoothness of the
   difference before applying the multiplier criterion on the specified
   open.  A GPT--6 Astra hostile exact-live audit passed after three dropped
   inline-math delimiters in the new theorem's opening sentence were
   restored, with no remaining mathematical or scope gap.  The result
   narrows but does not retire P21.3, and no novelty or priority claim is
   made.
   \par\endgroup}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Residual Picard regularity and mixed-tower Bedford--Taylor independence}
  {\cref{prop:p213-residual-picard-smooth-curve,%
   thm:p213-residual-picard-mixed-tower,%
   cor:p213-residual-picard-mixed-tower-bt,%
   cor:p213-residual-picard-mixed-tower-cover-bt,%
   cor:p213-residual-picard-mixed-tower-basis-bt}}
  {\begingroup\setlength{\emergencystretch}{2em}%
   Thuillier's harmonic-germ quotient and
   \cite[Remark~15.10]{GJR26} give an exact ordinary-smooth criterion on a
   smooth analytic curve: the sole obstruction is the type--\(2\) residual
   class in \(\operatorname{Pic}^0(C_x)\otimes_{\mathbf Z}\mathbf R\).
   Under its pointwise vanishing, the book's exact descent propositions for
   locally trivial strict open-polydisc, projective-space, and proper smooth
   connected curve bundles may be iterated in any finite order.  A
   continuous zero-current function on the whole top level is then an
   ordinary CLD-smooth pullback with zero smooth Hessian.  The ordinary
   multiplier rule yields Bedford--Taylor independence against every
   positive closed CLD current on a boundaryless top level; separate cover
   and basis statements preserve the whole-space versus every-open
   quantifier.

   This is a book-local synthesis of
   \cite[Theorem~2.3.21 and Lemma~2.3.22]{Thu05},
   \cite[Proposition~15.7 and Remark~15.10]{GJR26}, the Wanner comparison
   already imported in \cref{thm:wanner-curve-comparison}, and the three
   existing bundle-descent propositions.  The exact source map and scope
   firewalls are preserved in the dated dossier
   \nolinkurl{solver_dossiers_20260911/p213_residual_picard_mixed_towers_20260911}.
   A GPT--6 Astra exact-live audit passed after the cover proof was rewritten
   to globalize ordinary smoothness before applying the current multiplier
   rule; it found no remaining mathematical gap, including at point types
   \(1,3,4\) and for arbitrary real residual slopes.  The result narrows but
   does not retire P21.3, and no novelty or priority claim is made.
   \par\endgroup}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Direct trivially valued curve classifications and cross comparison}
  {\cref{lem:p213-triv-interval-current,%
   prop:p213-triv-abstract-cross-counterexample,%
   prop:p213-triv-p1-current-rigidity,%
   prop:p213-triv-algebraic-curve-current-rigidity,%
   prop:p213-triv-four-puncture-no-go}}
  {Over an algebraically closed trivially valued field, every positive closed
   bidegree-\((0,0)\) CLD current on a connected analytic open of
   \((\mathbf P^1)^{\mathrm{an}}\), or on the analytification of a smooth
   connected projective curve and any connected Zariski open in it, is a
   nonnegative multiple of the integration current.  The conclusion is the
   full Bedford--Taylor predicate of \cite[Definition~8.6.2]{CLD25}.  The
   packet also classifies the two boundaryless interval structures, gives a
   positive closed non-Bedford--Taylor current on the abstract weighted
   cross, and proves that the natural four-punctured algebraic cross cannot
   carry its unequal weights as a closed analytic current.

   The exact live-ready statement and proof have SHA-256 prefixes
   \texttt{cd1032050a8c} and \texttt{37ed90b0b7f0}; the full
   digests are recorded in the verification ledger.  Independent exact-byte

   \nolinkurl{pi_ed915de7970791904bc0ec6e} used

   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps.  These direct cases
   do not by themselves classify every singular, nonnormal, or
   nonalgebraizable trivially valued analytic curve.  No novelty or priority
   claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {CLD support exponent and its scalar-extension index}
  {\cref{thm:ch14-cld-support-corrected-packet,%
   prop:ch14-cld-support-printed-exponent-counterexample}}
  {The arbitrary-current assertion over a nontrivially valued field is the
   sourced theorem \cite[Theorem~16.8.5]{CLD25}, reconstructed through proper
   tropical pushforward and
   \cite[Corollary~4.1]{Lag12Super}.  For a trivially valued field, the
   scalar-extension argument applies to a product of \(q\) curvature currents
   and requires tropical \(H^{n-q}\)-negligibility, because \(n-q\) is the
   bidimension.  The exponent \(H^q\) printed in
   \cite[Corollary~16.8.6]{CLD25} is an index error; the displayed Gauss-point
   kink gives a diagnostic counterexample to the printed exponent.

   The exact statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{2b291fc55872ab32} and \texttt{9f21f7ece49c761d}.  Independent
   hostile audit \texttt{62520258d3a5a4d6} passed.  Production item
   \nolinkurl{pi_d8921af65dfc0c98f29a5fb7} used
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh} and returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps; its proof digest is
   exact.  The source theorem, internal diagnosis of the printed index, and
   diagnostic example are distinguished here, and no novelty or priority
   claim is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Finite-level closed-immersion reduction for Banach-base
   Monge--Amp\`ere continuity}
  {\cref{prop:p171-closed-immersion-finite-level-reduction,rem:p171-closed-immersion-reduction-scope}}
  {Multiplying an arbitrary basepoint-free tropical FS system by all
   degree-\(m\) monomials of one fixed projective embedding gives the Segre
   product with a Veronese embedding and therefore a closed immersion.  The
   exact metric identity yields \(\phi=(\phi+\eta)-\eta\).  Fieldwise
   multilinearity and the Deligne change-of-metric formula then express every
   finite-level FS/DFS integral as a signed sum of differences of Deligne
   weights with closed-immersion systems in all slots, using one index and
   sign set throughout the Banach spectrum.

   The exact live statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{9be78feb5c4b76ae} and \texttt{ebb4c34fc1d06909}.
   Independent exact-byte audit passed.  Production item
   \nolinkurl{pi_9d4f5f4d6c4a36b32834c0a5} used
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh} and returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps, with the exact
   proof digest.  The result by itself does not prove continuity of the
   resulting relative Chow/Deligne weights; the full theorem recorded in the
   next entry supplies that remaining passage.  No novelty or priority claim
   is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Banach-base continuity of fibrewise mixed Monge--Amp\`ere integrals}
  {\cref{thm:p171-full-assembly}}
  {Let \(A\) be an integral Banach ring and let
   \(X\to\operatorname{Spec}A\) be a flat projective finite-type family of
   relative dimension \(n\), with semiample line bundles, arbitrary
   continuous psh metrics, and an arbitrary continuous test function.  The
   theorem proves continuity of the fibrewise mixed Monge--Amp\`ere integral
   on \(\mathcal M(A)\).  It includes real and complex fibres, trivially and
   nontrivially valued fibres, nonpure and nonreduced fibres evaluated on
   their top fundamental cycles, and \(n=0\).

   The proof uses finite presentation, a finite DFS/FS reduction with exactly
   \(2^{n+1}\) signed weights (two for \(n=0\)), finite-level continuity of
   relative resultants and Deligne weights, uniform CLN control, and a
   cofinal product-net density passage.  Its dependency order proves
   finite-level algebraic continuity and uniform control before invoking
   density, so it does not assume the desired continuity.

   The principal live statement/proof SHA-256 prefixes are
   \texttt{0d95354a4cd2bd15} and \texttt{9cab2f0f679b095b}; the
   dependency-closed materialized statement/proof prefixes are
   \texttt{0ba26fe924a065af} and \texttt{cdce552b3750fae}.
   Production item \nolinkurl{pi_87b42b938a8d830222bed78f} used
   \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol} at \texttt{xhigh} and returned final
   \texttt{correct}, zero critical errors, and zero gaps; its proof digest is
   the materialized proof hash.  This settles Pille--Schneider Question~4.9
   in the displayed scope.  No novelty or priority claim is made.}

\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Failure of upper-semicontinuous local-envelope regularization}
  {\cref{prop:ch15-nodal-local-envelope}}
  {A continuous real-valued obstacle on the reduced strict nodal affinoid
   curve \(\mathcal M(K\langle x,y\rangle/(xy))\).  The raw envelope and
   its regularization are computed pointwise; the latter is discontinuous
   and violates the affinoid Shilov-boundary maximum principle, hence is
   neither classically psh nor CLD-v2 psh.  Exact production run
   \nolinkurl{20260825T094005Z_10ac70696f69}, item
   \nolinkurl{pi_31f95353b7e94f3cb98b09b6}, returned final

   classification on normal or analytically unibranch domains, and no
   novelty or priority claim, is made.}
\AppLProvenanceRecord
  {Nodal-product obstruction in every higher dimension}
  {\cref{prop:p031-nodal-product-envelope-obstruction-candidate}}
  {The reduced strict nodal affinoid curve is multiplied by a closed strict
   polydisc of arbitrary positive dimension, and the obstacle is pulled back
   from the curve.  The zero-section identity and a singleton-Shilov branch
   disc force the regularized envelope to violate the classical maximum
   principle; it is therefore neither classically psh nor CLD-v2 psh.  The
   products are pure-dimensional, so the obstruction occurs in every
   analytic dimension at least two.  The principal statement and proof

The proofs use only the dependencies displayed in
\cref{chap:verified-extensions}.  A conditional
criterion is not advertised as a regularization theorem, a compact-subset
argument is not promoted globally, and a no-go result for \(\cI\)-invariant
data says nothing about a genuinely \(P\)-sensitive construction.
The label ``first-draft result'' is an internal status, not a publication or
priority claim; only the displayed hypotheses and conclusions in
\cref{chap:verified-extensions}
are asserted.

\section{The curve comparison correction of 31 August 2026}
\label{sec:appL-curve-correction-20260831}

The current curve statements are
\cref{thm:p081-corrected-curve-comparison,thm:p081-irrational-tate-period-obstruction}.
They replace an earlier assertion that every degree-zero line bundle
carrying a continuous locally psh metric admitted nef rational model
approximants.  The earlier proof did not justify rationalization while
preserving both nefness and the fixed line-bundle cocycle.  The Tate
calculation shows that the missing existence statement is false in
the asserted generality.  The old statement, its dependent transfer
proofs, and their receipts are retained in the editorial archive but are
not live mathematical dependencies.

The corrected positive statement applies over any complete nontrivially
valued field to smooth proper geometrically connected algebraic curves.
For continuous locally CLD-psh metrics, positive degree gives relatively
ample rational model approximation.  In degree zero, one nef rational
model metric must exist
before the approximation conclusion applies.  The statement and proof
SHA-256 values are, respectively,

\section{Questions not promoted to theorems}
\label{sec:appL-open}

The user requested that the first draft attempt the adjacent open questions
rather than merely list them.  The research pass produced the preceding
theorems, counterexamples, and restricted solutions.  It did not justify
positive answers to the following broader questions:

The following thematic list is not the literal problem-environment inventory.
At the 2 October 2026 editorial checkpoint, \Cref{chap:major-open-problems}
contains 12 active \texttt{problem} environments, comprising 42
independently auditable atoms with tier counts \((0,3,7,15,17)\). Each
environment has a genuinely open residual scope. Resolved, disproved and
conditional subclaims are recorded as boundaries and are not counted as
additional active problems. The older dated research records above retain
their historical counts. The general literature cutoff remains
\eqref{eq:appL-cutoff}; targeted source updates and editorial repairs do
not amount to a new exhaustive literature search.

\begin{enumerate}
\item arbitrary continuous higher-dimensional zero-current regularity and
  a global CLD--BFJ singular-psh equivalence. The supplied complete P06.1
  theorem \Cref{thm:supplied-p061-singular-pluriharmonic}, recorded in
  \Cref{sec:provenance-supplied-20260924}, retires the classical
  pluriharmonic regularity atom in its good separated strict boundaryless
  scope over a complete nontrivially valued field;
  the earlier quasi-smooth, curve-factor, toric and descent records retain
  their separate provenance. For zero-current recognition, the real-PL
  case is \Cref{prop:ch18-real-pl-zero-current-harmonic}; the proper
  full-rank moment-factor case is
  \Cref{prop:p062-proper-full-rank-moment}. Neither supplies such a
  factorization for an arbitrary continuous function. Standard strict
  endpoint polycylinders are covered by
  \Cref{thm:p062-endpoint-polycylinder-v4-research}. For arbitrary radii,
  \Cref{prop:p062-nonstrict-boundary-resonance-counterexample} disproves
  the unrestricted downstairs statement, while
  \Cref{prop:p062-nonstrict-scalar-stable-repair} proves the scalar-stable
  replacement. Arbitrary nonproduct germs and weaker boundary control
  remain in the residual. Ordinary CLD-smooth harmonic approximation is
  disproved by \Cref{prop:ch19-cld-smooth-harmonic-nonregularization},
  while ordinary CLD-smooth psh regularization on smooth proper curves is
  proved by \Cref{thm:p063-current-psh-ordinary-regularization};
\item the trace Dirichlet problem without a transverse positivity and class
  splitting hypothesis;
\item a zero-mass Okounkov body retaining enough \(P\)-type information to
  recover actual nonpluripolar mass;
\item the nontrivially valued Monge--Ampère equation with every support and
  characteristic restriction removed;
\item a general intrinsic differential positivity theory for
  non-Archimedean Kähler classes; and
\item the full ordinary compact-Kähler YTD equivalence, including the
  passage from model stability and the required automorphism-relative
  statements.
\end{enumerate}

These are status statements, not assertions that every item has a positive
answer.  Some may require new definitions and some may fail.  The first
draft's mathematical claims stop exactly at the proved boundaries in
\cref{chap:verified-extensions}.

\section{Audit checklist for future editions}
\label{sec:appL-future}

Before a cited theorem is enlarged in a later edition, the following checks
must all be repeated:

\begin{enumerate}
\item verify the latest primary version and any erratum;
\item compare the base field, characteristic, and singularities of the
  space;
\item compare positivity: ample, nef, big, pseudoeffective, or Kähler;
\item compare regularity: model, smooth, continuous, bounded, or
  finite-energy;
\item determine whether the proof is sequential or valid for arbitrary nets;
\item audit every normalization of valuation, curvature, mass, and energy;
\item check whether the conclusion is equality, a one-way map, a
  correspondence, or an analogy;
\item confirm that zero-volume and infinite-value cases are defined; and
\item record whether the result is published, a dated preprint, a supplied
  manuscript, or a new proof in the book.
\end{enumerate}

This protocol is intentionally conservative.  The subject moves quickly,
but the logical dependencies of a textbook must remain stable even when the
literature changes.
