admissibility is not itself a nonlinear weak-measure construction.

The sufficiency argument is (3.2) and Remark~3.2, actual~8--9.
It uses the whole segment of Hessians and its elliptic
linearization. Its Proposition~3.1, actual~7, states the
Newton-divergence identity for any smooth symmetric matrix
field, but that wording omits the derivative symmetry. In the
acquired \cite{GuanCIMECourseNotes}, Lemma~2.1 on physical
and note pages~5--6 requires a Codazzi tensor. All six initial
pages of this forty-two-page course-hosted layout were read
and displayed, including that proof. It was compiled from
2012 CIME lectures and is not identified with the pagination
of the 2014 published chapter. Euclidean Hessians satisfy the
required Codazzi identity. The matrix-field calculation in
\Cref{sec:appH-Newton-segment} explains why the assumption
cannot be omitted. The complex analogue uses the holomorphic
and antiholomorphic Newton identities in the recent source's
Remark~3.3. The book states the real flux formula only for
smooth pairs with regular comparison boundary, avoiding an
unsupported Sard reduction for arbitrary C2 functions.

Theorem~1.2 and Corollary~1.3, actual~3, omit an additive
constant without imposing a normalization. Both integrals
change equally when that constant is added; the necessity
proof itself ends with an affine function, actual~9.
Proposition~1.4 additionally asserts equality with a G\"arding
cone without requiring maximality. Restriction to a proper
elliptic subcone preserves comparison. These literal range
issues are not a refutation of an appropriately normalized,
branch-qualified classification.

Remark~2.9 says only that F is differentiable, whereas the
pointwise expansion (3.7) differentiates its invariant
factor three times. The acquired
\cite{Ball84SymmetricPublished} is an author-hosted twenty-nine-image
scan. Its first ten physical pages, printed~699--708, were
read as OCR checked against the controlling images; the
nineteen remaining images were not reviewed. Theorem~3.2
and Corollary~3.3 on printed~705 distinguish derivative loss
at multiple eigenvalues from smooth factorization. The
simple-spectrum coordinate discussion on printed~706
preserves the given regularity; it creates no missing derivatives.
No full Ball proof review or C1 classification repair is claimed.

Two displayed real-jet formulas also require correction.
The quadratic in (3.8), actual~8, has Hessian 2D, rather
than D. For its real quartic, diagonal fourth derivatives
have coefficient three and off-diagonal ones coefficient
one. The printed (3.9), actual~9, uses ratio two. An
explicit coefficient comparison is needed before inferring
the vanishing in (3.10). The complex quartic in Remark~3.3
has diagonal ratio two and cannot inherit the real correction.
The simple-spectrum Jacobian in Lemma~3.4, actual~9--10,
has the expected Vandermonde invertibility; finite symbolic
checks do not settle the missing global regularity argument.

The example selection is exact. Example~4.1, actual~10,
gives the convex positive component of
\(\sigma_3-\sigma_1\) in complex dimension four.
Example~4.2 on that page compares quadratics on two
different real elliptic components; it does not contradict
the convex connected-branch sufficiency. Example~4.3,
actual~11, has a star-shaped nonconvex locus for
\(\sigma_3+\sigma_1\). Both later examples occur in this v1.
Its product-positivity notation (2.3), actual~5--6, must be
restricted to the positive-sum branch, rather than the
entire set on which a product happens to be positive.

The product-operator obstruction does not rely on the
recent classification. We read the entire published
\cite{Dinew22SadullaevPublished}: sixteen physical PDF
pages including one publisher cover and the research pages
995--1009. Its smooth p-plane positivity convention is
on printed~996. Theorem~4.1 is on printed~1004--1008;
Claims~4.3--4.4 supply the large-diagonal perturbation
and radial-family bounds on the exponent. The proof
outline in \Cref{thm:appH-Dinew-product-comparison}
retains both bounds. Remark~4.5's radial-only statement
does not have universal-pair scope. The viscosity approach
mentioned on printed~998 is not excluded by failure of
unweighted integral comparison.

Two narrow published typos are avoided: the comparison
display on printed~1004 repeats v on both sides, and the
invariant range on printed~1000 is written only through m,
despite the same paragraph's
\(\mathcal M_2=\sigma_1\sigma_2-\sigma_3\) in dimension three.
The book uses all n invariants and the comparison orientation
fixed by the source's question and proof. Its unrelated
A/B-classification argument is not imported here.

The primary-source checkpoint totals forty-four physical
pages in twenty-two displayed sheets and twelve elementary
formula/scope diagnostics. This integration reuses that
frozen evidence and adds no new primary-page reading,
independent model call or production receipt. The smooth
coordinate/form identity
\eqref{eq:appH-coordinate-Hessian-form} uses the book's
\(\ddc=\rmi\partial\bar\partial/(2\pi)\) and retains
its binomial and volume factors. Constant and mixed-degree
operators remain distinct from raw quotients. No canonical
problem status, general quotient weak measure, arbitrary
Hermitian integral principle or BJ22 homogeneous Siu
division conclusion follows from this addition.

\section{Positive entropy in hyper-K\"ahler families and the relative-map boundary}
\label{sec:provenance-HK-group-entropy}

\Cref{sec:hybrid-HK-dense-entropy} records the dense existence
statement of \cite[Theorem~1.1]{DenisiEtAl26EntropyV1}, exact
arXiv:2610.05173v1, submitted 4 October 2026, 12:31:16 UTC.
All eight physical pages were read as complete text and displayed.
The H2-level entropy convention is on actual~1; Corollary~1.3
is on actual~2 and its local-disk argument on actual~6--7.
The full proof is not independently certified by this integration.
The statement is attributed with polarization, projectivity,
non-isotriviality and $b_2\geq6$ retained. Its maps need not
form a chosen relative automorphism. Remark~1.5's additional
Zariski-dense generic-orbit conclusion uses a separate external
result and is not imported as Euclidean orbit density or
equidistribution.

The entire twenty-page \cite{DenisiEtAl26GroupsV1} and
fourteen-page \cite{AmerikSoldatenkovVerbitsky26V1} were also
read and displayed in their exact v1 layouts. The former's
Theorem~1.1 and proof on actual~10--13 retain a finite
exceptional set. A very general positive-entropy element may
be replaced by a power in its finite-index common subgroup;
the element itself need not belong to that subgroup. Example~1.4(1)
on actual~3 removes finite exceptions from its fourfold family.
The later entropy preprint's Example~1.6 says all members have
positive-entropy maps. That wording requires an already restricted
family or a further argument for the excluded parameters; the
cited density theorem alone does not remove them.

For the cone input, six selected pages, actual~22--27, of
\cite{Markman11SurveyV3} were read. Theorem~6.18 and
Lemma~6.23 distinguish the birational monodromy subgroup from
its image in $O(\operatorname{NS})$. If
$\rho:\operatorname{Mon}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}\to
O(\operatorname{NS})$ and $\Gamma=\operatorname{im}\rho$,
then $\Gamma$ has finite index in $O^+(\operatorname{NS})$,
the restriction kernel belongs to the birational subgroup,
and the chamber stabilizer is its image inside $\Gamma$.
It must not be identified without qualification with the full
ambient orthogonal-group stabilizer, as in the entropy preprint's
actual~3 wording. Finite kernels and finite indices remain
part of the passage from geometric groups to lattice actions.

The first ten of thirteen pages of \cite{Oguiso05EntropyV5}
were read, including Theorem~2.1 and its complete displayed
proof on actual~4--8. That theorem assumes an infinite group
all of whose elements have spectral radius one, and gives a
fixed primitive isotropic direction and virtual free abelian
rank at most $r-2$. The earlier Theorem~1.3 omits the infinite
qualifier from its rank bound; a trivial group at Picard rank
one cannot have rank at most $-1$. The infinite-group input
used here retains its own hypothesis.

Eight selected pages of \cite{Nikulin95ReflectionV6} include
Definition~1.1.2 and Theorem~1.1.3 on actual~5--7. Its
parabolic reflective lattice finiteness is modulo similarity
and isomorphism. Passing to finitely many actual Picard
lattices also uses the fixed deformation type's negative
square bound, as in the recent sources' proofs. The full
Picard lattice contains the positive polarization; its
orthogonal negative part is a different lattice. The entropy
preprint's actual~4 and ASV's actual~11 wording must retain
this distinction. Huybrechts' Remark~1.1, printed~231 of
\cite{Huybrechts18PolarizedPublished}, is the specified
embedding/vector finiteness input; only physical~1--6 of that
twenty-page published PDF were read. These are not readings
of the published Nikulin or Springer Markman layouts.

ASV's strong-density proof, actual~9--12, uses a homogeneous
dynamics input from the published Amerik--Verbitsky article.
The acquired \cite{AmerikVerbitsky16AuthorOrbits} is instead
the complete thirteen-page author version~0.9 of 13 April
2016, with the earlier parabolic-orbit title. It calls the
stabilizer of a nonisotropic negative vector a parabolic
subgroup, although that stabilizer is reductive and does not
have the Borel-containing property defined in the same copy.
That older wording is not attributed to the changed-title
2020 publication. Its controlling published proof and the
measure classification and non-divergence chain remain
external gates. ASV Proposition~2.11's local tangent argument
also needs a noncritical parametrization; a nonconstant
holomorphic map need not have nonzero derivative at an
arbitrary center. Rouch\'e intersection requires a restricted
defining function that is not identically zero.

The earlier Picard-jump mechanism was read in actual~1--6
of the twelve-page \cite{Oguiso00PicardV1}, including the
Main Theorem and its local period/Rouch\'e proof. Its layout
is distinct from the combined 2003 JAG article and does
not itself give strong density for the new prescribed
lattice-divisor collection. In ASV's construction immediately
before Proposition~2.6, actual~8, mere integrality and
divisibility one are used to choose an isotropic pairing-one
partner. The odd unimodular Gram matrix
$\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\1&1\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$
shows why an evenness or additional application argument is
needed. The entropy preprint's Proposition~2.9 avoids that
demand by allowing the partner's square to be arbitrary.
Neither diagnostic supplies a counterexample to the new
density theorem or a certified replacement proof.

The frozen source audit contains eighteen scoped records
and six elementary diagnostics. Its actual reading totals
ninety-one physical pages in forty-six displayed sheets;
the integration adds no fresh primary-page audit. The
source's finite-index, positive-cone, discriminant-action
and local Hilbert-scheme inputs keep the detailed qualifications
recorded there. No canonical problem status, original result,
independent model call or production receipt is added.
In particular, \eqref{eq:hybrid-HK-dense-existence} supplies
neither the family-uniform estimate
\eqref{eq:hybrid-K3-uniformity-input} nor the mixed-product
input for \eqref{eq:hybrid-K3-conditional-limit}. It provides
no general BJ22 homogeneous Siu/envelope division assertion.

\section{Unbounded boundary obstacles and divisor Green approximation}
\label{sec:provenance-boundary-envelopes}

\Cref{sec:appH-boundary-envelopes,sec:ytd-boundary-fixed-obstacle}
use two exact first versions: Dao--Nguyen,
\cite{DaoNguyen26UnboundedDirichletV1}, arXiv:2610.00914v1,
submitted 1 October 2026, 01:46:02 UTC; and Dao--Nguyen--Tran,
\cite{DaoNguyenTran26HypersurfaceGreenV1}, arXiv:2610.00909v1,
submitted the same day, 01:39:03 UTC. Their complete returned
thirty-three- and thirty-five-page PDFs were read as full text and
displayed. These are attributed source conclusions, not independent
certifications of their complete external proof chains.

The Dirichlet paper's Theorem~1.1, actual~3 and proof~14--15,
requires extended continuity on the closed bounded domain and
ordinary real superharmonicity inside. It proves local boundedness
and maximality of the interior psh envelope, including extended
boundary limits at barrier points. The obstacle is not an arbitrary
continuous function, and its upper envelope is not interchangeable
with the Perron envelope of arbitrary boundary data. Definitions~1.2--1.3
and Theorem~1.4, actual~4--5 and proof~15--18, use maps on a fixed
closure neighborhood and real-analytic inverse trajectories. The
moving-boundary cluster uses the closed domain; the identity map
has the entire boundary as its cluster. The one-sided obstacle
condition has a finite target value, and the cluster avoids the
infinite locus with the stated barriers. No nesting or Runge
property of moving domains is assumed.

Theorem~1.4's increasing whole-boundary approximation differs from
Lemma~3.3 and Corollary~3.4's nonmonotone convergence off a
neighborhood pluripolar hull. Proposition~3.6, actual~19--20,
requires the stronger finite-truncation boundary control for
whole-domain convergence. Example~5.2, actual~28--29, prevents
replacement by bare pointwise boundary convergence. The hull
representation in Theorem~1.5, actual~5--6 and proof~21, has its
own pseudoconvex ambient domain, holomorphically convex closure
and neighborhood-barrier inputs. It concerns an \(\mathcal O(U)\)
hull. Corollary~1.8, actual~23--24, requires uniform radius
convergence on the specified closed ambient set and domain
convergence from both sides. These further statements are not
inferred merely from interior continuity.

Theorem~1.9, actual~6--7 and proof~24, keeps the same obstacle,
a global lower bound, complete neighborhood pluripolarity of
the exceptional boundary set and barriers elsewhere. Its
comparison class explains the Poisson example on actual~24--25.
The comparison mechanism quoted in Appendix H is the selected
locally bounded maximal case of
\cite[Lemma~2.6(i), actual~8--9]{DieuLongHieu25QuasiBRegularV1}.
The read edition of that source is arXiv:2509.12063v1;
only actual~1--3 and~6--9 of its twenty-three-page PDF were read.
Its quasi upper bounded approximants, upper boundary limits for
the minorant and lower boundary limits for the comparator are
retained. Its domain boundary-pluripolar witness differs from
a neighborhood pluripolar hull. Part~(ii) uses a harmonic
comparator and a different polar exceptional set. The corresponding
2026 JGA article, DOI \nolinkurl{10.1007/s12220-026-02412-1}, was identified
from publication metadata, but its publisher PDF request returned
an HTML error page. The selected proof and v1 diagnostics are
not attributed to that unread published edition.

For the Green-function paper, Definition~1.4, actual~4--5,
requires a jointly holomorphic map on a full parameter disk,
an inward image of the entire closed domain on a complex sector,
uniform displacement, quantitative depth near the boundary divisor
and nonidentically-zero escape at singular boundary divisor points.
The defining function is reduced on a neighborhood of the closure.
Theorem~1.7, actual~5 and proof~29--33, first proves canonical
truncation approximation without B-regularity. B-regularity
subsequently makes the bounded envelopes continuous Perron
solutions. The pointwise residual identity is asserted off the
divisor; its upper-regularized identity holds throughout the
domain. Continuity of the Green function at the divisor follows
from the residual's local upper bound. Residual continuity on
the whole divisor is not a further conclusion imported here.

The local proof uses a finite Weierstrass cover, moving centers
selected by Lemma~5.5's submean inequality, and relative estimates
in Lemmas~5.1--5.4. It does not presume the residual's continuity
or holomorphic variation of individual roots. The singular relative
constant is independent of truncation; regular losses on short
arcs vanish at the required rate. The target contours and Rouch\'e
margins precede the source cutoffs and root-distribution integer.
The final radial shrink carries a logarithmic error. Proposition~2.1's
convexifying maps extend across both closures; the Reinhardt and
Kohn--Nirenberg examples retain their separate geometric and
external B-regularity inputs. An absence-of-boundary-discs criterion
is not imported for arbitrary domains.

The classical divisor input was read in the published
\cite{LarussonSigurdsson99GreenPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1512/iumj.1999.48.1767}: actual~1 and~11--22, printed~1513
and~1523--1534, including the full displayed Section~3.
Proposition~3.2 gives psh divisorial subtraction, whereas
Theorem~3.3 retains its Stein ambient and subspace-extension
hypotheses. The original Theorem~3.9, printed~1532--1533,
has the missing comparison explicitly identified in the complete
\cite{LarussonSigurdsson01GreenErratum}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1512/iumj.2001.50.2279}. The returned erratum has one
actual page, printed~1705; citation metadata extending to~1706
does not create a second read page. It says the theorem remains
unproved and the other results are unaffected. It distinguishes
global upper boundedness of the residual from its local bound
near the divisor. This is a proof gap, not a supplied counterexample.
The acquired older math/9807164v1 was not substituted for the
published copy or counted as read.

The full twenty-page \cite{Nguyen05HypersurfaceGreenPublished},
DOI \nolinkurl{10.1007/BF02383618}, was read. Its Theorem~2.2, printed~184--185
and proof~189--192, and Corollary~2.9 retain homogeneous hyperplanes,
radial trace geometry, closure hull and good-barrier inputs. Its
whole-domain residual continuity and non-B-regular star-shaped
examples keep their own scope. Radial preservation of a singular
homogeneous boundary pole fails the new escape test. Neither
result's hypotheses are silently substituted for the other's.

The functional interface and root-cell argument were read in
actual~1--4 and~9--14, printed~171--174 and~179--184, of the
twenty-two-page returned published layout of
\cite{DrinovecForstneric12DiscPublished}, DOI \nolinkurl{10.4064/ap106-0-13}.
Theorem~1.1 retains the manifold or locally irreducible space scope.
The functional definitions distinguish point Lelong weights from
divisor intersection orders. Lemma~3.2 and Theorem~3.1 fix source
arcs and target points before the large root integer; the spray
construction preserves the selected target points. The new
additive divisor gluing is a distinct construction. The printed
root-phase slash formula is not reproduced; the standard spacing
is \(2\pi j/k\). The metadata of arXiv:1109.3947v3 was consulted,
but that preprint was not the read edition. General manifold
splitting and the remaining external functional chain are not
fully audited by the selected pages.

Other literal auxiliary defects remain narrowly scoped: the 2005
example \(-\log(|f|+1/j)\) is not C2 at a simple zero; its
coordinate map in Corollary~2.11 acts on the wrong coordinate;
and its peak base in Proposition~3.2 needs normalization for a
general maximum M. The quasi-B-regular v1's positive power
\((-\log|z|)^\alpha\), \(0<\alpha<1\), is not psh.
These local diagnostics do not refute either new main theorem
or certify complete replacement proofs.

The frozen source review records twenty-four scoped entries,
twelve elementary checks, and 119 actually read source/page pairs
in sixty-two displayed sheets across seven exact PDFs. Four
returned PDFs were read completely; three were read in the
specified selections. The integration reuses that evidence and
adds no fresh primary-page reading, original result, independent
model call or production receipt. Its unsquared logarithms
retain \(\ddc\log|f|=\tfrac12[\operatorname{div}(f)]\) in the
book's normalization. The open complex directions on the Green
paper's actual~33--34 do not add canonical Chapter~72 atoms.
Scalar divisorial subtraction, SID approximation and obstacle
comparison do not repair general non-Archimedean Siu division
or the BJ22 Theorem~6.21 envelope-closure boundary.

\section{Unbounded-domain Perron comparison and source qualifications}
\label{sec:provenance-unbounded-domain-comparison}

\Cref{sec:appH-unbounded-domain-comparison} distinguishes bounded
capped data on unbounded domains from the unbounded obstacle on a
bounded domain in \Cref{sec:appH-boundary-envelopes}. Its controlling
recent source is \cite{Pawlaschyk26UnboundedPerronV1}, exact
arXiv:2606.16584v1, submitted 15 June 2026, 11:30:28 UTC.
All thirteen actual PDF pages were read. Definition~2.3 and
Theorem~2.8, actual~2--3, use the Hunt--Murray eigenvalue-count
class, distinct from p-plane trace positivity. Definition~2.11
and Remark~2.12, actual~3--4, specify the dual comparison class;
the determinant-zero interpretation is restricted to smooth functions.
No general positive Bedford--Taylor measure is assigned to the
non-psh q-class.

Definition~3.1 and Proposition~3.4, actual~5--6, retain the finite cap
and distinguish continuous closure competitors from usc ones.
Definition~4.15, actual~9, requires a negative psh witness continuous
in the open domain and uniformly tending to \(-\infty\) at infinity.
An enlarged primary view corrected an initial reading of \(C(D)\)
as \(C(\overline D)\); the controlling definition has no closure bar.
Proposition~4.17's printed continuous closure extension is not assumed
by that definition. Its bounded maximum argument can instead use the
witness's nonpositive boundary upper limits.

Theorem~4.19(1)--(4) and Remark~4.20, actual~10--12, supply the
attributed bounded existence and capped-envelope identification with
\(0\leq r\leq q\leq n-r-1\), r-peaks, bounded nonnegative f and
the stated infinity witness. Additive shifts treat bounded real data.
The source constructs a decreasing bounded-domain sequence, controls
the finite boundary with an extension, and uses translated capped
competitors to prove interior continuity. Classical q-Dirichlet,
semiconvex approximation and local comparison remain imported inputs;
reading the displayed argument does not certify their complete chain.

Theorem~4.19(5) and Claim~5, actual~10 and~12, literally omit growth
bounds on the competing q-Bremermann function. The complete elementary
exterior-disk check is \eqref{eq:appH-unbounded-exterior-family}:
all hypotheses hold for n=1,r=q=0, yet every \(c\log|z|\) has zero
finite-boundary trace. The positive multiple violates the first use
of a bounded-above maximum principle; the negative multiple violates
the asserted outer comparison with \(\varepsilon\Psi\). Thus that
uncapped uniqueness clause is false as written. The bounded uniqueness
in the book is the direct consequence of the attributed addition and
infinity maximum principles, not an import of this defective clause.

Other literal recent-source claims are kept separate. Example~2.13,
actual~4, does not justify an arbitrary holomorphic pullback of a
maximal log-norm potential: \(F(z)=(1,z)\) in \(\mathbf C\) has
\(\log\|F\|=\tfrac12\log(1+|z|^2)\) with positive Levi form.
The polynomial example in~4.16, actual~9, needs positive degree;
a constant polynomial would satisfy its inequality on every domain.
Remark~4.21, actual~12, literally takes the relative q-hulls of
\(\overline{D\cap B_r(0)}\) inside \(\overline D\). Since every compact
lies in its displayed hull, their union already equals \(\overline D\),
contrary to the claimed proper inclusion. On the exterior disk the
uncapped zero-data envelope is infinite in D. An intended hull of
boundary subsets is not silently substituted from the unread older source.

The entire returned published \cite{NguyenHung08UnboundedPublished},
DOI \nolinkurl{10.5802/aif.2388}, was read: twenty-five actual pages,
including a publisher wrapper and printed~1383--1406.
Definition~2.2, printed~1386, uses bounded maximal solutions for
bounded boundary data; Definition~2.6, printed~1389, uses a finite
negative psh infinity witness without requiring continuity.
Theorem~4.4, printed~1398--1400, expressly restricts uniqueness
to bounded solutions and retains isotopy, moving-boundary peak/Jensen
and real-regularity hypotheses for its existence statement.

Its full Jensen chain is not certified. The arbitrary-cone
Theorem~3.1, printed~1390--1392, lacks the minorant/negative-constant
condition making every envelope finite. On a one-point space,
the cone \(\{0\}\) and obstacle \(-1\) give an empty supremum,
whereas the subprobability infimum is \(-1\). The compact-support
cone \(A_1\) does not contain negative constants. Proposition~3.4's
negative obstacle lies outside Proposition~3.3's displayed nonnegative
signature. The real \(2n\)-dimensional mollifier printed on~1398
has factor \(\delta^{-n}\), rather than mass-one \(\delta^{-2n}\);
the following fixed-point inequality also drops a negative witness
term without first scaling it. Theorem~2.1(c)'s missing negative
exhaustion condition, the continuity step in Lemma~3.2 and the
nonproper transfer's infinity control retain separate audit gates.
These auxiliary diagnoses do not certify a replacement proof or
disprove the scoped bounded nonnegative existence assertion.

The published \cite{Slodkowski84BremermannPublished} has twenty-five
returned pages including its wrapper; actual~1--5 and~16--22,
printed~303--306 and~317--323, were read. Theorem~5.1 and
Lemma~4.2 supply the q-index addition interface. Theorem~5.3 and
Lemma~5.4, printed~320, require bounded open domains.
Theorem~5.6, printed~321, omits the lower bound r\(\leq\)q in its
parameter range. On the \(\mathbf C^3\) shell \(1<|z|<2\),
a defining function \(\rho+\rho^2\), with
\(\rho=(|z|^2-1)(|z|^2-4)\), is strictly 2-psh near both boundaries.
The literal range admits q=0. Inner value one and outer value zero
cannot extend continuously as psh: restriction to
\((1,\zeta,0)\) gives a bounded subharmonic function on
\(0<|\zeta|<\sqrt3\), extending to one at the removable center and
zero on the outer circle, contrary to the maximum principle.
The full modern range is retained instead. The selected minimum
proof also prints a maximum in its auxiliary class; its unread
approximation foundations are not declared verified.

The frozen source review records fifty actual source/page pairs on
twenty-six displayed sheets, two repeated details, twenty scoped
records and fifteen elementary checks. The integration reuses that
reading; it adds no fresh primary-page audit, original result,
independent model call or production receipt. Neither bounded
q-comparison nor this local infinity witness supplies general
non-Archimedean Siu division or repairs the BJ22 Theorem~6.21
envelope-closure boundary. Canonical problem statuses are unchanged.

\section{Conifold contractions, mass bounds and metric completion}
\label{sec:provenance-conifold-contractions}

\Cref{sec:hybrid-conifold-contractions} imports
\cite{GuyettMcGowan26ConifoldV1}, exact arXiv:2610.00942v1,
submitted 1 October 2026, 02:19:41 UTC. All twenty-eight actual
PDF pages were read. Theorem~1.1 permits non-closed Hermitian
backgrounds and gives bounded normalized potentials, compact-away
smooth estimates, a positive scalar limit independent of the
Hermitian perturbation, and subsequential potential limits.
Corollary~1.1 adds the closed K\"ahler background: the scalar becomes
the cohomological volume, the potential descends to normal $Y$, and
bounded-potential uniqueness gives convergence of the full family.
Theorem~1.2 additionally requires dimension three, ordinary double
points and a perturbation K\"ahler near the exceptional curves.
The resulting Gromov--Hausdorff conclusion retains these hypotheses.

The smooth positive-parameter existence theorem of Tosatti--Weinkove,
Collins--Tosatti's closed nef/big null-locus theorem, EGZ bounded
singular K\"ahler uniqueness and the full regularity/descent chains
remain external inputs. The selected primary dependencies below
verify their stated interfaces; they do not certify every underlying
proof. No original theorem or independent verification receipt is
claimed by this integration.

The published \cite{GuedjLu22VolumesPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.14231/AG-2022-021}, has twenty-six actual pages,
printed~688--713. Actual~1--4 and~10--17 were read.
Proposition~3.2, printed~700, gives monotonicity of lower and upper
mass for ordered semipositive forms. Corollary~3.8, printed~704,
gives positive lower and finite upper Hermitian mass on Fujiki
manifolds. Lower positivity for the degenerate pullback in the new
source is separately obtained by comparison with a closed big form.
Positive background volume alone is insufficient for an arbitrary
non-closed semipositive form, as Example~3.5, printed~702, shows.
The literal proper-map statement in Lemma~3.6 is used here only for
degree-one modifications, where its mass change of variables applies.

\cite{GuedjLu21Envelopes3V1} is exact arXiv:2107.01938v1, submitted
5 July 2021, 11:12:56 UTC. Its thirty-four-page layout is distinct
from the unavailable 2023 Crelle edition. The cumulative reading
covers actual~1--13, 17--18 and~26--34: twenty-four pages, including
fifteen previously read pages and nine fresh ones. Theorem~A on
actual~2 requires positive lower mass, probability measure, a
normalized psh $L^m$ bound with $m>n$, and control of the ratio
of the equation constant to lower mass. Theorem~B separates
uniqueness of the untwisted scalar from uniqueness for the
positive-exponential equation. Theorem~4.2's local trace proof,
actual~26--30, is read with its singular barrier and torsion terms.
Its original unbounded-product warning is distinguished from the
later published product construction below. The v1 condition~(B)
has a bidegree typo in its upper bound; the correct squared-form
bound is retained from GL2, not copied from that literal line.

The published \cite{BoucksomGuedjLu25VolumesPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1007/s42543-025-00105-2}, appeared online on
16 June 2025. The returned PDF has forty-three pages; actual~1--5,
13--18 and~35--41 were read, eighteen pages. Theorem~2.2 extends
non-pluripolar products to quasi-closed, quasi-positive currents
under bounded mass. Theorem~4.7 and the introduction explicitly
distinguish a unique normalizing scalar from the largely open
untwisted potential uniqueness problem. The final continuation of
that proof on actual~42 and the full volume-continuity argument

and higher ranks. Proposition~3.2, actual~12--13, realizes a prescribed
strictly positive curvature map near a point by a global metric,
equal to the direct-sum Fubini--Study metric outside an arbitrary
prescribed neighborhood. These are statements about a smooth
metric-dependent representative, not a failure of numerical
ample-bundle positivity.

omits $1/\ell$; its closed kernel and subsequent Einstein

\section{Globally bounded minorants and pointwise Perron envelopes}
\label{sec:provenance-bounded-minorants}

\Cref{sec:appH-bounded-minorants} explains attributed results from
\cite{Wikstrom26BoundedMinorantsV2}, exact arXiv:2607.24139v2,
revised 29 September 2026, 12:05:49 UTC, nine pages, titled
\emph{Perron envelopes with globally bounded minorants}.
All actual~1--9 were read as complete selected text with matching images.
The eight-page v1 of 27 July 2026, 08:19:08 UTC, titled
\emph{Restricted Perron envelopes and quasibounded functions}, was also
read completely for comparison; it does not control the revised formulas.

Theorem~1.1, actual~1 and proof~3--4, says the restricted SH envelope
equals its own upper regularization for finite-above usc obstacles.
It does not recover every obstacle. The version allowing an obstacle
value $+\infty$ retains a separate subharmonic-regularization assumption.
Each competitor has its own global upper bound; the two-sided version
has its own possibly empty family. The revised proof uses Poisson
differences and max-pasting rather than v1's Brelot-resolutivity input.
The incomplete sentence after v1~(2.5) is a text defect, not a new
counterexample. Ordinary harmonic corrections are not automatically
pluriharmonic in higher complex dimension.

Theorem~1.2, actual~2 and proof~5--6, and Remark~3.6, actual~8--9,
give the B-regular Hartogs example in every complex dimension $n\geq2$.
The obstacle is finite and positive pluriharmonic in the interior, with
boundary unboundedness. Its raw envelope is zero on the analytic axis
and equal to the obstacle off that axis; its regularization is the
continuous obstacle. The domain's cuspidal boundary is not globally
smooth. Conjugation in the boundary peak is image-controlled.
The higher-dimensional proof uses the tangential Levi form, not a
product with extra discs. Remark~3.3 permits an infinite majorant
on the axis; Remark~3.5 distinguishes raw-envelope discontinuity from
a discontinuous tame Dirichlet solution.

Proposition~3.4(a), actual~7--8, gives recovery wherever a strong
majorant is finite; this sufficiency already occurs in v1.
The converse in~(b), actual~8, is added in v2 and requires a finite
pluriharmonic obstacle. Its summable nonnegative deficits are
plurisuperharmonic because of that hypothesis. No converse for an
arbitrary psh obstacle is imported. Proposition~3.2, actual~6--7,
gives the one-sided boundary Jensen estimate; it supplies neither a
general equality nor a limit/infimum interchange.

\cite{Nilsson22EnvelopesPublished} controls the thirteen-page published
Math.~Z.~301 (2022), 3959--3971 layout, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1007/s00209-022-03043-2}. Actual~1--4 were read.
Definition~1, Lemma~2.1 and Proposition~2.2 occur on actual~3,
printed~3961, with the proposition's proof ending actual~4/3962.
The separately read arXiv:2109.13625v1 author file, actual~1--4 of
twelve pages, instead has Definition~2.1 and Lemma~2.2/Proposition~2.3.
Wikstr\"om's locators use the preprint numbering alongside the journal
citation. The book uses the published numbers when citing the
published entry. This edition mismatch does not refute the majorant
equivalence or assert continuity on the majorant's pole set.

\cite{NilssonWikstrom20QuasiboundedV1} is exact arXiv:2005.09505v1,
19 May 2020; actual~1--6 of thirteen pages were read.
Example~1.2 on actual~1 already gives the disk/ball-axis Poisson
obstruction. The later Hartogs geometry adds tameness and exact
off-axis recovery. Theorem~3.1, actual~4, only guarantees bounded
approximation quasi-everywhere. Its adjacent question display omits
the obstacle condition; that omitted inequality is retained in the book.
Theorem~3.2, actual~4--5, gives the superlinear-transform criterion
used here. The related 2021 journal layout does not control these locators.

Theorem~4.1 of that preprint, actual~6, prints the direction
$I^F g\leq S_F g$ for an arbitrary Borel obstacle. Jensen's definition
gives the opposite direction. On $[0,1]$, the cone of continuous
functions and an obstacle equal to one at zero and zero elsewhere
give $I^F g(0)=1$ but $S_F g(0)=0$. This diagnoses the literal
inequality direction, not the lower-semicontinuous equality clause
or a counterexample to a corrected Dirichlet theorem.

\cite{Wikstrom01JensenPublished} is the published twenty-page
Ark.~Mat.~39 (2001), 181--200 file. Actual~1--3 and~5--12 were read.
Theorem~2.1, actual~3/printed~183, has the correct general-Borel
direction. Definition~3.7 and Corollary~3.8, actual~8/188, give the
B-regular boundary-extension and Dirac-Jensen criterion. The explicit
Hartogs exhaustion and continuous boundary peaks in
\eqref{eq:appH-Hartogs-peaks} verify its geometric hypotheses.
Theorem~4.1, actual~9--11, and Corollary~4.3, actual~12/192, give
the equality of bounded-above and continuous-closure Jensen classes.
Original smooth-exhaustion, Walsh extension and standard peak inputs
retain their external scope. The original Sibony DOI returned anti-bot
HTML and the Ransford chapter endpoint returned non-PDF content;
neither original file is credited as read.

\cite{Nilsson25QuasiboundedSolutionsV1} is exact arXiv:2505.09301v1,
14 May 2025, seventeen pages. Actual~1--3 and~7--12 were read.
Definition~3.1, actual~7, uses absolute-value domination by a positive,
pole-allowing plurisuperharmonic majorant. The positive Hartogs obstacle
satisfies it directly. The 2026 Wiley PDF attempt returned HTTP~403;
the published definition's numbering is not authenticated by that attempt.
The preprint's Theorem~3.7 needs compliant measures, a Cegrell
$\mathcal E$ minorant, and the stated boundary regularity outside a
b-pluripolar set, with comparison and balayage inputs.

Its selected proof text has three literal issues: a positive lower bound
for negative approximants in Lemma~3.3 on actual~9, $u$ in place of
$u'$ in a comparison conclusion on actual~11, and $\max(v,-j)$
for strictly positive $v$ in a purported negative-psh truncation there.
The last expression equals $v$ for every $j$ and does not justify that
$\mathcal E$-class approximation step. These source-local defects
are retained without declaring the theorem statement false or attributing
them to the unread 2026 published proof. That general Dirichlet chain
and its preprint-local minorant conjecture are not imported here.

The reused frozen source review comprises fifty-one source/page pairs
on twenty-seven individually displayed sheets: two complete main
versions and five partial dependency files. Twenty-six scoped records
and twenty-four elementary algebra/sign/quantifier diagnostics preserve
the hypotheses and edition boundaries. This integration adds no fresh
primary-page reading, original result, independent model call or

\section{Positive pluriharmonic currents and compact Siu analyticity}
\label{sec:provenance-pluriharmonic-Siu}

\Cref{sec:appH-pluriharmonic-Siu} uses the exact thirteen-page
arXiv:2606.29680v2, \cite{DinhNguyenNguyen26SiuV2}, revised
1 October 2026, 23:18:16 UTC. Its title is \emph{Siu's analyticity
theorem for positive pluriharmonic currents}, and the authors are
Tien-Cuong Dinh, Duc-Bao Nguyen and Viet-Anh Nguyen. All actual~1--13
were read with complete selected text and matching images. The forty-page
v1, \cite{DinhNguyen26SiuV1}, submitted 29 June 2026, 01:05:21 UTC,
was also read completely. It has two authors and a projective,
bidimension-one main theorem. The added author and the compact-K\"ahler,
arbitrary-bidimension scope belong to v2. Dates printed inside the PDFs
are not their arXiv submission timestamps.

Theorem~1.2, actual~2, proof~11--12, controls
\Cref{thm:appH-pluriharmonic-Siu}. Its Theorem~2.1 supplies analytic
restriction and fixed component coefficients. Lemmas~3.1--3.2 produce
a closed representative dominating the whole Lelong function, through
finite point blow-ups and weak compactness in a fixed class. This is
not a positive current inequality between the two representatives.
The $\ddc$ lemma identifies their cohomology classes without making
the original current closed. The source uses $2\ddc$ relative to the
book's operator; its $2^{-q}$ Lelong factor gives the same plane-one
convention in \eqref{eq:appH-PH-Lelong}.

The induction resolves an offending analytic hull component and takes
a tangent along its smooth exceptional divisor. Lemma~4.6 supplies
the divisor current and the Lelong inequality. Formula
\eqref{eq:appH-PH-full-fibre-locus} makes the required analytic set on
the base explicit: every point of each relevant projective fibre is
in the threshold set. A proper analytic subset of a projective bundle
may surject onto its base; the full-fibre locus, whose fibre dimension
is maximal, is proper and analytic. Analytic resolution, fibre-dimension
and the older support/extension inputs retain their external hypotheses.
This proof explanation is not a new recursive proof-chain certificate.

\cite{AlessandriniBassanelli03Transforms} controls the official
twenty-five-page Osaka J.~Math.~40 (2003), 717--740 file, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.18910/5464}. Actual~1--14 were read. Its cover changes
the physical/printed-page offset: physical~12--13 are printed~727--728.
The positive pluriharmonic total transform is Theorem~3.11 there,
not a theorem numbered~2; its Aeppli-class characterization uses
Theorem~3.9. General exceptional coefficients are weakly psh functions,
and the strict transform is plurisuperharmonic. Compactness forces
the coefficients constant and makes the strict transform pluriharmonic.
The separate psh strict-mass statement is Proposition~3.13 on
physical~13/printed~728. The original older existence input cited in
Theorem~3.11 was not independently read here.

\cite{Vigny19LelongSkodaV2} is the exact fifteen-page
arXiv:0711.3782v2, 17 December 2019, 10:20:20 UTC; all pages were
read. The compact pluriharmonic transform is Theorem~5.1, actual~12,
whereas Theorem~3.1 is closed-current theory. Preprint Lemma~5.2 on
actual~13 is an integration-by-parts identity. It does not authenticate
the point-pullback lemma numbered~5.2 in the journal citation used by
the controlling main paper. The journal PDF attempt returned non-PDF
content; neither a clearance nor a disproof of that journal locator
is claimed. Preprint Lemma~2.1 and Section~5 give the related
exceptional-mass content with their own hypotheses.

The official thirteen-page Numdam PDF of \cite{DS04} was read entirely.
Theorem~4.1 and Corollary~4.2, printed~965--966, provide bounded-mass
\emph{differences} of positive smooth pluriharmonic approximants and
the compact pluriharmonic strict-transform input. Theorem~5.1,
printed~967--969, treats a closed positive $(1,1)$-factor with continuous
potential wedged with a positive pluriharmonic factor. Its formula~(12)
retains cross derivatives for a nonclosed second factor. General local
pluriharmonic wedge products are not supplied by that theorem.
The ancillary Proposition~5.5 proof has an omega-degree mismatch and
is not imported uncorrected. Older extension/support theorems cited
by DS04 remain external.

\cite{FuXiao14ConesV2} is exact arXiv:1203.2978v2,
21 August 2014, 14:33:51 UTC, twenty-one pages. Actual~1, 11--12
and~16--21 were read. Lemma~3.3 and Remark~3.4, actual~11--12,
control the balanced/pluriharmonic duality. The lemma requires strict
pairing on every nonzero positive pluriharmonic current; the dual cone
uses the closure of the balanced cone. Appendix Theorem~5.2 on
actual~18--19 is not the related journal locator A.2. Cones generated
by modification pushforwards and their closures are distinguished.

All fourteen pages of \cite{Tosatti26MorseV2}, exact
arXiv:2608.30153v2, 8 September 2026, 11:47:48 UTC, were read.
Theorem~1.1 controls \eqref{eq:appH-Tosatti-Morse}; Corollaries~1.2
and~1.4 give the closed cone comparison used for Lelong domination.
Volume differentiability is inside the big cone, not asserted at all
boundary points. Proposition~2.1's transition set is Lebesgue-null;
this does not make it pluripolar. Its concentrated approximants and
mass comparisons do not make an arbitrary difference of positive
currents positive. The older smooth potential approximation input
retains its external scope.

The author-hosted journal PDF of \cite{Bou02b} was read on actual~1
and~5--7 of twenty-one pages. Proposition~2.1 is actual~6/printed~1048.
The literal pointwise limsup assertion fails by the flat-elliptic-curve
example in \Cref{sec:appH-PH-cone-boundary}; convolution and Fatou
only yield a liminf, and point-dependent subsequences do not repair
that assertion. The common fixed determinant lower bound needed in
Tosatti's Proposition~2.3 survives by the direct concavity/convolution
argument displayed there. The ancillary false stronger assertion is
not a counterexample to the Morse inequality. The rest of the volume
paper's proof chain was not read in this audit.

\cite{Nguyen22TangentsV2} controls the 241-page
arXiv:2111.11024v2, 30 May 2022, 22:35:51 UTC. The actual selected
pages were~1, 9--18, 23--25, 190--194 and~226--239, thirty-three
pages total. Theorem~1.18 and Corollary~1.21 on~16--17 explicitly
distinguish pluriharmonic tangent theory from the closed case.
The local hypotheses include boundary regularity and differences
of positive approximants; the compact boundaryless specialization
removes those boundary requirements. The compact regularization
input is independently anchored in the read DS04 theorem.

This preprint's local Appendix~B estimates remain uncleared. A kernel
bound in the integration variable alone does not give the stated
uniform measure-to-$L^p$ estimate; a difference kernel contains a
diagonal delta, and an approximate identity minus the identity does
not send every discontinuous bounded function into a Lipschitz
function. Its final bootstrap still invokes those estimates. These
literal local issues do not refute the separately supported compact
pluriharmonic difference approximation.

\cite{Nguyen25IntersectionV1} is exact arXiv:2501.02150v1,
4 January 2025, 00:46:43 UTC, seventy-eight pages. Actual~1, 9--19
and~45--58 were read, twenty-six pages. Theorem~2.14(2), actual~13--14,
explicitly states compact pluriharmonic tangent class independence;
the closed-current density intersection theorems have separate
integrability hypotheses. Section~10 needs the infinity inclusion
$\mathbf P(E)\hookrightarrow\mathbf P(E\oplus\mathbf C)$ and the
separate zero-section term. Its literal bundle inclusion and fibre-basis
dimensions are not copied. The numerical formulas in Theorems~8.4--8.5
on~45--47 have a radial normalization mismatch: under their stated
$\ddc=\rmi\partial\bar\partial/\pi$, the normalized disk integral
of $\ddc|t|^2$ is~2 rather than~1. Qualitative class independence
does not clear those exact coefficient formulas.

Both Nguyen preprints have a signed quasi-positive boundary-convergence
assertion requiring an additional domination condition. For
$R_j=\delta_{1/j}-\delta_{-1/j}$, the weak limit is zero and has no
boundary mass on $(0,1)$, but $R_j((0,1))=1$ for $j>1$.
The positive dominating limits charge its boundary. Avoiding their
boundary mass repairs this particular convergence use; avoiding only
the signed limit's mass does not. Compact boundaryless statements
are separate from this local defect.

\cite{DinhSibony14DensityV2} controls exact
arXiv:1203.5810v2, forty-nine pages; actual~1, 25--29 and~43--44
were read, eight pages. Proposition~3.10 and its neighboring lemmas
give the original \emph{closed} conic-current argument. Its whole-fibre
and zero-section separation cannot be transferred to nonclosed currents
without the corresponding mass/support inputs. Lemma~5.5 on~43--44
treats a measure paired with a closed current, as needed in the main
paper's mixed-tensor application.

\cite{DinhSibony17InvariantCurveV2} is exact
arXiv:1509.07711v2, twenty-eight pages; actual~1 and~12--14 were read.
Lemma~3.7 on~13 proves the vertical positive pluriharmonic pullback
statement for the whole normal line bundle. Positive harmonic functions
on all of $\mathbf C$ are constant; a proper fibre domain does not
have the same conclusion. The source's surface dimension is retained.

\cite{DinhNguyenSibony19SurfaceV2} is exact
arXiv:1811.07450v2, fifty pages; actual~1--10, 22--26 and~38--42
were read, twenty pages. Theorem~2.2 and Lemma~B.2 retain the L2
energy terms for nonclosed factors. Its Appendix~A combines coefficient
bounds with shrinking diagonal support, not weak convergence alone.
The literal positive-psh Liouville phrase in Lemma~3.15 on~24 is false
for $|z|^2$; the positive pluriharmonic statement needed in the
whole-fibre argument is valid. Hyperbolic-foliation conclusions retain
their separate foliation assumptions.

The general movable-class application in the controlling main paper's
Lemma~4.7 and Theorem~1.5 imports Nguyen 2026 Theorem~1.9 for the
density mass/cup-product identity. No authentic PDF was found for that
listed to-appear work. This input is not used in the main analyticity
induction. Chiose--Toma's original surface approximation theorem and
several older support/resolution inputs were also not read here.
An unavailable source is not certified by a favorable model opinion.

The older forty-page main version retains diagnostic threshold, dimension,
tensor-bidegree, shrinking-annulus, lifting and logarithmic-kernel defects
in the frozen source review. They are not counterexamples to the revised
main theorem and are not used as its proof. The reused audit comprises
213 distinct source/page pairs on 111 displayed sheets, five complete
PDFs and eight partial readings, with thirty-one scoped records and
fifteen elementary diagnostics. This integration adds no fresh primary
reading, original result, independent model call or production receipt.
Canonical statuses and the BJ22 Theorem~6.21 critical division and
general envelope-closure boundaries remain unchanged.

\section{Affine toric analytic multiplier modules and Newton thresholds}
\label{sec:provenance-toric-analytic-multiplier}

\Cref{sec:appH-toric-multiplier-pairs} imports the affine-pair
criterion of \cite{Seo26ToricMultiplierV1}, exact
arXiv:2610.08741v1, submitted 6 October 2026 at 17:40:00 UTC.
All thirty-three physical pages were read with matching images.
Theorem~6.1 is on actual~28--29; the $A_1$ calculation is
Example~6.2 on~29--30. The canonical shift is defined by
$q(K_X+D)=\operatorname{div}(\chi^{-q u_D})$, in the actual
character lattice. The source's half-log weight is half the
squared-norm potential used in Chapter~51.

The proof route retains coherence before global Fourier projection,
finite jet quotients before Krull intersection, compact fibre covers
before shrinking the base, and compact transverse annuli for a
non-full cone. Its literal equality between an open logarithmic
neighbourhood and a closed translated cone is false, already for a
disk. \eqref{eq:appH-toric-cofinal-tails} gives the cofinal local
comparison. This source qualification is not an original theorem
or an independently certified repair. It is not a counterexample
to the character criterion. Negative boundary coefficients permit
fractional modules; effective boundaries give holomorphic ideals.
Negative ideal weights retain positive rational $c$ and a fixed
boundary in the strong-openness perturbation.

The smooth antecedent \cite{Guenancia11ToricArchivedV2} is exact
arXiv:1011.3162v2, 12 May 2011, twenty-four pages; actual~3--11
were read. Proposition~1.9 on~5--6 gives the convex exponential
test, and Theorem~1.13 on~7--8 gives the smooth character criterion
with its Parseval argument. This file is distinct from the later
\cite{Guenancia12} journal edition used elsewhere in Chapter~51.
The v2 finite-generation proof on~7 selects inequalities between
adjacent terms of a subsequence, which does not establish a decreasing
subsequence of the selected values. Standard polynomial Noetherianity
supplies the finite-generation fact. On~8, the substitution
$t=-\log r$ has a printed lower-endpoint sign error; a unit
polydisk avoids it. The valuative proof on~10 invokes isolated
singularities beyond the displayed signature. No unchecked valuative
or adjoint statement is imported from that passage.

\cite{Blickle03ToricArchivedV1} is exact arXiv:math/0310487v1,
31 October 2003, eight pages, all read. Its Theorem~1 defines
$\operatorname{div}(\chi^u)=r(K_X+\Delta)$ and $w=u/r$, but
then prints the shift $m+w$. Those conventions disagree: on
$\mathbf C$, $\Delta=0$ gives $w=-1$, while direct integration
puts $z$ in the multiplier ideal of $(z)^1$ and the printed shifted
test excludes it. The conflict is repeated in the proof on~5;
the corollary on~7 instead uses
$K_X+\Delta=-\operatorname{div}(\chi^w)$, consistent with the
positive shift. An intermediate module inequality also has the
opposite canonical-divisor sign. These are diagnostics of the
inspected archived v1. The subscription journal PDF was not obtained,
and its edition is not declared wrong by inference.

The author-distributed third-printing file of
\cite{RockafellarWets09Author} has 743 physical pages. Actual
1--2, 94--97, 99--100, 482--486 and 501--502 were read,
fifteen pages in total. The controlling convex anchors are
Theorem~3.21, printed~88/actual~96; Theorem~3.26(a),
printed~91--92/actual~99--100; Theorem~11.1,
printed~474/actual~482; Theorem~11.5,
printed~477--478/actual~485--486; and Theorem~11.23(a),
printed~493--494/actual~501--502. The two-page March 2009
second-printing errata and one-page December 2013 third-printing
errata were also read completely; they do not alter these selected
locators. The cover distinguishes the 1997 author text, second
printing 2004 and third printing 2009. These checked alternatives
do not authenticate Seo's unread 1970 Rockafellar locators, and
the selected biconjugacy argument still invokes an earlier foundational
separation result not audited recursively here.

\cite{Li22KollarArchivedV1} is exact arXiv:2201.13069v1,
31 January 2022, nine pages; actual~2--5 were read. Definition~2.2,
Remark~2.3 and Proposition~2.4 on~3 provide the checked adapted

\cite{FujinoSchwedeTakagiAuthorRevision} controls the undated
forty-six-page author-hosted file
\nolinkurl{supplements_revised2.pdf}; actual~10--13 were read.
Definition~7.1, Lemmas~7.2 and~7.5 and Theorem~7.8 retain the
effective-boundary qualification and the maximal non-lc rounding.
The small negative ideal-power description uses a locally sufficiently
small parameter. This author revision is not the forty-seven-page
journal layout, and closed maximal non-lc Newton membership is not

\cite{GuanZhou15AnnalsPublished} controls the official twelve-page
Annals~182 (2015), 605--616 file. Actual~1--2 were read;
Theorem~1.1 is printed~606/actual~2 and uses $\exp(-\phi)$.
Apply it to $2\phi$ in the half-log convention. Its whole proof
is not claimed read here. The older strong-openness conjecture

The reused source audit contains seventy-eight distinct source/page
pairs on forty-one individually displayed sheets: four complete PDFs,

Twenty scoped records and six exact diagnostic groups include two

\section{Canonical KE volumes and the plurigenera proof boundary}
\label{sec:provenance-KE-family-normalization}

\Cref{sec:appH-KE-family-normalization,sec:ch51-KE-family-normalization}
connect fixed-fibre MA normalization with an attributed family route.
The controlling new preprint is \cite{LPWZ26KEV1}, exact
arXiv:2610.07826v1, submitted 6 October 2026 at 06:20:42 UTC,
forty pages, all read with complete text and matching images.
Theorem~1.3 is actual~5; its proof is actual~33--34. The statement
uses $\beta_t^n$ with $\theta_\omega+\beta$, while proof equation
(7.8) uses $\omega_t^n$. The simultaneous reference change in
\eqref{eq:appH-KE-volume-covariance} is explanatory bookkeeping,
not a general repair of the independent-volume statement.
A constant scalar $\beta=s\omega$ is distinguished from a
nonconstant density change.

Theorem~8.1 on actual~34--35 needs fibrewise canonical
pseudoeffectivity for all positive twists and the untwisted envelope.
Section~9 supplies that reduction by an external family input in
its nonzero-section application. Its Theorem~9.1 on actual~36
states extension over the original disk but uses a metric built
after shrinking; the local/global bridge is not credited here.
The metric exponent on actual~9 also differs from the correct
$(m-1)$ exponent used on~36. These proof qualifications do not
constitute a verified counterexample to plurigenus invariance.

The exceptional-fibre argument of Lemma~7.2 on~32--33 retains
punctured parameter choices, bounded maxima, a mass sandwich
and nonpluripolar locality at finite truncation levels. Proposition~7.1's
finite-parameter comparison is distinguished from the limiting
inequality established before it. The operative inner limits take
the density regularization first and the K\"ahler regularization
second. Neither is the outer canonical-twist limit. A displayed
Green-function conjugate on~26 was confirmed by a matching enlarged
image; an apparent extraction error is not attributed to the source.
Its mixed $\ddc$ and fibre-cycle conventions are not copied.

The fixed-fibre existence anchor is
\cite[Theorem~6.1, actual pp.~47--48]{BEGZ10BigV2}, the exact
fifty-four-page 0812.3674v2 file already used elsewhere in the book.
Actual~1--2 and~47--48 were freshly read in the preceding source
checkpoint. This is neither a new acquisition of the published
edition nor a claim that all its proofs were re-read. The logarithmic
mass ratio in its fixed-point setup is added as a constant; the
printed concluding $\log c_\psi$ conflicts with its definition of
$c_\psi$ as an already logarithmic ratio. The defining equation
controls the normalization used here.

\cite{DGG26FamiliesCorrectedV3} controls exact
2003.08178v3, revised 5 February 2026, sixty-five physical pages.

All five published pages of \cite{DGG26CorrigendumPublished},

\cite{GuedjLu25EnvelopeEstimatesPublished} is the official

\section{Segre degrees, finite pullbacks and generic L\^e numbers}
\label{sec:appL-Segre-source-gates}

\Cref{sec:appC-Segre-dependence,sec:appF-Segre-ample-boundary,sec:appH-finite-pullback-Segre}
give attributed ideal-theoretic, projective and local analytic
comparisons. The global degree criterion and the local generic
numbers have different definitions. Numerical equality is used
only under the nested-ideal and positivity assumptions stated
in the corresponding source.

\cite{CidRuiz26SegrePublished} is the controlling published
twenty-three-page article, Math.~Ann.~395 (2026), article~101,
accepted 18 June and published online 6 July 2026.
All twenty-three physical pages were read. Theorem~A is
Theorem~4.3, on actual~12--14: equidimensional projective schemes
over a field, an ample line bundle and $W\subset Z$ are
required; nonreduced schemes are included. Setup~4.1 and
Lemma~4.2 are on~11--12. Proposition~4.4 and its two claims
were read in full on~14--18. Their Kleiman--Thorup,
multigraded vanishing and intersection-theoretic foundations
remain imported, rather than recursively re-proved here.
Example~4.7 is on~18--19 and Corollary~5.2 on~20--21.
The latter's saturated ideals carry integral-closure bars,
which the extracted text loses but the images retain.
The arXiv history records 2512.08863v1 on 9 December 2025
and v2 on 18 June 2026; those PDFs were not acquired or
read, and their theorem numbering is not substituted.

\cite{JelonekPerezSantos26SegreV1} controls the exact
seventeen-page 2610.07528v1, submitted 5 October 2026;
all pages were read. The finite-flat bounds in Theorem~3.4,
actual~6--7, concern extension of an ambient ideal, while
Theorem~5.1, actual~10, compares Jacobians lexicographically
for multiplicity-one pullback. Theorem~4.2 on~8--9 and
Corollary~4.3 on~9 give the isolated Milnor comparisons.
Proposition~6.1 on~13--14 requires compatible smooth finite
transversal slices; Corollary~6.2 on~14 adds a pulled-cut
multiplicity equality. Examples~7.3--7.4 are on~15--16;
the first has transverse Milnor number one on both sides,
and the second distinguishes prepolar from generic values.
Conjectures~8.1--8.3 are on~16--17. The componentwise Segre
and generic-L\^e formulations are equivalent and include
arbitrary pullback multiplicity. Their known special cases
do not close the general nonisolated frontier.

\cite{AchillesManaresi06Author} is the twenty-eight-page
2006 author file; actual~1--4 and~7--13 were read.

\section{Mixed Segre zeta numerators and Lorentzian normalization}
\label{sec:appL-mixed-Segre-source-gates}

\Cref{sec:appC-mixed-Segre-zeta,sec:appD-Lorentzian-normalization,sec:appF-Chern-volume-bridge}
connect global ideal intersection data with factorial-normalized
polynomials. The formal statements are attributed imports;
the examples and homogenization identity are explanatory
specializations or direct formal consequences. No original
theorem or independent verification receipt is added.

\cite{CidRuiz26MixedSegreV2} controls the exact
twenty-nine-page arXiv:2507.06424v2, submitted 10 July 2026
at 20:56:10 UTC and internally dated 14 July. All twenty-nine
physical pages were read. The initial version was submitted
8 July 2025; its PDF was not acquired or read. The arXiv
comments and author website say Algebra \& Number Theory,
to appear, without providing an inspected published edition.
The v2 numbering controls this integration.

Definition~3.2 is on actual~5--6; Remark~3.4 and Example~3.5
distinguish the generally non-Cartier deformation transform
from the Cartier inverse image of the parameter divisor.
The ordinary-blowup formula is Theorem~3.7 on~7--9.
Corollary~3.9 and Proposition~5.7 on~20 require multinomial
weighting for the product-ideal specialization. Theorem~3.10
retains its equidimensional, nowhere-dense, dominance and
degree premises. The equal-degree graph Setup~3.11 is not
extended by saturation to arbitrary stabilized homogeneous
ideals. The stronger dimension bound in Theorem~4.8 is
retained in Lemma~5.5 on~19.

Definition~5.2 and Theorem~A, numbered~5.3, are on~17;
Lemmas~5.4--5.6 occupy~18--20. Positive degrees are explicitly
used in the proof. The book states nonzero proper homogeneous
ideals with nonzero positive-degree generators; zero/unit
conventions are not added by interpreting the broad wording
of the preprint. Proposition~5.8 on~21 supplies invariance
under integral closure, without a general mixed converse.
Definitions and rationality use a field; the Lorentzian
statements require it to be algebraically closed.

Definition~6.1 is on~21, Proposition~6.2 and its proof on
~22--23, and Theorem~B, numbered~6.3, on~24. The last concerns
the chosen numerator $Q=D-P$ of $1-\zeta$, including its
degree of homogenization and factorial normalization.
The book's equal-dimension Chern specialization avoids
negative hyperplane exponents. Positive kernel rank is
arranged, and a dimension drop of the incidence image is
treated as a zero pushforward. The passage from a degree-$N$
coefficient polynomial to the usual homogenization is the
termwise identity \eqref{eq:appD-homogenization-derivative}.
These source descriptions import their intersection-theoretic
foundations; they are not recursive proof certificates.

The published \cite{BrandenHuh20Lorentzian} is the
seventy-one-page Annals article, physical page~1 corresponding
to printed page~821. Actual~2--3, 11, 14--15, 25, 34,
51--53 and~58 were freshly read for this source checkpoint.
Definition~2.6 is on physical~11 / printed~831;
Theorem~2.25 on~25 /~845; Corollary~2.11 on~15 /~835;
Corollary~3.7 on~34 /~854.
The volume input is \emph{Theorem~4.6}, physical~52--53 /
printed~872--873, with the irreducible projective,
algebraically closed context on physical~51 / printed~871.
The mixed v2 cites ``Theorem~4.16'' for this input on
actual~3 and~23, while listing the published Annals article.
In those exact published bytes, physical~58 / printed~878,
4.16 is a Corollary about $M$-matrix support. The live book
uses the checked 4.6 locator. No older-edition explanation
is inferred, and this citation defect is not declared a
counterexample to the mixed result.

\cite{Aluffi23CovolumeV4} is the twenty-nine-page
2304.02043v4 of 17 December 2023, internally dated 1 December;
actual~1--3, 5--9 and~13--18 were read. Definition~2.1 is
on~5, Example~2.7 on~7--8, Proposition~2.8 on~8--9,
and Proposition~3.9 with its complete displayed proof on
~16--18. Theorems~3.5--3.6 on~15--16 and the start of their
proof on~18 were read; the remaining proof on~19--20 was
not selected. Its recorded Adv.~Math.~437 (2024), article
109440, thirty-seven-page journal edition was not acquired.
Preprint locators do not authenticate that edition.

The covolume polynomial records a cohomology class; a volume
polynomial records intersection degrees on the irreducible
variety. The exponent-reversed normalization in Proposition~2.8
is distinct from direct normalization. The p.3 conjecture
on the normalized modified numerator of one multihomogeneous
ideal in a product of projective spaces is a source-era
question. Its current literature status is not certified
here, and the homogeneous-sequence theorem is not labelled
its solution. A failure of Lorentzian normalization for a
general covolume polynomial is not a counterexample to
that more specific conjecture.

\cite{Aluffi16SegreZetaV2} is the nineteen-page
1606.03098v2 of 27 July 2016; actual~1--2 and~9--12 were
read. Example~5.6 on~9 distinguishes the homogeneous vertex
from a fixed projective zero scheme. Theorem~5.8 on~10 and
Proposition~5.12 on~11 give the one-ideal rationality and
integral-closure antecedents; the displayed proof on~12 uses
globally generated Chern cycles. The uninspected 2017
published edition has different page extent. The preprint's
reduced-denominator ``monic'' wording is not imported;
the chosen product denominator suffices for this exposition.

Source Examples~7.1--7.3 on actual~24--27 retain the
complete-intersection, disjoint-variable and convergence
premises. Examples~7.4 on~27 and~7.6 on~28 have exact
integer/rational arithmetic checks, as does the source's
existing covolume signature example. No Macaulay2
run, acquisition or reading of the author's linked code,
general binomial-convolution theorem, or floating-point
signature certificate is claimed. Fulton, Vasconcelos,
Kleiman--Thorup, Bertini, Hodge index and the unselected
Br\"and\'en--Huh proof inputs remain imported boundaries.

The frozen source audit records sixty distinct source/page
pairs in thirty-one individually displayed sheets: one
complete PDF and three partial readings, with no reading
attestation reuse. This integration reuses that evidence
and adds no fresh primary-page reading. The canonical atomic
problem inventory, the local componentwise Segre/L\^e frontier,
and the general BJ22 critical Siu-division and envelope
