\section{Volume operators and Richardson intersection models}
\label{sec:appL-volume-Richardson-sources}

\Cref{sec:appD-volume-operators,sec:appD-reverse-KT,sec:appF-total-Chern-volume,sec:appF-Richardson-volume,sec:appG-multidegree-support}
connect factorial normalization with exact projective realization.
All formal additions are definitions or attributed imports.
The displayed Chern shift, finite-dual identity and coefficient
diagnostics are source reconstructions or direct formal specializations;
no original theorem or independent-model receipt is added.

\cite{GrundHuhMichalekSuessWang25VolumeV1} is the twenty-seven-page
arXiv:2506.22415v1 of 27 June 2025. All pages were read.
Definitions~1.1--1.2, physical~2--3, distinguish rational
realization by semiample Cartier divisors from the coefficientwise
closed cone. Theorem~1.3 and Corollary~1.4 are on~3--4,
with proofs on~12--13. The cutoff uses divided powers and a
coordinatewise bound. Theorem~1.12, on~6 and~14--15, gives
a sufficient bounded-symbol criterion; homogeneity means a fixed
degree shift. The dual converse in Theorem~1.9 uses additional
variables, and Remark~3.4's omitted quartic computation is not
independently verified here. Proposition~4.4, on~19, retains
multiaffinity and rational $0\leq t\leq1$ for the displayed
convex mixing operation. Its scalar quadratic realization input
is imported; its source's full classification proof was not read.

The component argument in Lemma~2.1, physical~7--9, relies on
\cite[Lemma~2.6]{HuangHuhMichalekWangWang25RealizationsV2}.
The selected physical~1--9 of that forty-page v2 include the
lemma's entire displayed statement and proof on~9. Proper
surjectivity, integral source and target, generic fibre components
and their positive multiplicities matter. Algebraic equivalence
allows comparison of intersection degrees; it is not an assertion
that the general fibre is irreducible. The v2 notation on~5
extends the stated results to arbitrary algebraically closed fields
with its explicit exceptions. This clears an earlier provisional
complex-only reading note, without crediting its unselected later
proofs as read.

GHMSW physical~5, footnote~2, identifies an irreducibility gap in
\cite[Proposition~2.6]{RossSuess25DiagonalizationsV1} and the
earlier two-author preservation route. All ten pages of that fixed
Ross--S\"uss v1 were read. Its physical~3--4 assert an irreducible
representative for a Schubert degeneracy class and invoke Fulton
1992, Theorem~8.2. That foundational theorem was not recursively
read here. The live construction uses the separate GHMSW
component argument. A gap in one route is not a disproof of
every preservation conclusion; unscaled factorial and binomial
identities from that route are not copied as literal identities.

\cite{NguyenDang26RichardsonV2} is exact arXiv:2608.10516v2,
submitted 25 August 2026. Its author is Khai-Hoan Nguyen-Dang,
with an appendix by Zhenpeng Wang. All forty-two pages, including
the appendix, were read. Theorem~3.1 on physical~6--9 uses a
projective complex variety and globally generated bundles.
The quotient-projective convention, positive evaluation-kernel
ranks, dimension $d+\sum_i(s_i-1)$ and factorials control
\eqref{eq:appF-Chern-shift-removal}. The smooth clause uses
characteristic-zero resolution and need not preserve the specific
projective-bundle model after differentiation.

Theorems~4.1--4.2, physical~9--13, use opposite-flag ordinary
Richardson varieties, the rectangle dual Schubert index, Pieri
and Poincar\'e duality. Section~5, on~13--17, uses Hopf-dual
ordinary $P/Q$ functions and the modified-$Q$ Chern presentation
on the Lagrangian Grassmannian. The rank and shift are $N$ and
$N-1$. The author-hosted May 3, 2003 version of
\cite{KreschTamvakis03LagrangianAuthor} has twenty-seven pages;
physical~1--4 and~12--13 were read. The modified Pfaffians and
set-of-parts complement are explicit on~2 and~12. This is not
the unacquired thirty-four-page published edition, and the
unselected quantum proofs do not control a new quantum claim.

Wang's Appendix~A, physical~28--42, uses the integral modified-$P$
spinor convention, quotient rank $N+1$ and shift $N$.
The publisher PDF of \cite{KreschTamvakis04OrthogonalPublished}
has nineteen pages, printed~482--500; physical~1--3 and~7--8
were read. Its classical presentation confirms
$c_j(E_D)=2\tau_j$ and the top-Chern zero relation.
Appendix~A.5--A.6 supplies the all-characteristic cycle extension;
it does not supply smooth resolution in all characteristics.
The characteristic-two ambient maps of A.7 are finite flat
radicial. A.8--A.10 retain reduced Richardson preimages and
restricted degrees $2^b,2^{d-b}$, rather than asserting flatness
of the restrictions. A mistaken preliminary degree note was
explicitly corrected before integration. The integers in Chow
groups are not scalar coefficients modulo two. A.12's bridge
is a regular section of the stated codimension; A.13 does not
identify the complex type-C and type-D varieties.

\cite{Zhang26SkewSchurV1} is the nine-page arXiv:2608.12266v1
of 12 August 2026; all pages were read. Theorem~1.2 uses a
shifted flagged Schubert specialization. Theorem~1.3, physical
~5--7, uses the nonzero finite alphabet and $L\geq\lambda_1$
in the complementary shape $(L^n,\mu)/\lambda$. Dual
Jacobi--Trudi gives exact reversal with exponent bound
$(L,\ldots,L)$; Theorem~1.1 treats zero and empty shapes
separately. This is distinct from rectangle Poincar\'e duality
in the Richardson construction. Its root log-concavity does not
prove log-concavity of individual LR multiplicities. Flagged
Schubert, dually Lorentzian and symmetric-function foundations
remain cited imports, not recursively certified papers.

The published thirty-four-page
\cite{CastilloCidRuizLiMontanoZhang20MultidegreesPublished}
was read on physical~1--4, 17--20 and~22--25. Theorem~3.12
requires every subset projection inequality; Corollary~3.13
requires one common top-dimensional component in the reducible
case. Proposition~5.1 on~23 gives the function-field rank.
GHMSW Proposition~5.4 on~21 explicitly answers the older
Question~5.8 over any field via a finite-generator graph closure.
That later answer supersedes provisional comparisons made while
reading the older question. It does not classify closed-cone
supports or prove general algebraic-matroid duality.

\cite{JiangLi23ReverseKTV3} controls all fourteen pages of
arXiv:2112.02847v3, submitted 21 August 2023 and internally
dated 22 August. The published Math.~Z.~305 (2023), paper~26,
was not acquired. Theorems~1.1 and~3.5 and their complete
displayed proofs on physical~1--2 and~8--9 retain arbitrary
characteristic, nef Cartier divisors and the reciprocal binomial
factor. Multipoint, rather than one-point, Okounkov volumes
are used. Ample strictness and the nef sharpness example have
different hypotheses. Proposition~5.1's equality characterization
on~12 concerns nonzero equality on a smooth surface only;
the higher-dimensional discussion is a guess. Transposed
dimension exponents and graph-composition notation elsewhere
in that fixed source are not reproduced as literal formulas.
Its external Okounkov and restricted-volume foundations remain
imported boundaries.

The entire twenty-four-page \cite{Huh26VolumeSurveyV4}, revised
4 June 2026, was read. Physical~14--15 supplies the rational
Lorentzian cubic excluded by reverse KT. Footnote~1 on~3
reports, with attribution to Helen W.~J.~Zhang and Chengdong
Zhao, a counterexample to the exact five-dimensional
codimension-two convex projection conjecture, and separately
discusses Shouda Wang's approximations and a closure variant.
The footnote does not give a full counterexample proof, so it
is an attributed report, not an independent proof certificate.
It does not concern the distinct area-projection conjecture.

The survey's field-independence, analytic-preserver, Fano
closed-cone and normalized-general-Schubert questions are tied
to that revision. They are not certified as today's open-problem
inventory. The ordinary skew-Schur theorem alone is not a proof
for all Schubert polynomials. Subsequent literature must be
checked separately before any current-status assertion.
No change is made to the canonical atomic problem inventory,
the UNVERIFIED LPWZ density diagnostic, or the general BJ22
critical Siu-division and envelope boundary.

The frozen source checkpoint covers 158 distinct source/page
pairs in 81 individually displayed sheets, with complete text
and sequential notes: six entire PDFs and four partial readings.
This integration reuses that evidence. Exact rational diagnostics
check finite tableau complementation, Chern-shift factorials,
symbol contraction, quadratic signatures, the cubic obstruction
and zero matroid boundaries. Finite checks are not universal
geometric proofs, and no new independent-model call is claimed.

\section{Chern flow, full moments, and tropical certificates}
\label{sec:appL-Chern-flow-sources}

\Cref{sec:appD-Chern-packets,sec:appD-Chern-apolar-obstruction,sec:appF-Chern-flow,sec:appF-Chern-moments,sec:appG-tropical-Chern-certificates,sec:appG-Chern-nonvanishing}
continue the volume and Richardson material through generated
quotient-flag bundles and full Chern moments. The formal additions
are definitions and attributed imports. Their displayed identities
and examples reconstruct the cited sources. No original theorem,
independent-model receipt, or canonical problem-status promotion
is added.

The controlling primary source is the seventy-four-page
arXiv:2609.02850v4, submitted 13 September 2026,
\cite{NguyenDangWang26ChernFlowV4}. Khai-Hoan Nguyen-Dang
and Zhenpeng Wang are both authors of this main paper. It is
distinct from the preceding Richardson preprint with Wang's
appendix. All seventy-four physical pages were read.
Theorem~1.1 on~4--5 gives the arbitrary-field packet result;
the characteristic-zero smooth realization is a separate clause.
Propositions~2.8 and~2.10 on~13--15 use integral incidence
and evaluation-kernel-dual projective bundles. The former avoids
assuming an integral general section zero scheme; the latter
retains the fibre-top shift and factorial removal.

Section~3 on~16--22 uses actual quotient flags, whole rows
at row completion, and old upper roots for co-rows at row start.
Proposition~4.1 on~22--23 is the actual $K^0$ divergence identity,
with no acyclicity assumption. Definitions~4.3--4.4 on~23--24
retain creation states. Lemma~4.6 and Theorem~4.7 on~25--26
give the all-kernel terminal criterion and balanced generated
packet bundle. A later terminal root cannot repair an internal
state deficit. The general Appendix~C path and flow-polytope
statements have their additional graph hypotheses.

Sections~5 and Appendix~A, physical~27--37 and~59, keep the
ordinary operator word, finite complement, variable reversal and
changing homogenizing cap. The selected physical~1--3 and~5--7
of the twenty-three-page
\cite{BuciumasScrimshawWeber20LascouxV2} check the braid and
atom conventions; its full vertex-model proof was not selected.
The full-packet realization precedes differentiation into layers.
Independent row-variable identification occurs before normalization:
the source's Remark~B.8 on~64 has
$\mathcal N_{u,v}((u+v)^2)|_{u=v=z}=3z^2$, whereas
$\mathcal N_z(4z^2)=2z^2$.

The historical support comparison uses
\cite{MonicalTokcanYong17NewtonV2}, the returned thirty-page
v2 of 10 March 2017 (arXiv:1703.02583v2); physical~1--3, 14--17 and
20--23 were read. Its numbers are key SNP~3.10, Demazure
atom SNP~3.14, ordinary Schubert SNP~5.1, Grothendieck~5.5,
Lascoux~5.6 and Lascoux atom~5.7. The double-Schubert
question~5.2 and stronger Schubitope descriptions~3.9 and~5.13
are distinct. The fifteen-page returned arXiv:2201.09452v1 of
24 January 2022, \cite{MeszarosSetiabrataStDizier22SupportV1},
was read on~1--3. Conjectures~1.1--1.4 concern support;
the signed-coefficient claims~1.5--1.6 do not follow from
support convexity alone. These locators belong to the stated
editions. Straight flagged-Schur conclusions use an additional
Postnikov--Stanley input not read here; arbitrary flagged skew
shapes are not included by that argument.

Section~6, physical~38--47, constructs the multiplication
annihilator and monic inverse presentation before imposing a
full positive cone. Geometric coefficients are real diagonal
Hodge cohomology, not an arbitrary Chow ring presumed to
satisfy standard conjectures. Apolar recovery in Proposition~6.12
on~43 requires degree-one generation and spanning directions.
The quartic Example~6.17 on~46--47 has an all-field toric
volume model and Hard Lefschetz, but a negative primitive
degree-two square. The selected~1--2 and~15--18 of the
twenty-one-page returned arXiv:1008.4749v3, 10 February 2012,
\cite{Huh12MilnorV3}, include the ordered-simplex formula in
Lemma~36 on~17. That formula cites Shephard; its foundational
proof was not reproduced or independently read here.

Boundary descent uses
\cite[Lemma~4.6, physical p.~26]{AminiHuhLarson25DecompositionV1}.
The selected~1--5, 14--16 and~24--28 of that thirty-nine-page
v1 include Proposition~2.10's statement and proof. Its subring
transfer requires a nondegenerate pairing, one-dimensional top,
closure-direction generation, and the relative-interior cone
with an orientation sign. Its proof invokes the main decomposition
results; their full proofs were not selected. Proposition~2.8's
numerical Chow conclusion is conditional on standard conjectures.
Early primary reading notes incorrectly abbreviated reference~[26]
as AHK; the final primary note explicitly corrects it to
Amini--Huh--Larson. The earlier notes remain preserved.

All thirteen pages of arXiv:0707.1352v2, dated 19 February 2008,
\cite{Cattani08MixedLefschetzV2}, were read. Theorem~3.2 on~8
states the image, regrading, pairing and all-$\lambda>0$
Lefschetz hypothesis used for descent. Physical~9 explicitly
refers to the involved Cattani--Kaplan--Schmid proof rather than
reproducing it. This classical foundation remains imported.
The special interior case and the printed mixed Lefschetz and
Hodge--Riemann proofs were read; that does not constitute a
recursive verification of every cited input or the unacquired
twenty-page published edition.

The controlling KM edition is the thirty-eight-page
arXiv:2405.03576v2 of 14 August 2024,
\cite{KavehManon24TropicalBundlesV2}. Physical~1--4, 9--17,
19--21 and~23--25 were read. The global-generation witness is
Definition~6.4/Theorem~6.5 on~24. The circuit and upper-flat
criteria determine the sign convention. On~14, Definition~3.2's
positive flat-indicator formula and its successive-layer prose
have opposite orders; Proposition~3.7's increasing initial-span
paragraph inherits that discrepancy. For $U_{2,3}$, weights
$(1,0,0)$ satisfy the minimum circuit condition: the upper set
$\{1\}$ is a flat but the lower set $\{2,3\}$ is not.
The live formula uses the circuit criterion and fundamental
circuits, not that flag-order shorthand. This bounded observation
is not a disproof of the Chern-flow main theorem and makes no
claim about an uninspected later edition. Tropical higher sheaf
cohomology remains undefined in the selected KM discussion;
its Euler characteristic is an alternating sum of ranks.
The historical Problem~1.10 is not certified currently open here.

All nineteen pages of
\cite{LarsonPartida26ProjectiveBundlesV1}, arXiv:2604.21925v1,
were read. The biflag proof of Theorem~2.6, the duality and
relative-class arguments, and the joint construction in Theorem~3.9
on~17--18 give the abstract certificate. The proof invokes AHL
Proposition~2.10, foundational fan Hodge theory, Higgs lifts and
Speyer truncation. These do not turn nonrepresentable matroids
into actual generated evaluation bundles. The selected~1--3 and
11--17 of the seventy-one-page
\cite{BergetEurSpinkTseng23TautologicalV4} check tautological
classes and inverse ambient characters; Appendix~III was not read.
The citation KM26 in Larson--Partida means Khan--Maclagan,
not Kaveh--Manon.

Physical~1--8 of the thirteen-page
\cite{Ardila04UltrametricsV1} include the complete displayed
projection proof on~4--8. Its minimum-basis and maximum-circuit
convention is negated for \eqref{eq:appG-matroid-weight-closure}.
Primary \S7, on~47--53, then gives the finite dual certificate,
joint moments and the attributed answer to KM Conjecture~1.7.
The full equivariant product, dual matroid and $+\xi$ sign are
retained. Primary \S8 on~53--58 gives ample detection,
Chern nonvanishing ranks and weighted coefficient inequalities;
fixed insertion and active-index shift are distinct operations.
Appendix~D on~66--71 separately treats nonflat base change,
certificate rank compression and fixed-source obstructions.
Those refinements do not certify every smaller apolar algebra
or an actual geometric kernel of every chosen auxiliary rank.

The frozen source checkpoint has 182 distinct source/page pairs
and 94 individually displayed matching images, with complete
selected text and sequential notes: three whole PDFs and eight
partial readings. This integration reuses that evidence with no
fresh primary-page credit. Nine exact diagnostic groups include
2,141 small terminal profiles, packet reciprocity, apolar signs,
moment quotients and the flag-order witness. They are finite
checks, not universal geometric proofs. The failed first
normalization transcription remains a failed diagnostic.
The canonical forty-two-atom/twelve-problem inventory, the

\section{Tropical multidegrees and augmented polymatroid Hodge theory}
\label{sec:appL-tropical-multidegree-sources}

\Cref{sec:appG-tropical-positive-multidegrees,sec:appG-augmented-polymatroid-volume}
extend the projection-support discussion to positive tropical
cycles and distinguish it from coefficientwise Hodge inequalities.
The formal additions are attributed theorems and worked examples;
no original result, independent-model receipt or canonical
problem-status promotion is introduced.

The controlling primary source is Yairon Cid-Ruiz's
\cite{CidRuiz26TropicalMultidegreesV2}, arXiv:2608.25987v2.
All twenty-four physical pages were read. The arXiv version
stamp is 28 August 2026; the internal manuscript date is
31 August 2026. These dates are recorded separately.
Definition~2.4 on~6--7 requires positive stable intersection
against every nonempty tropical curve, not just positive
facet weights. Definitions~2.6 and~2.8 on~7 distinguish
projection-purity, common-facet selection and the stronger
translation-admissibility condition.

Proposition~2.12 on~8--9 gives submodularity under
projection-purity. Theorem~3.5 on~14--15 tests a single
facet against every subset, and Theorem~3.7 on~16 gives
the global rank criterion under the two weaker hypotheses.
Lemma~3.1 on~10--11 uses generated line bundles on an
irreducible complete complex variety. In its application,
one first separates the case where the joint image has
dimension less than the variety: the top intersection is
zero and the full-set inequality fails. In the equal-dimension
case the positive generic degree of the image map gives
the reduction to the multidegree theorem. The toric
linear-space proof in Proposition~3.2 on~11--13 uses a
smooth compatible compactification. Its zero contribution
for a cone outside the subspace follows from disjoint
orbit closures, not from an unjustified set-theoretic
intersection with excess dimension. Lemma~3.3 on~13--14
gives generated big nef divisors; it does not assert
ampleness on every refinement.

Example~2.10 on~7--8 and Proposition~4.2 on~17 give
the two-plane example and its Hessian with two positive
eigenvalues. Proposition~4.3 on~18--19 retains the support
of an irreducible tropicalization while changing its weights.
The polynomial $5s^2+8st+5t^2$ shows that even
translation-admissibility and codimension-one connectedness
do not imply Lorentzian coefficients. This is not a
counterexample to the volume theorem for an irreducible
algebraic variety: the new weights do not give the same cycle.

Section~5 on~19--22 constructs the augmented polymatroid
fan with unit facet weights. Lemma~5.5 on~20--21 handles
all block projections and chooses one coordinate facet for
an integral base. Theorem~5.4 on~20--22 adds the Hodge
certificate for arbitrary caged polymatroids and positive
divisors. Corollary~5.6 on~22 has zero-or-one coefficients
only for standard tropical hyperplanes. The top-intersection
polynomial has coefficients $r!a_n/n!$. Low degrees use
the nonnegative constant and linear conventions rather than
negative-order derivative notation from Definition~4.1.
The main source's Questions~1.2 and~1.3 concern a general
Lorentzian criterion and translation-admissibility of all
augmented polymatroid fans. They are source-era questions,
not separately certified current open problems here.

All eleven pages of \cite{He24TropicalMultidegreesV2},
arXiv:2306.10589v2 of 23 May 2024, were read.
The positive divisor and admissibility definitions are
2.7 and~2.8 on~4. Lemmas~2.11 on~5 and~3.1 on~7
give projection stability and a common facet; the generic
vector in the latter can be chosen rational outside the
finitely many forbidden subspaces. Lemma~2.13 on~6
uses the source's irreducibility convention and torus
sweeps. Theorem~3.2 on~8--9 and Proposition~3.4 on~9--10
give positive support and submodularity. The source's
abbreviated hyperplane-replacement proof is not used to
infer equality of all weighted coefficients. Algebraic
tropical lifting remains an explicitly cited external input.

Physical~3--5 and~7--9 of the seventeen-page
\cite{AllermannHampeRau15RationalEquivalenceV2},
arXiv:1408.1537v2 of 25 August 2015, were read.
Proposition~3.2 on~4 uses letters (a)--(e) in this version;
the published roman numerals in later citations are not
copied as its locators. Stable intersection preserves bounded
rational equivalence, and zero-cycle degree needs boundedness.
Proposition~3.3's proof on~5 constructs translation invariance
with a truncated linear function. Theorem~5.3 on~8--9
reduces to the translated-fan decomposition in Theorem~5.4;
the latter's Section~7 proof was not selected. Such a
decomposition can have signed summands, so it does not
give a positive or Hodge-preserving decomposition.

Physical~5--15 of the twenty-four-page
\cite{FultonSturmfels94ToricIntersectionV1},
arXiv:alg-geom/9403002v1 of 1 March 1994, were read.
The controlling numbers are Theorem~2.1 on~6 for operational
Chow classes and Minkowski weights, Corollary~2.4 on~7 for
the Picard group, Proposition~3.1 on~10 and Theorem~3.2
on~11--13 for multiplication and displacement, and
Lemma~3.4 on~12--13 for the saturated subtorus class.
These differ from the 1997 published numbers used in the
main source. V1 Lemma~4.4 is not the subtorus lemma.
Operational cohomology, completeness and all lattice
indices are retained; the underlying Chow duality and
classical toric pushforward inputs remain imported.

Physical~9--18 and~20--21 of the twenty-seven-page
\cite{EurLarson23PolymatroidsV2}, arXiv:2301.00831v2
of 30 August 2023, were read. Definition~3.7 on~12
has a strict compatibility inequality. Theorem~3.8 on~12--14
identifies the support with the augmented multisymmetric-lift
fan; its building-set foundation is Feichtner--Yuzvinsky.
Theorem~3.14 on~15 gives the zero-or-one class product,
and Theorem~3.17 on~16 gives the Hodge package using the
free-coextension support. Corollary~3.18 on~17 identifies
the multiplication annihilator. Its proof prints a wrong
complementary degree $n-r-k$: for ambient dimension $n$
and fan dimension $r$, the test degree is $r-k$.
The book uses this correct degree. Proposition~3.20 on~17--18
adds a wonderful compactification only for a represented
subspace arrangement over an infinite field.

Physical~41--46 of the fifty-eight-page
\cite{EurHuhLarson23StellahedralV3}, arXiv:2207.10605v3
of 7 September 2023, were read. Theorem~8.3 on~42--43
has the support-invariance and nef-volume inputs. Its
part~(4) describes the unnormalized mixed-coefficient
polynomial as denormalized Lorentzian; factorial normalization
and multiplication by $r!$ give the displayed top-intersection
polynomial. Theorems~8.7 and~1.12 use additional valuative
and projectivization axioms. Those axioms are not automatic
for arbitrary tropical bundles or weighted supports.

For \cite{ArdilaDenhamHuh22LagrangianV4}, we read physical
pages~10--13 and~51--69, including all of Section~5.
The checked edition is arXiv:2004.13116v4 of
14 February 2022, with seventy-three pages.
Definition~1.5 on~11 includes a positive fundamental
weight, duality, hard Lefschetz, primitive Hodge--Riemann
and the hereditary star condition. Theorem~1.6 on~12 and~67
requires identical supports, simplicial fans and nonempty
strictly convex cones; v4 removes the earlier unimodularity
assumption. The proof uses projective weak factorization
and edge subdivision; the external factorization and
Adiprasito--Huh--Katz foundations were not recursively reread.
The mixed Hodge theorem~5.20 and its full proof on~62
use induction on dimension, star restrictions and connected
signature constancy.

Two explicit formula issues in this ADH version are kept
separate from its support-invariance theorem. Under the
printed pullback and projection-formula relations, Lemma~5.21
on~63 gives
$x_\sigma=-a_1a_2p_*(x_\rho^2)$, so the coefficient in
$p_*(x_\rho^2)$ is $-1/(a_1a_2)$, not the printed
$-a_1a_2$. The corresponding star-pairing factor is still
positive, so its sign and signature argument are unaffected;
the book does not copy that coefficient. Theorem~5.29's
proof on~68 also overstates negative definiteness on the
orthogonal complement of an arbitrary primitive vector.
For its inequality, mixed Hodge--Riemann in degree zero
gives $\deg(\ell_2^2L)>0$ for strictly convex inputs.
Applying degree-one primitive Hodge--Riemann to
$u=\ell_1-\deg(\ell_1\ell_2L)\ell_2/\deg(\ell_2^2L)$
gives $\deg(u^2L)\leq0$, hence the stated inequality;
continuity handles boundary inputs. Neither issue is
reported as a disproof of the correctly formulated Hodge
or Lorentzian results.

The source checkpoint contains ninety-three distinct
source/page pairs and forty-eight individually displayed
matching images, with complete selected text and sequential
notes: two whole papers and five partial readings.
Six exact diagnostic groups check lattice saturation,
projection ranks, both printed counterexamples, factorial
normalization and the rank-two cage $(2,2)$ cones and
common-facet witnesses. They are finite checks, not a
universal proof or an open-problem solution. The initial
signed-minor expectation and later serialization failure
remain failed diagnostic records. The canonical forty-two-atom/
twelve-problem inventory, unverified LPWZ candidate and
general BJ22 critical Siu-division/envelope input are unchanged.


\section{Pointwise Nakano determinants and higher-degree boundaries}
\label{sec:appL-Nakano-determinant-sources}

\Cref{sec:appH-Nakano-determinants} imports theorems and
worked constructions from Zhangchi Chen's
\cite{Chen26NakanoDeterminantsV2}, arXiv:2609.07964v2,
revised 28 September 2026. All eighteen physical pages were
read as complete selected text with nine individually
displayed matching sheets. The arXiv source archive contains
the TeX file and its metadata, but not the two Python programs
advertised in Appendix~A. Those programs were acquired from
the author's linked homepage, pinned, completely read and
then executed using the installed SymPy; both finished with
all advertised checks passing.

Definition~2.1 on physical~5 gives the path formulation of
the pointwise Hodge--Riemann cone. Section~2 on~5--7 fixes
the $\rmi/2$ convention, coefficient tensors, partial fibre
transpose and $k!$ mixed-discriminant coefficients. The
diagonalization condition concerns form coordinates: the
positive coefficient matrices need not commute. Lemma~2.2
on~7 retains an arbitrary positive reference form, and
Lemma~2.3 on~7--8 extends the ambient dimension by positive
identity coefficients and antiholomorphic wedge factors.

Theorem~A and its complete \S3 construction on~8--9 give
the Nakano-positive range $2\leq k\leq n-2$. The two
exterior identities, scalar root and full coefficient tensor
are explicit. Partial transpose has eigenvalue $1-t$ with
multiplicity two, so the displayed matrix fails dual Nakano
positivity at the kernel parameter. Transpose supplies a
separate dual-positive example with the same determinant;
it does not make the original tensor satisfy both conditions.
Strict weak determinant positivity under either stronger
hypothesis uses Finski's published Theorem~1.1, retained as
the attributed input already discussed in
\Cref{sec:provenance-Griffiths-Chern}.

Proposition~4.1 on~9--10 gives disjoint-subset Lefschetz
blocks. Lemma~4.2 on~10--11 supplies a common finite rank-one
frame with strictly positive coefficients for every matrix.
Lemma~4.3 on~11 uses Cauchy--Binet and stability of the
multiaffine reversal. The exact stability input is
\cite[Proposition~2.2, physical p.~8, printed p.~828]{BrandenHuh20Lorentzian};
physical pages~7--9 of its seventy-one-page published PDF
were read in this checkpoint.

For \cite{RossSuessWannerer25DuallyPublished}, the thirty-page
2025 published edition was acquired, and physical pages
1--5, 8--10 and 16--21 were read. Definitions~4.1--4.2 on
physical~8 use factorial reversal normalization. Theorem~4.4
on~9 uses the Br\"and\'en--Huh symbol criterion; Theorem~6.4
on~17 adds strictness by surjectivity and openness. The
degree-consistent surjectivity fact can also be expressed
by apolar duality: the adjoint is multiplication by a nonzero
symbol. Theorem~7.2 on~18--19 imports Br\"and\'en--Leake's
cone-Lorentzian characterization. Theorem~8.2 and its full
proof on~20--21, printed~514--515, require positive definite
\emph{real} quadratic forms and a nonzero dually Lorentzian
symbol of degree $d-2$. Its equality case is proportionality.
Chen applies it to real diagonal matrices formed from the
strictly positive frame coefficients. The resulting quadratic
has full signature $(1,k+1)$, rather than just at most one
positive eigenvalue. This is Proposition~4.4 on~11--12 and
the essential nondegeneracy input in Theorem~B on~12.

The classical strict mixed-discriminant inequality,
cone-Lorentzian foundations and mixed linear Hodge theorem
remain external imports. The selected RSW proof has minor
degree/variable notation slips; the book uses the consistently
polarized identity and nonzero closed cone directions.
Its adjacent global K\"ahler integral statement is not copied
with unqualified global hypotheses. No complete recursive
certificate of all cited foundations is claimed.

Section~5 on~12--15 proves Theorem~C. The six-dimensional
seed on~13--14 uses positive definite, generally noncommuting
two-by-two coefficient matrices. The fifteen-by-fifteen block
factor and explicit $(2,2)$-kernel have their exact constants.
The rank extension on~14--15 uses counting identities and
an auxiliary signature $(1,m+5)$. An explicit congruence
propagates opposite endpoint signs. Adding
$\varepsilon\omega_{\mathrm{new}}I_2$, with small positive
$\varepsilon$, is essential: the unperturbed extra coefficients
in that block are zero. The interval argument applies after
this strict-positive perturbation, then Lemma~2.3 gives the
ambient-dimension extension.

The seed's attribution is
\cite[Example~9.2, physical p.~37]{RossToma21SchurHRV3},
arXiv:1905.13636v3 of 8 January 2021. Physical pages~36--38
of this forty-one-page version were read. The example on
$(\mathbf P^2)^3$ concerns $c_2$ of an ample \emph{real-twisted}
rank-three bundle. It does not give an ordinary holomorphic
bundle at every irrational twist, or identify its rank with
Chen's rank-two tensor. The cube-zero relation gives the
shared degeneration, up to a positive scalar.

Chen's Question~6.1 on~16 is historical relative to
\cite[Theorem~1.1]{Du26GriffithsTopChernV1} of 5 October 2026.
The Du source already fully read in the earlier checkpoint
gives negative top Chern jets in every rank at least nine.
Plane restriction and positive extension in new tangent
directions transfer that negative answer to Chen's weak
determinant question and its two boundary dimensions.
Ranks four through eight are the remaining candidates in
the compared sources, not an exhaustively certified open
list. Question~6.2 retains both stronger positivity conditions,
rank two, dimension four and the $(1,1)$-map. Neither
worked counterexample answers it, and the SD positive theorem
excludes coordinate-diagonal candidates. It is recorded as
a source-era local question without a canonical status change.

This checkpoint actually reads thirty-eight distinct exact
source/page pairs in twenty individually displayed matching
sheets: one whole paper and three partial dependency readings.
The older Du, Finski and Wan evidence is reused with zero
fresh reading credit. Seven independent exact diagnostic
groups check unrestricted ranks two through five, both
coefficient-tensor spectra, the full SD characteristic
polynomial and kernel, the symbolic auxiliary quotient, and
the one-positive-eigenvalue fixture with a zero eigenvalue.
The author programs have their own execution credit; they
are not called independent implementations. A first helper
failed because SymPy changed the assumptions on an identically
printed characteristic-polynomial variable. That failure is
retained; a new repaired helper passed, without changing
the mathematical formulas. Finite rank checks supplement
the read general proof rather than replacing it.

No original theorem, independent-model receipt or canonical
problem solution is introduced. The forty-two atoms in twelve
canonical problem environments, unverified LPWZ density
candidate, and general BJ22 critical homogeneous Siu-division/
envelope input retain their existing status.

\section{Triangular relations, finite metrics and signature boundaries}
\label{sec:appL-triangular-analytic-sources}

\Cref{sec:appH-triangular-Lorentzian} imports analytic
comparisons from \cite{BakerHuhKummerLorscheid26AnalyticV2},
arXiv:2607.15375v2, revised 18 August 2026. All eighty-eight
physical pages were read as complete selected text with
forty-four individually displayed matching sheets and
sequential notes. This is one complete primary reading,
with the exact selected dependency scopes below.

Definition~2.23 on physical~11--12 uses the exponential
embedding $f_\rho=\sum\rho(\alpha)x^\alpha/\alpha!$.
Consequently $N_tf_\rho$ has denominator $(\alpha!)^{t+1}$.
Theorem~3.1 and Proposition~3.7 on~12--21 establish the
upper containment with $N$, not by assuming its inverse
preserves Lorentzianity. Theorem~5.1 on~28 and its full
\S5 proof use $t>0$ for the lower containment. The
repeated-index upper calculation of Proposition~5.16
on~42--43 gives $2\max\{1,t\}$. Lemma~5.4's standalone
binary characterization on~29--30 requires the surrounding
exact-full-support assumption: local adjacent inequalities
alone allow the zero function and non-M-convex sparse support.

Physical pages~1--2 and~32--41 of the eighty-two-page
\cite{BakerHuhKummerLorscheid25TopologicalV3} were read.
The controlling edition is arXiv:2508.02907v3,
revised 10 October 2025.
Theorem~4.7 on~34--35 and its complete proof supply the
parallel-class factorization, including rank-one two-class
blocks, before any small uniform-matroid shortcut.
The uniquely normalized weights are those on active
variables; unused indices do not acquire unique weights.
Lemma~4.15 on~39 gives the derivative diagonal factors.
Theorem~4.4 on~33 concerns normalized coefficient powers
of the non-strict Lorentzian locus. The displayed logarithm
in Theorem~4.13 needs positive coefficients on every square
present in the prescribed support; zero coefficients there
are excluded before taking logarithms.

Part~2's quadratic-support notation on physical~34 instead
uses only off-diagonal active indices. For the singleton
support $\{2e_i\}$ this set is empty. The book separates
its immediate positive rank-one Hessian before using the
simplification and weighted-copy argument. Quartets with
three or four copies of the same index have coincident
products and are checked directly; an inadmissible
downstairs quartet is not used. Copy classes that contain
a two-element basis are not called parallel matroid classes.

Part~2 Remark~5.7 on~33 justifies an arbitrary-minor
threshold inequality by descending representations and
Lorentzian polynomials. This only controls representations
that extend from the original matroid; it does not prove
the assertion quantified over every representation of the
minor. That claim is not imported. A finite PG$(2,3)$
equality-matrix diagnostic is recorded in the editorial
source review as an unverified original candidate, with
no independent-model receipt or book theorem promotion.
Uniform-size comparisons and the reduction to rank-two
contractions are kept separate from this arbitrary-minor
claim.

The published \cite{FaverEtAl14RoundnessPublished} has
seventeen pages; physical~1 and~5--9 were read.
Theorem~3.6 on physical~8, printed~526, places the affine
independence conclusion strictly below
$\log_2(1+1/(m-1))$ for $m$ points. The endpoint in
Part~2 Theorem~4.3 follows by closure and permits zero
eigenvalues. Faver's older quantitative embedding and
strict-negative-type inputs remain attributed foundations,
not recursively reread in this checkpoint.

Theorem~6.1 and its complete proof on physical~44--48
give the balanced two-block upper bounds. Section~7
on~49--65, including Theorem~7.16 and Corollary~7.17,
was read in full. Its determinant-concavity argument
checks metric endpoints, tight Ptolemy via inversion,
attached tails and the centre-with-three-leaves case.
Example~7.11 on~56 supplies the sharp four-point
negative-type obstruction. The five-by-five singular
endpoint matrix in the book is its direct normalized
matrix realization, not a new theorem.

Physical pages~1--2, 17--20 and~35--40 of the ninety-page
\cite{BakerHuhKimKummerLorscheid25PolymatroidsV2} were read.
The controlling edition is arXiv:2507.14718v2,
revised 17 September 2025.
Definition~4.3 on~35--36 gives exact support, permutation
signs and the width-bounded strong and weak relations.
The near-idempotent vector formulation on~39--40 removes
signs over $\mathbf T_q$. Proposition~4.8 and its full
proof on~38--39 establish the up-operator equivalence.
For full binary support its natural matroid is
$U_{d,2d}$, the lift used in Part~2 Corollary~8.3.
Propositions~8.2--8.5 on~67--73 give the weak-to-strong
parameter obstruction and the explicit failures under
variable identification. Questions~8.6--8.7 on~73 are
recorded with their source-era status only.

For the published seventy-one-page
\cite{BrandenHuh20Lorentzian}, physical pages~14--16,
25--27, 30--37 and~44--46 were read: seventeen distinct
pages. Theorem~2.10 is on~14, printed~834; it permits
every nonnegative rectangular substitution. Theorem~2.25
and its following quadratic-derivative characterization
are on~25, printed~845. Theorem~3.2 and its full proof
on~30--32 give the homogeneous symbol criterion;
Corollary~3.7 on~34 has the factorial-log-concave
bivariate symbols. The M-convex generating-polynomial
direction of Theorem~3.10 is read through completion
on~35--37. Proposition~3.25 on~45 concerns normalized
coefficient powers $0\leq p\leq1$. Its Lemma~3.24 on~44
assumes every matrix entry positive; the proposition's
reduction handles zeros rather than silently applying
that lemma directly to a zero-diagonal Hessian.

Appendix~A of the analytic source was read in full.
Theorems~A.1--A.2 on~74 have the positive-edge,
distinct-leaf assumptions. The book uses the explicit
edge embedding, split energy and ultrametric congruence
of proofs two and three and Lemma~A.8. The canonical
metric-graph measure invoked in proof five need not be
a positive probability measure; no such assertion is
imported here. Proof six on~84--86 incorrectly uses
closedness of a strict-signature stratum and a strict
target for squared Pl\"ucker coordinates. Part~1
Theorem~5.1 on~41 has a non-strict target and explicit
boundary conclusions. Real columns $(1,j)$,
$j=0,1,2,3$, give a singular squared-Pl\"ucker matrix
with positive off-diagonal entries. The logarithmic-limit
argument alone therefore cannot establish strictness.
The elementary tree argument proves the stated strict
inertia without that route.

This checkpoint contains 135 distinct exact-source/page
pairs and sixty-eight individually displayed matching
images: one whole paper and four partial readings.
Seven exact diagnostic groups check determinant identities,
normalization/support boundaries, the finite PG$(2,3)$
data, all thirty weak and fifteen nontrivial strong
relations of the cubic example, the squared-Pl\"ucker
kernel and strict limit, a four-cycle obstruction, and
the signed canonical-measure wording boundary. The last
external canonical-measure formula is not independently
source-cleared; it is not a book input. Serialization
and administrative-path failures remain failed records;
corrected helpers have separate receipts. No finite
diagnostic is called an independent-model verification.

The author-linked Lean repository
\href{https://github.com/icarm/FourPointPtolemaic}{\texttt{FourPointPtolemaic}}
is pinned at commit
\begin{quote}
\small\ttfamily 1564ff7909a3adc980bf2a7e0efa5ced88c1383b
\end{quote}
Its README and file inventory were inspected; no Lean
source-proof reading or execution is claimed. The
canonical forty-two atoms in twelve problem environments,
the unverified LPWZ density candidate and the general
BJ22 critical homogeneous Siu-division/envelope input
retain their existing status.

\section{Positive-weight estimates and variational conventions}
\label{sec:appL-positive-weight-sources}

\Cref{sec:appH-positive-weight-estimates,sec:ytd-positive-weight-estimates}
use \cite{JubertPan26PositiveWeightsV1}, exact
arXiv:2610.10216v1, submitted 7 October 2026. All thirty physical
pages were read as complete selected text with fifteen individually
displayed matching two-page images and sequential notes.
The directly checked imports are the smooth PDE estimates of
Theorems~2.1, 2.3 and~3.1. The first statement and complete proof
occupy physical~9--17, the twisted statement and complete proof
17--20, and the direct soliton estimate~20--22. Introductory
Theorem~A omits the detailed dependence on bounded potentials and
densities; the book retains Theorem~2.1's full data and uses $n\geq2$
for its forward-trace exponent.

The source uses $D^c=4\pi\dc$, $D=4\pi\ddc$ and
$\mathcal R=-D\log\omega^n$, twice the usual Ricci normalization.
The book names these operators locally and translates its potential
by $\psi=4\pi\varphi$. Its smooth soliton class is therefore
$4\pi c_1(X)$ in those local units. No factor is transported alone.
The source's Claim~2.2 on~10 and equations~(2.3)--(2.4) on~11
give the fixed-background moment pairing and drift cancellation.
The integrations by parts on~13--16 retain all derivatives of $v$.
The twisted argument on~18--20 keeps reciprocal-trace remainders;
fixed ordinary volume bounds the weighted mass. Its H\"older step
has $1<q<p_0$, with no endpoint conclusion.

Four printed conventions prevent importing the larger equivalences
without repair. First, the definition
$\operatorname{Scal}_v=v\operatorname{tr}_v\mathcal R_v$ on~6
and equation $\operatorname{Scal}_v=w$ on~7 give the prescribed
function $h=w/v$ in the volume-density system. The system on~9
instead prints $-w$. The book estimates the independently named
function $h$, so it does not silently adopt that equivalence.

Second, physical~8 prints an additional factor $v$ in the Mabuchi
variation, but its integral condition~(1.4) has no such factor.
Definition~1.1 and Remark~1.2 of the published
\cite{ApostolovJubertLahdili23Published}, physical~14, printed~3241,
give the variation with $\omega^{[n]}$ and no extra $v$.
Definition~5, equation~(14), of
\cite{Lahdili19WeightedScalarV4}, physical~11, agrees after its
slope constant is absorbed in the prescribed scalar. The printed
Jubert--Pan variation is not used as a definition of the book's energy.

Third, the new Theorem~B uses $T_{\mathbf C}$ for an arbitrary
compact torus. Published Apostolov--Jubert--Lahdili Theorem~1 on
physical~6, printed~3233, requires maximal $T$. Its immediate
discussion and Remark~7.7 on physical~61, printed~3288, replace
$T_{\mathbf C}$ by the connected centralizer for a nonmaximal
torus. Proposition~1.4 and Theorem~1.5 on~15, printed~3242,
make the same group distinction. Thus the printed arbitrary-torus
equivalence is not imported here.

Fourth, the new weighted Donaldson--Futaki formula on physical~23
uses $8\pi$ times ordinary volume. The published source on~6
and Lahdili Definition~11/Remark~6 on~19 instead use
$8\pi\int_Xv(m_\omega)\omega^{[n]}$, with divided powers.
The weighted correction cannot be replaced by unweighted volume.
The corresponding new Corollary~C is not certified here as a
consistent algebraic equivalence.

The published Apostolov--Jubert--Lahdili publisher PDF contains
seventy-seven physical pages, including additional material.
Physical~2--3, 6, 13--15 and~61 were read: seven pages in four
matching images. The journal edition is Geometry \& Topology
27(8), 3229--3302, published 9 November 2023, with DOI
10.2140/gt.2023.27.3229. These selected checks clear definitions
and hypotheses; they do not recursively reread every proof invoked
by its coercivity theorem. The forty-seven-page Lahdili source is
specifically arXiv:1808.07811v4, revised 27 April 2019.
Physical~1--2, 7, 11--12 and~19--20 were read: seven pages in
four matching images. The citation does not assert inspection of
the separate published PLMS edition.

The new Sasaki construction on physical~24--26 fixes a regular
free-circle reference and a smooth quotient. Its candidate Reeb
may be irregular within the specified deformation family.
The reciprocal-affine weights on~25 explain why log-concavity
was restrictive. The full AC21/ACL21 geometric reduction is an
attributed antecedent, not reread in this checkpoint. The cone
comparison on~27 adds vanishing contact Futaki and distinguishes
normal central fibres. The cKEM correspondence on~28 additionally
needs the polarization required by Corollary~C and control of its
extremal affine factor: Lahdili physical~1--2 places the cKEM
reduction in a weighted cscK equation. Corollaries~4.7--4.8 and
the log-Fano extension left to readers in Remark~3.3 are not
promoted to checked theorems.

This checkpoint contains forty-four distinct exact-source/page pairs
in twenty-three individually displayed matching images: one whole
paper and two partial readings. Five exact diagnostic groups check
the absorption and H\"older parameters, determinant-trace comparison,
affine reciprocal powers, variation/Futaki normalization and the
$\mathbf P^1$ automorphism orbit. A polynomial-constructor failure
is preserved separately; a new helper clears positive denominators.
No computational diagnostic is an independent-model receipt.
The canonical forty-two atoms in twelve problem environments,
the general critical homogeneous Siu-division/envelope input of
BJ22 Theorem~6.21, and the unverified LPWZ density candidate
retain their existing status.

% Review fragment for the end of appL; not yet an active input.
\section{Metric entropy and the conventions of its analytic inputs}
\label{sec:appL-metric-entropy-sources}

\Cref{sec:appH-metric-entropy} imports the three main theorems of
Hisamoto \cite{Hisamoto26MetricEntropyV1}. The controlling version is
arXiv:\allowbreak2609.29002v1, submitted 24 September 2026. The controlling forty-nine-page PDF
was read in full. Its covering entropy uses natural logarithms,
open balls and internal centers. Ordinary relative entropy uses
probability Monge--Amp\`ere measures and equals twice the entropy
in \eqref{eq:ytd-analytic-entropy}. The background $L^1$ theorem
does not require polarization. The $d_1$ upper bound does, while its
lower bound does not. The toric import retains polarization and
uses zero symplectic mean rather than supremum zero. The dependence
on the entropy budget is not asserted optimal.

Eight cited primary dependencies were read in selected portions,
not in full. \cite{DLR18QuantizationV1} is arXiv:1806.03800v1;
its Theorem~4.1 is fixed-input convergence and Proposition~2.12
retains the product-positivity and adjoint hypotheses. It does not
itself give the entropy-uniform rate for the auxiliary-degree
operator. The endpoint comparison in its \S2.2 explicitly divides
both integrals by the volume. \cite{Darvas14KahlerGeometryV2}
and \cite{Darvas21GeometrySurveyV2} fix the probability-volume
Finsler definition and the strong finite-energy topology. Some
later unnormalized integral displays in the survey omit volume
factors; the book uses the consistently divided comparison with
a dimensional constant.

The author-hosted controlling edition of
\cite{BBEGZ16TameAuthor} supplies Theorem~2.17 and Corollary~2.19
on tame entropy-sublevel compactness and stability. Its printed
Pinsker coefficient is inconsistent with its full total-variation
norm, and two Orlicz-density displays use the density measure
where the reference measure is required. The argument is retained
with the reference-measure integrals; neither numerical discrepancy
is used to replace the entropy compactness theorem or to infer
strong compactness from an energy bound alone.

\cite{BBGZ09VariationalV1} is the fifty-four-page arXiv:0907.4490v1,
not an unchecked journal locator. Section~2 normalizes the big-class
volume to one; its $\ddc=i\partial\bar\partial/\pi$ and half-weight
conventions must be converted. The energy pushforward display in
Proposition~6.2 omits $1/(n+1)$, as a constant family shows. Its
zero-product affineness conclusion is unchanged. The unbounded
Bedford--Taylor product-limit sentence is not imported. In the
fixed-probability-measure case of Lemma~7.4, the intermediate
Hilbert--Fubini--Study trace is the rank $N$, not one; the normalized
ratio and the final fixed-point condition are consistent after
that correction. Exact fixed-point assertions for unnormalized
canonical or anticanonical measures require a compatible mass or
scalar convention and are excluded here.

\cite{CGSZ18ToricV1} is arXiv:1804.03387v1. The real Monge--Amp\`ere
measure has density $n!\det\operatorname{Hess}F$; the raw toric
distance display in its Proposition~3.9 omits the matching $n!$.
Probability normalization gives the intended constant-shift test.
Its citation to Guedj's $d_2$ Proposition~4.3 alone does not establish
a general $d_p$ identity. The $d_1$ identity used here is the separate
Legendre-geodesic argument in Hisamoto's \S9.1. Interior finiteness
and $L^1$ integrability allow infinite boundary values.
\cite{Abreu00SymplecticV1} supplies the smooth Guillemin boundary
model with a smooth positive determinant factor and primitive
Delzant facet normals. Neither interior Hessian positivity alone
nor the paper's broad projectivity wording is used to infer the
missing boundary or polarization assumptions.

The convex entropy theorem is
\cite[Theorem~1(ii)]{GaoWellner17AuthorPublished} in the controlling
author-hosted journal file, rather than the arXiv numbering
Theorem~1.1(ii). The import uses a fixed polytope, $p=1<r$ and
constants depending on that polytope. Literal auxiliary boxing
formulas, bracket radii and triangulation prefactors are not copied;
the polytope estimate is used with consistent radius allocation and
an unspecified geometric constant. Classical analytical inputs
such as Skoda integrability, point extension, Bergman asymptotics,
smooth Monge--Amp\`ere solvability and the underlying real transport
identity remain explicitly attributed. Selected-source reading is
not a recursive independent proof certificate for them.

Two corrections concern Hisamoto's own displays. For a row
$g=zH^{-1/2}$ of complex Gaussian coefficients,
$\mathbf E[\overline{g_j}g_k]=(H^{-1})_{jk}$ and
$\mathbf E[g_j\overline{g_k}]=(H^{-1})_{kj}$.
The component indices on actual page~23 are transposed, while the
matrix identity $\mathbf E(g^*g)=H^{-1}$ and the subsequent
Gaussian-law argument are consistent. On actual page~46 the
printed lower numerator in (10.5) is $-n\log k+C_X$, while its
displayed proof gives $-(n\log k+C_X)$. Only this weaker
proof-supplied bound is used for
\eqref{eq:appH-entropy-normalization-cost}; the sign difference
is not a counterexample to a stronger possible estimate.

The three formal statements in H are attributed imports with
unnamed numbered headings. Their explanatory calculations and
convention checks make no original-theorem or priority claim.
None changes a canonical research verdict, supplies an independent
model verification receipt, or repairs the general non-Archimedean
homogeneous Siu-division input.

\section{Holomorphic restriction entropy: source and normalization audit}
\label{sec:appL-holomorphic-entropy-sources}

\Cref{sec:appH-holomorphic-entropy} uses the entire twenty-one-page
\cite{Finski26HolomorphicEntropyV1}, arXiv:2607.18890v1.
Its formula~(1.9) and Theorem~1.3 are the two numbered attributed
imports. The function class uses restrictions of holomorphic
functions bounded by one on a connected domain, with the uniform
norm on $K$. This differs from the probability-normalized
K\"ahler-potential metric entropy in the preceding section.
The source operator is $D=2\ddc$, giving the factor $2^n$ in
local capacity. Ambient centers, internal centers and open or
closed covering radii are transferred with fixed radius changes,
not literal equality at every endpoint.

Four primary dependencies were read in selected portions.
\cite{Zeriahi91ParabolicPublished} was read in twenty-six of its
thirty-eight original scan pages. The scan controls mathematical
symbols; OCR is navigation only. Its Theorem~3.16 retains the
ambient holomorphic hull inside the specified neighborhood.
Theorem~5.4 and Corollary~5.5 give regular compact neighborhoods
and controlled polynomial approximation. The homogeneous-cone
construction retains total degree. Growth is controlled by the
positive part of the parabolic potential; a finite global lower
bound at its poles is not used. The smoothed maxima in the proof
of Proposition~3.4 decrease, despite the printed increasing
wording.

Theorem~4.2 of the July preprint states polynomial approximation
under intrinsic holomorphic convexity alone. The annulus example
in H excludes that generality. The actual strictly Runge
application retains the ambient hull condition and the source
approximation estimate. Its Theorem~4.1 proof also requires a
fixed radius reallocation, harmless at the small-radius
logarithmic scale. Section-space volume comparison is used only
with $\log(1/\varepsilon)=O(k)$.

\cite{Finski26Kodaira}, arXiv:2603.22194v1, was read in
thirteen of forty-one actual pages, including the whole printed
Lemma~4.7 proof. Its comparable upper semicontinuous metrics
retain a positive continuous lower bound on a nonpluripolar
subset. The minimal-singularity mixed-product passage uses
monotone potentials, uniformly bounded quasi-continuous tests
and convergence in capacity. The underlying classical proofs
remain attributed. The volume is ordinary real Hermitian
volume, with no stated square-root convention. The complete-series
case of its formulas~(4.37) and~(4.40), and of its introductory
Theorem~1.7, needs $2/n!$ times the raw energy in (4.36).
The constant-metric example on $\mathbf P^n$ detects the omitted
multiplier; accidental agreement in dimension two does not
establish the general coefficient. A corrected theorem for every
noncomplete graded series is not asserted.

\cite{Finski25BigQuantizationV1}, arXiv:2512.10466v1, was read in
ten of sixty-one actual pages. The metadata's fifty-seven pages
exclude references. Its norm conventions and large-subspace
min-max proof were read, not its whole quantization theorem.
For complex dimension $v$, the ordinary real-volume identities
in (2.75)--(2.76) need $1/(2v)$ rather than $1/v$. The scalar
one-dimensional norm example has zero $\log v$ error allowance
and detects the factor directly. Hermitian quotient-volume loss
has the same real factor two; John errors are lower order in the
linked degree limit. The space of all Hermitian norms is
$\mathrm{GL}/\mathrm U$; $\mathrm{SL}/\mathrm{SU}$ requires
a fixed determinant. Neither neat algebraic singularities nor
the large-subspace comparison proves a critical homogeneous
non-Archimedean division theorem.

\cite{BB10} was checked in fourteen pages of the fifty-nine-page
author-hosted published PDF, including the whole printed proof
of Theorem~A in \S5.1. The publisher cover is not counted as
read. Its controlling definitions use $1/(2kN_k)$ for the
logarithm of an ordinary real unit-ball volume, and
$E_{\mathrm{eq}}=E_{\mathrm{raw}}/\operatorname{vol}(L)$.
Its constant-shift test yields $2/n!$ times raw energy at
scale $k^{n+1}$, consistent with the July preprint's coefficient.
Two intermediate energy endpoints in its Step~2 omit the
volume divisor supplied by the theorem and closing sentence.
Remark~4.5's constant-family fiber formula requires $1/(n+1)$,
as in the earlier variational-source audit. An unrelated
mixed-current fragment on printed page~372 has the wrong
bidegree and is not copied. These local repairs do not alter
the unit-ball theorem's correctly normalized statement.

The July preprint's inequalities~(5.21)--(5.22) reverse the
$c'$ and $c''$ endpoints; H uses the increasing energy-gap
order. The outer-barrier supremum in Proposition~6.1 retains
a common positive barrier and a common small-$c$ threshold.
The sentence before~(6.13) must concern the top Monge--Amp\`ere
measure, not the whole curvature current. The bounded local
capacity limit then has its intended $c^{n+1}$ scale.

The source's general Stein result keeps the exhaustion capacity
on the abstract envelope of holomorphy separate from the
underlined capacity over containing hyperconvex Stein manifolds.
Its global-function variant uses an ambient holomorphic-hull
component instead. No identification of these objects is made
without its specific geometric assumptions. The capacity-equality
characterization is a direction posed in Remark~1.2, with no
solution asserted here.

Both formal statements are explicitly attributed imports with
unnamed numbered headings. Their explanatory calculations,
finite convention checks and proof repairs make no original
theorem or priority claim. Classical Bedford--Taylor,
minimal-singularity, approximation, resolution and Bergman
inputs remain attributed; selected reading is not recursive
independent certification of them. No canonical research status,
independent model receipt or general BJ22 Siu-division verdict
changes.

\section{Curve section-ball rates: sources and repairs}
\label{sec:appL-section-ball-rates-sources}
\index{source audit!section-ball volumes on curves}

\Cref{sec:appH-section-ball-rates} is an attributed account of
\cite{Wu26SectionBallRatesV1}, arXiv:2604.12333v1, posted
14 April 2026. Its complete twenty-eight-page PDF was read
with fourteen matching images. Five dependencies were read
in sixty-eight further pages and thirty-four matching images:
the whole thirty-four-page author-hosted published
\cite{DMN17FeketePublished}; eighteen of thirty-three pages
of \cite{Klevtsov19LaughlinV3}; six of fifty-three pages of
\cite{Zelditch11ZerosV1}; six of seventeen pages of
\cite{Wu25HoleProbabilityV1}; and four further pages of the
fifty-nine-page published \cite{BB10}. The last four are
physical pages~41--44, distinct from the earlier fourteen-page
BB10 selection in the holomorphic-entropy audit. Only Wu's
volume paper and DMN were read in full in this checkpoint.

The ninety-six selected pages and forty-eight images carry
actual reading credit. Acquisition, metadata, search, image
preparation, export and note concatenation do not. The frozen
reading ledger records source hashes, physical pages, matching
text/image hashes and notes. Six finite diagnostic groups,
with thirty-eight fixtures and six symbolic sphere checks,
are convention examples rather than an independent solver
receipt or recursive certification of classical inputs.

\subsection{Degree and rank}

The primary source uses the ordinary real volume divided by
$nN_n$, twice BB10's normalization. Its degree-one proof
does not justify its introductory formula with an unscaled
external field in arbitrary degree. At a degree-one root the
index becomes $dn$, the field becomes $\varphi/d$, and the
volume and limiting minimum acquire the factor $d$ in
\eqref{eq:appH-curve-degree-transport}. The nonconstant elliptic
example \eqref{eq:appH-curve-degree-test}, controlled by BB10
Theorem~A already read in the earlier checkpoint, detects
the missing degree divisor. Constant fields do not.

The root construction printed in Wu's Lemma~2.1 only obtains
equality after a genus power and leaves a torsion ambiguity.
Multiplication on $\operatorname{Pic}^0$ gives existence of a
root; no canonical choice is asserted. Riemann--Roch gives
$N_n=nd-g+1$ only in the nonspecial range. The selected
canonical/Arakelov constructions divide by $g$ or $1-g$, so
H keeps $g\geq2$ and sufficiently large tensor degree.

\subsection{Numerical determinant conventions}

\cite[Proposition~3.2]{Klevtsov19LaughlinV3} gives the Fay
prime-form identity. Its coordinatewise divisor comparison
is understood first on the generic locus of independent
fixed evaluations, and extended holomorphically to exceptional
tuples. The scalar may depend on the bundle, basis and degree;
the notation $C(\Sigma)$ does not prove uniformity in degree.

In that paper, equation~(4.39) regularizes the Arakelov Green
function by adding $\log\operatorname{dist}$, so its singularity
is $-\log r$. Equation~(3.27) prints $\exp(-G_A)$ over
unordered pairs for a squared determinant norm. At a generic
simple pair collision the theta factor is nonzero and the
prime form has a simple zero. The squared norm needs order
two, whereas the printed exponential supplies order one.
The calibrated factor is $\exp(-2G_A)$ over unordered pairs,
equivalently $\exp(-G_A)$ over ordered pairs. The later
ordered-pair exponent in~(4.40) has this scale. The numerical
equation~(3.27) is not copied literally.

The January 2011 arXiv version of \cite{Zelditch11ZerosV1}
is distinct from the 2013 journal pagination cited by Wu.
Its equation~(71) squares the determinant of the Gram matrix,
which gives the wrong basis-change scaling. Equation~(72)
puts $\rmi$ into the Gaussian factor of an invariant squared
norm, and several nearby potential formulas mix squared
and unsquared norms. They are not numerical inputs to H.
The controlling exact identity is \cite[Lemma~5.3]{BB10},
read on printed pages~376--377: the squared determinant
integral is $N!$ times a single Gram determinant.

In Wu's smooth metric change, the remaining coefficient
is $2(N_n-1)-2n=-2g$, rather than $-2$. This is a fixed
smooth one-body term and therefore contributes $O(N_n)$.
The logarithmic determinant integral has the opposite sign
to the ball volume, as its own equation~(4.2) states; the
later positive-sign line on page~25 must be reversed. The
normalizer, factorial and logarithms of theta bounds are
kept until the error estimate. The final source-volume
sign is recovered with these repairs.

\subsection{The boundary-envelope input}

The general boundary-contact assertion in
\cite[Lemma~2.3]{Wu25HoleProbabilityV1}, repeated in the
volume paper, is false for arbitrary negative continuous
boundary data. Its harmonic-replacement step cannot raise
an envelope already harmonic near an inactive boundary point.
A direct smooth example also contradicts the claimed conclusion.

On the affine sphere coordinate $z=re^{\rmi\theta}$, let
$\rho=\tfrac12\log(1+r^2)$ and $\omega=D\rho$. For
$u=-a+A\cos\theta$ on $r=1$, with $A>1/2$ and
$a>A+\tfrac12\log2$, the two Dirichlet solutions are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 V_{\rm in}&=-a+Ar\cos\theta+\tfrac12\log2-\rho,\\
 V_{\rm out}&=-a+Ar^{-1}\cos\theta+\tfrac12\log2-\rho+\log r.
 \end{aligned}
\]
They match the boundary data, extend continuously at zero
and infinity, and are strictly negative everywhere.
Their radial derivative jump at $r=1$ is $1-2A\cos\theta$.
Consequently $DV+\omega$ is the boundary measure
$(1-2A\cos\theta)d\theta/(2\pi)$, negative near $\theta=0$.
If $\widehat U$ were the asserted envelope, the maximum
principle would first give $\widehat U\leq V<0$ on both
components. Its asserted off-boundary harmonicity and boundary
contact would then force $\widehat U=V$ by Dirichlet uniqueness,
contradicting its required positivity.

The actual section-ball smoothing uses boundary traces of
existing Green potentials. Those potentials are admissible
competitors and give contact. Local harmonic balayage then
replaces the logarithmic pole with a positive boundary
measure. H uses this special construction, not the false
arbitrary-trace lemma. The remaining hole-probability proof
and its large-deviation inputs were not read or certified.

\subsection{Regularity, perturbation and variation}

\cite[Theorem~2.7]{DMN17FeketePublished} gives H\"older
envelopes for exponent strictly between zero and one. Its
endpoint estimates are log-Lipschitz. A Lipschitz input can
use a fixed smaller exponent for the displayed volume rate.
DMN also uses the positive shorthand norm $1+\|\cdot\|$.
Thus the early conditional zero-field objections in the
frozen Wu reading notes are not treated as established
defects if that convention is inherited. H uses $1+B$ and
fixed bounded families throughout.

Three $O(m^2\log m)$ terms in Wu's Lemma~5.3 require inverse
powers by its preceding lemma. The energy sum is over
distinct labels; geometric-diagonal deletion would lose
the infinite penalty for collisions. Theta-safe perturbations
must use product boxes inside a good neighborhood and a
finite compactness argument in the Jacobian parameter.
Replacing $g$ points changes $2gm-g(g+1)$ ordered pairs.
The shrinking-radius separation and perturbation estimates
hold for sufficiently large $m$. Exponentiating a local
H\"older estimate allows dependence on the fixed budget,
not a globally linear dependence on the ordinary norm.

For the last sup-ball comparison, the global functional
uses the envelope field $P_K^\omega\varphi$ before its
minimum is identified with the original $K$-functional.
Weighted $L^2$ comparisons retain the Bernstein--Markov or
mass-density constants. A supported measure alone may give
a degenerate norm. DMN's full variational proof, including
Lemmas~3.13--3.14, explains the square-root loss in the
Fekete-test rate; Wu states that corollary without a separate
final proof. No optimality claim is added.

These are explicitly attributed imports, convention repairs
and direct examples. The classical Green, Fay, envelope,
energy-differentiability and regularization inputs remain
attributed. No original theorem, independent model receipt,
canonical research status or general BJ22 Siu-division
verdict changes.

\section{Ricci entropy and singular distances: sources and repairs}
\label{sec:appL-ricci-entropy-sources}
\index{source audit!Ricci entropy and singular distances}

\Cref{sec:appH-ricci-entropy} uses the exact first versions of
\cite{GSS26RicciEntropyV1,GSS26GeometricHolderV1,GSS26GeometricStabilityV1},
all posted on 24 September 2026. Their respective thirty-five,
twenty-one and twenty-six physical pages were read in full.
The other complete readings are the thirteen pages of
\cite{EGZ13ContinuousApproximationV1}, twenty of
\cite{Honda18AlmostSmoothV3}, twenty of
\cite{CCHSTT25CurrentsV1}, six of
\cite{EGZ16ViscosityErratumV1}, twenty of
\cite{ChoChoi25ContinuityV2}, thirty-seven of
\cite{GLZ17EnvelopesV1}, and thirty-one of
\cite{GKSS25HolderV1}. These are ten complete PDFs, with
their acquired versions and hashes fixed in the reading ledger.

Four further sources were read only in selection:
sixteen of forty-six pages of \cite{FGS25RCDThreefoldV1};
four of the nine-page publisher file of
\cite{Vajaitu01EmbeddablePublished}, whose first page is a
repository cover; thirty of fifty-two pages of
\cite{GPSS23SobolevV1}; and sixteen of thirty-five pages
of \cite{EGZ08SingularKEV2}. The combined actual reading
is 295 physical pages with 151 matching, individually
displayed images and sequential notes. It is a source
checkpoint, not another complete rereading of the book.
Acquisition, search, preparation, merging and export add
no reading credit.

The criterion, its proposed approximation construction, its
topological consequences, and the potential regularity used
by the geometric companion are separate dependencies.
The following audit retains that separation. An error in
a printed implication is not a disproof of every conclusion
of the paper containing it.

\subsection{The curvature quantity and the analytic domain}

\cite[Proposition~5.1]{GSS26RicciEntropyV1} assumes its
equation~(2.2), smooth convergence away from the exceptional
set, and a common entropy bound for the negative Ricci
eigenvalue relative to the approximating metric. This
entropy is \(\int\rho\log(1+\rho)\,dV_g\); neither
\(\int |\log(\omega^n/\theta^n)|^p\omega^n\) nor an
entropy of a fixed-background trace can replace it.
The volume normalization in H is \(dV_g=\omega^n/n!\).
This matters when asserting noncollapse, even though
constant rescaling of the measure preserves the RCD condition.

The selected Sobolev sections of \cite{GPSS23SobolevV1}
provide a common subcritical Sobolev estimate under their
intersection, volume, density and lower-density hypotheses.
The lower density may vanish on the controlled analytic
set. Their singular-space class has additional logarithmic
and resolution conditions; it is not synonymous with every
bounded-potential current having an arbitrary \(L^p\) density.
The approximating family must satisfy the common hypotheses,
not just each of them with unrelated constants.

The minimal Laplacian on compactly supported regular-locus
functions is symmetric, but is not thereby self-adjoint.
The closed Dirichlet form gives the Friedrichs realization.
Zero-capacity removal identifies the relevant finite-energy
domain. The subcritical Sobolev tail estimate, local Rellich
compactness and exceptional-volume control give compactness
of its embedding into \(L^2\). These are the spectral inputs
used in H's two min--max passages. Heat-kernel convergence
also needs conservation, symmetry and density of compactly
supported tests before a full eigenfunction expansion can
be identified.

\cite{Honda18AlmostSmoothV3} supplies the final almost-smooth
criterion with four substantive requirements: the regular-locus
Ricci bound, Sobolev-to-Lipschitz, compact finite-energy
embedding, and Lipschitz eigenfunctions, in its specified
measure and cutoff setting. Its glued examples with a
zero-capacity junction show that the regular Ricci bound
does not force the needed global Poincar\'e or continuity
properties. A Bakry--\'Emery inequality alone also does
not supply Sobolev-to-Lipschitz. H retains all these inputs.

The earlier criterion in
\cite[Theorem~1.5]{CCHSTT25CurrentsV1} uses negative-Ricci
\(L^p\) control with \(p>(2n-1)/n\), not the same
exponent condition as a volume density. Its Kato estimate
has constant below \(1/(2n-1)\); the permitted gradient
power must then allow \(1/(1-\beta)<p\).
The newer logarithmic estimate uses the K\"ahler
\(J\)-invariant Hessian energy to avoid that curvature-power
threshold. It does not eliminate the approximation or
Dirichlet-domain hypotheses.

\subsection{The approximation construction is a separate step}

In \cite[Sections~4 and~6]{GSS26RicciEntropyV1}, the
fixed smooth nonnegative twist allows a regularized adapted
volume equation on the resolution. The discrepancy terms
give the nonnegative forms in
\eqref{eq:appH-ricci-divisor-form}. After the polynomial
trace and density estimates, their logarithmic contributions
are bounded by fixed cohomological intersections and
\(|\log\varepsilon|\int_{E_i}
(\pi^*\theta+\varepsilon\theta_Y)^{n-1}\).
The image codimension is essential to the vanishing of
this last term. No statement about arbitrary divisorial
boundary images follows from this calculation.

For a merely lower-bounded Ricci current, Section~7.2 is
shorter than this fixed-twist proof. Lemma~7.1 obtains
smooth functions with a larger background curvature loss,
local smooth convergence and an integrable exponential
dominator. Such functions need not lie above the original
function, and the curvature loss cannot be ignored.
Lemma~7.2 requires a smooth nonnegative twist in an exact
Ricci equation. Here write \(T_j\) for the source's target currents on \(X\).
Converting \(-T_j-C\theta\) into \(-AT_j\) by a tensor lower
bound does not itself make that twist smooth: the compensating
term contains the nonsmooth \(T_j\).
Thus the latter requirement needs its own construction.

The regularized maximum in
\eqref{eq:appH-ricci-exact-class-max} records a possible
exact-class calculation under explicit global regularization
hypotheses. It includes both the pole correction and the
division by \(1+\delta^2\). The unrescaled maximum only
has the enlarged curvature bound. It also agrees on a
regular compact set with \(q+\delta+\delta^2\rho\),
not with \(q+\delta\). For a Ricci application one must
establish the global psh extension for \(A\varphi+\psi\),
positivity of the chosen background, the correctly signed
positive-exponential equation, uniform density domination
on the resolution, and local elliptic convergence.
This checkpoint does not certify the whole general
approximation pipeline by quoting its abbreviated final step.

The selected sections of \cite{FGS25RCDThreefoldV1} keep
their projective-threefold category, or the paper's separately
stated resolution condition. They do not provide an
arbitrary-dimensional nonprojective substitute. The selected
published \cite{Vajaitu01EmbeddablePublished} justifies the
local extension of pluriharmonic functions on the regular
locus of a normal space; it is a distinct smoothness input,
not a curvature regularization theorem.

The primary paper's topological identification and its
applications to density gaps, fundamental groups and uniform
polarized embeddings use further rigidity and peak-section
inputs. Those applications are not recursively certified
by the conditional entropy proof. In particular, a
statement about the fundamental group of the underlying
compact space is not a statement about the regular-locus
or orbifold fundamental group.

\subsection{A reference-volume test}

The \(L^p\) conversion must specify its measure.
On the crepant resolution chart of the ordinary double
point \(uv=w^2\), use
\[
 \pi(t,z)=(u=t,v=tz^2,w=tz),\qquad
 r=|t|^2,\quad s=|z|^2.
\]
For the pullback of the ambient Euclidean K\"ahler form
and the quotient-flat adapted metric, the local potentials
can be taken as
\[
 r(1+s+s^2),\qquad \sqrt r(1+s).
\]
Their Hermitian determinants are respectively
\(r(1+4s+s^2)\) and \(1/4\). Thus a density
\(b\) relative to the ambient volume behaves like \(r^{-1}\)
on a bounded \(z\)-chart. With \(R=|t|\),
\[
 \int b^p\theta^2\asymp\int_0 R^{3-2p}\,dR,
 \qquad
 \int b^p\Omega\asymp\int_0 R^{1-2p}\,dR.
\]
The first is locally finite for \(p<2\); the second fails
for every \(p\geq1\). The relative potential is bounded,
the adapted metric is Ricci flat off the exceptional set,
and its logarithmic density is in \(L^1(\theta^2)\).
This direct local test detects the unsupported unweighted
transfer of \(e^{pF}\) in the resolution estimate on
physical page~7 of \cite{CCHSTT25CurrentsV1}.
It does not refute a global theorem or a regularization
estimate that retains compensating Jacobian factors.

The same test detects the assertion on physical page~24
of \cite{GKSS25HolderV1} that the adapted-to-background
density belongs to \(L^p(\Omega)\) for some \(p>1\).
What is useful is a small \(L^{1+\eta}(\theta^n)\)
exponent for that density, equivalently an \(\eta\)-power
integrable against the adapted measure. H uses the exact
H\"older inequality
\eqref{eq:appH-ricci-reference-conversion}.

\subsection{Continuous approximation after the viscosity erratum}

The acquired \cite{EGZ13ContinuousApproximationV1} is the
2013 first version, not its later journal pagination.
\cite[Section~2.2]{EGZ16ViscosityErratumV1} withdraws the
general global step used in its Corollary~2.5. The defect
comes from an uncontrolled doubled-variable penalty
Hessian. The corrected comparison works on the ample
locus; a global comparison needs the additional continuity
condition stated in the erratum. Projective Hodge-class
approximation and Richberg regularization once continuity
is known are different inputs and are not withdrawn.

The acquired second version of
\cite{ChoChoi25ContinuityV2}, dated 14 August 2025,
provides a newer capacity and envelope route. Its
Theorem~4.2 is for the exact resolution pullback of a
K\"ahler form; its Theorem~5.5 supplies the corresponding
continuous approximation. Smooth obstacles on the resolution
have envelopes with bounded Monge--Amp\`ere density by
\cite[Theorem~3.5]{GLZ17EnvelopesV1}. The penalized
positive-exponential equation stays below a smooth obstacle;
the exponentially small mass on the strict noncontact set
and domination identify the envelope limit.

For this bounded upstairs density,
\cite[Lemma~3.2]{EGZ08SingularKEV2} uses integrability of
a small negative power of the Jacobian to obtain an
\(L^{1+\eta}\) density against the degenerate pullback
volume. This is the direction needed for Cho--Choi's
continuity theorem. It is not the reverse arbitrary-density
transfer disproved above. Normality and connected fibres
allow descent, and Richberg regularization then applies.
An exact curvature class still requires the negative-function
rescaling, rather than dropping its small curvature loss.
No conclusion for all arbitrary big semipositive backgrounds
or a uniform initial trace for every discontinuous potential
is imported here.

The desired global smooth-obstacle case of GLZ is distinct
from its local Remark~5.6. The latter claims an envelope
is identically \(-1\) in the unit ball of \(\mathbb C^2\)
for the obstacle equal to \(-1\) on
\(\{|z_1|=|z_2|\}\) and zero elsewhere. In fact
\[
 v(z)=\max\{-1,\log(\sqrt2|z_1|)-1\}
\]
is psh and lies below that obstacle: on the indicated
set, \(\sqrt2|z_1|<1\). At \((3/4,0)\) it is
\(-1+\tfrac12\log(9/8)>-1\). This direct example
invalidates the local remark's asserted envelope value;
it does not affect the global smooth-obstacle inequality
used in the new approximation route.

\subsection{What the geometric H\"older paper assumes}

\cite[Proposition~1]{GSS26GeometricHolderV1} begins with
an ambiently H\"older potential. Its finite-projection
argument gives the upper distance estimate in
\eqref{eq:appH-ricci-holder-comparison}. The finite
monodromy chain is needed to join the prescribed sheets;
a single root lift does not do this. The proof first
uses the fixed background intrinsic distance. Its passage
to an ambient endpoint distance uses an additional
subanalytic intrinsic--extrinsic estimate.

The analytic companion cited for that paper's Theorem~7
was still in preparation in the acquired version. Thus
the geometric proof alone does not give analytic H\"older
regularity for every bounded solution with adapted
\(L^p\) density. The available \cite{GKSS25HolderV1}
has different scope: its singular results use a polarized
projective \(\mathbb Q\)-Gorenstein smoothing with smooth
punctured fibres, and its general-density result also
assumes logarithmic positivity. The arbitrary nonsmoothable
